" data-portal-copyright= " Prado Museum " **28;259;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78577358 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A group of professional restorers in Spain is calling for greater government regulation after a botched attempt to clean a copy of a famous painting by baroque artist Bartolom&amp;eacute ; Esteban Murillo resulted in an unrecognizable Virgin Mary . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Valencia-based owner of the copy of The Immaculate Conception of Los Venerables ( the original is in the Prado Museum in Madrid ) sent the painting to a furniture restorer to be cleaned for 1,200 euros , &lt;a href= " https : **33;289;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The Guardian reported&lt;/a&gt; . But the artwork was left distorted despite two attempts to fix it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;Spain has been hit by yet another bungling restorer , who turned this Virgin Mary painting into an unrecognizable blob : &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/G2uKfLNf8o " &gt;https : //t.c ... &lt;a href= " https : //t.co/0ASSjL1ybZ " &gt;pic.twitter ... -- Artnet ( @artnet ) &lt;a href= " https : **33;324;TOOLONG ... 23 , **33;359;TOOLONG &lt;script async src= " https : **33;394;TOOLONG " charset= " utf-8 " &gt;&lt;/script&gt; &lt;p&gt; Spain&amp;rsquo ; s Professional Association for Restorers and Conservators ( ACRE ) called it a &amp;ldquo ; heritage attack , &amp;rdquo ; according to a translated copy of a statement issued by the group . The association urged people not to call this &amp;ldquo ; vandalizing act&amp;rdquo ; a restoration as it would call into question the legitimacy of their profession . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;429;TOOLONG ... citizen behind botched Spanish fresco has last laugh as painting becomes lucrative hit for small **25;464;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;491;TOOLONG ... Canary Islands , royals push to save tourism in **26;526;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... &amp;ldquo ; We want to make it clear that no professional with an official academic background would carry out this kind of heritage attack , &amp;rdquo ; the statement said . &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The group also decried the lack of regulations that allow anyone without the appropriate skills and knowledge to attempt to restore artworks . The association called for the government to regulate the industry and impose stricter sanctions on people who damage precious artworks under the guise of restoration . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " twitter-tweet " data-width= " 550 " data-dnt= " true " &gt;&lt;p lang= " en " dir= " ltr " &gt;A church in northern &lt;a href= " https : **27;554;TOOLONG ? src=h ... In June 2018 , a 16th-century statue of Saint George and the dragon came under scrutiny after a local artist attempted to restore the statue , but made it look more like a cartoon character . The Church of St Michael in Estella , Navarre , paid about 33,000 euros ( $50,000 ) to restore the statue to its original state and was fined another 6,000 euros ( $9,000 ) , &lt;a href= " https : **33;688;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;The Guardian reported&lt;/a&gt; . &amp;nbsp ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;723;TOOLONG ... title= " The Ecce Homo painting after a botched attempt at restoring it ( right ) . The painting , which was called Ecce Homo ( Behold the Man ) came to be known as Ecce Mono ( Behold the Monkey ) after the infamous restoration attempt . However , the fresco garnered international attention and a spike of tourists was observed in the town of Borja , Spain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;788;TOOLONG ... title= " The Immaculate Conception of Los Venerables by the Spanish artist Bartolom ? " data-portal-copyright= " Prado Museum " **28;823;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78577357 " /&gt; National Post StaffTue , 23 Jun 2020 23:32:53 **45;853;TOOLONG ... government nearing revived trade spat with U.S. as tensions mount over dairy , aluminumhttp : **32;900;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;934;TOOLONG ... title= " U.S. Trade Representative Robert Lighthizer took issue with Canadian trade policy in a Senate committee hearing last week , where he blasted quotas for cheese , milk and poultry . " alt= " U.S. Trade Representative Robert Lighthizer took issue with Canadian trade policy in a Senate committee hearing last week , where he blasted quotas for cheese , milk and poultry . " data-portal-copyright= " Anna Moneymaker-Pool/Getty Images " **28;969;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78577356 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;mdash ; The Canadian government could be edging toward a revived trade spat with the U.S. , after America&amp;rsquo ; s top trade advisor accused Canada of &amp;ldquo ; shading&amp;rdquo ; its dairy obligations and breaking agreements over aluminum exports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Renewed trade rifts with the U.S. come just days ahead of a key deadline for the U.S.-Mexico-Canada Agreement ( USMCA ) , which comes into force July 1 and replaces the former North American Free Trade Agreement ( NAFTA ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The two countries have enjoyed a year of relative calm on the trade front , after agreeing in mid-2019 to remove tariffs on steel and aluminum and finally ending years-long negotiations over USMCA . But comments last week from U.S. Trade Representative Robert Lighthizer suggest that relationship is beginning to sour once again , after the Trudeau government introduced new dairy quotas that Lighthizer said could be purposely skirting international trade obligations . Those tensions add to a separate spat over aluminum supplies , in which the U.S. has threatened to re-impose tariffs on Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; James McIlroy , a trade consultant who works with the International Cheese Council of Canada ( ICCC ) , said the recent moves by Ottawa exposes a &amp;ldquo ; two-faced foreign policy&amp;rdquo ; that has long frustrated its international trading partners , who claim they are unfairly and routinely cut out of the Canadian market . He said a revived spat with the U.S. , if left unresolved , could threaten to hamper Ottawa as it looks to climb out of a deep economic slump caused by the COVID-19 pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It almost seems like the Trudeau government is sleepwalking into this , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;999;TOOLONG ... U.S. aluminum producers are again pushing for tariffs on Canada as others warn of ' great Canadian distraction ' &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt; &; ... href= " https : **33;1034;TOOLONG ... dairy and agriculture biggest losers of new NAFTA : **27;1069;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p ... Concerns have persisted for decades over Canada&amp;rsquo ; s protection of its dairy industry , and came up in a review launched by European trade partners under the Comprehensive Economic and Trade Agreement ( CETA ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s a concern in the international community that the Trudeau government is signing agreements and then not respecting them , &amp;rdquo ; McIlroy said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lighthizer took issue with Canadian trade policy in a Senate committee hearing last week , where he blasted quotas for cheese , milk and poultry , while also blaming Canada for a recent influx in U.S. aluminum imports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; International Trade Minister Mary Ng on June 15 published updated guidelines for allocating so-called trade tariff rate quotas ( TRQs ) for a range of dairy products . The updated TRQs allocated 85 per cent of some cheese quotas to Canadian processors , for example , which effectively hands the bulk of the market to three entities : French multinational firm Lactalis , Montreal-based Saputo , and the dairy organization Agropur Coop&amp;eacute ; rative. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The U.S. has now threatened to file a formal complaint with Canada over the allocations , saying it gives market access directly to American competitors , rather than opening up the Canadian market to foreign firms , as USMCA sought to do . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Dairy is something we&amp;rsquo ; re going to be very closely monitoring with Canada , &amp;rdquo ; Lighthizer said in the hearing last week . &amp;ldquo ; If there&amp;rsquo ; s any shading of the benefits to American farmers , we&amp;rsquo ; re going to bring a case against them , &amp;rdquo ; he told the committee . He said he would be &amp;ldquo ; very closely monitoring&amp;rdquo ; Canadian dairy allocations to protect American producers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lighthizer also told the committee that a &amp;ldquo ; surge&amp;rdquo ; in aluminum supply , mostly from Canada , ran counter to previous anti-dumping arrangements , and was &amp;ldquo ; something that we&amp;rsquo ; re looking at and talking to both Mexico and Canada about . &amp;rdquo ; A report by Bloomberg News on Monday , citing anonymous sources , suggested the Trump administration was mulling the re-imposition of tariffs on aluminum , and could make an announcement by Friday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A spokesperson for Deputy Prime Minister Chrystia Freeland , who still oversees the Canada-U.S. trade file , said Ottawa has been discussing the disagreement with its American counterparts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The free flow of goods and services , including aluminum , is important for jobs and economic growth in both of our countries , &amp;rdquo ; the person said in a statement . &amp;ldquo ; We firmly believe that our aluminum exports do not harm the U.S. market . We are emphasizing this in our ongoing conversations with our American partners . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A response to questions around dairy quotas was not immediately answered . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mark Warner , international business and regulatory lawyer at MAAW Law , said the trade relationship between the U.S. and Canada has remained tense , as any slight move by Ottawa could quickly dust off old resentments. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Anyone who is seen to be playing close to the bone , Trump and the Trump administration respond , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dairy industries in New Zealand , Europe , the U.S. and elsewhere have long claimed that Canada has unfairly used its quota system to protect domestic producers , while expecting foreign countries to maintain an open market . The Canadian dairy industry rejects those claims , saying various forms of protections exist in other jurisdictions as well . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jaime Castaneda , a senior vice-president at the U.S. National Milk Producers Federation , said in an interview last week that their group had &amp;ldquo ; significant concerns&amp;rdquo ; over Canada using TRQs in a way that it claims is counter to free trade . He said the &amp;ldquo ; Canadian government has used this system for other trade agreements and , in fact , in USMCA there are specific provisions to prevent this from happening again . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;1098;TOOLONG " **35;1128;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **32;1165;TOOLONG ... class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;jessesnyder ... &lt;/p&gt; Jesse SnyderTue , 23 Jun 2020 23:07:06 **45;1199;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " Supplied " **28;1316;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78555285 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; EDMONTON &amp;mdash ; At least two United States senators&amp;rsquo ; offices have been briefed in the case of a former Saudi intelligence official who has been living in Canada and is facing escalating pressure from the Saudi government to return home , including the recent disappearance of two of his children who were living in the Saudi capital of Riyadh . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saad Aljabri , a former intelligence official well-known in Western diplomatic and intelligence circles , came to Canada in 2017 after falling out of favour back home during the tumult and purges to overhaul the line of succession to the Saudi throne . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Most of Aljabri&amp;rsquo ; s family also fled , but two children , Omar and Sarah , both now in their early 20s , were barred from leaving the country in 2017 . News that Aljabri had settled in Canada &amp;mdash ; and of the Saudi attempts to coerce him home &amp;mdash ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;1346;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;started trickling out in recent weeks . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Canadian government confirmed to &lt;em&gt;National Post &lt;/em&gt; earlier this month that it is aware of the abduction of the two Saudi children , and pointed out there is no extradition treaty between Canada and Saudi Arabia that could be used to force Aljabri home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In early June , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s foreign affairs department said , when contacted by the &lt;em&gt;National Post , &lt;/em&gt; it &amp;ldquo ; is concerned by the detention of Saad al-Jabri&amp;rsquo ; s two Children ( sic ) in Saudi Arabia . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The family has also sought assistance from legislators in the United States . &amp;nbsp ; Among the U.S. senators who&amp;rsquo ; ve taken an interest are Vermont Democrat Patrick Leahy and Florida Republican Marco Rubio. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;1381;TOOLONG ... of ex-Saudi intelligence official living in Canada disappear amid Saudi efforts to force him **25;1416;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;1443;TOOLONG ... put pressure on another dissident living in Canada as RCMP warns about threats made by **27;1478;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p ... &amp;ldquo ; Hostage taking is wrong under any circumstances , &amp;rdquo ; said Leahy , who has expressed concern about the case . &amp;ldquo ; The Saudis have apparently detained Mr. Aljabri&amp;rsquo ; s children to try to coerce him to return to Saudi Arabia . That is abhorrent and unacceptable , and the Trump Administration and the Canadian government should determine their whereabouts and demand their release . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prior to coming to Canada , Aljabri was the right-hand man of Mohammed bin Nayef , the nephew of King Salman bin Abdulaziz Al Saud , who was originally thought to be heir to the throne . In a palace coup , bin Nayef was deposed in favour of Mohammed bin Salman , making Aljabri a target for the new regime , a source close to the Aljabri family told the &lt;em&gt;National Post&lt;/em&gt; earlier this month . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In March 2020 , bin Nayef was rounded up in Saudi Arabia on suspicion of plotting a coup against bin Salman . Days later , Aljabri&amp;rsquo ; s kids , Omar and Sarah , were&amp;nbsp ; also detained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to a Reuters &lt;a href= " http : **34;1507;TOOLONG ... the international drama , the pressure campaign was upped in May , when Aljabri&amp;rsquo ; s brother was also detained . The Reuters report says Mohammed bin Salman &amp;mdash ; the Crown Prince is often referred to as MBS &amp;mdash ; is after documents detailing financial dealings of senior royals , including King Salman , and information on assets of those opposed to MBS , including bin Nayef. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;1543;TOOLONG ... title= " U.S. Senators Vermont Democrat Patrick Leahy and Florida Republican Marco Rubio have taken an interest in Saad Aljabri 's situation with Saudi Arabia . The Saudi embassies in Ottawa and in Washington , did not immediately respond to the &lt;em&gt; Post&lt;/em&gt; &amp;lsquo ; s enquiries on Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aljabri has had a long relationship with Western governments and intelligence agencies , and has been credited with passing along information that foiled a 2010 plot to bomb two cargo planes with printer cartridge bombs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The news comes as another Saudi in Canada , Omar Abdulaziz , told the &lt;a href= " https : **33;1608;TOOLONG ... **29;1643;TOOLONG , a British newspaper , the Royal Canadian Mounted Police warned him of threats from the kingdom . " data-portal-copyright= " Andrew Vaughan/The Canadian Press/File " **28;2121;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78577150 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; The RCMP did not rely on any information from confidential informants when seeking judicial approval to search properties owned by the Nova Scotia mass shooter , court was told . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A prosecutor representing the RCMP made the declaration when defending the heavy censorship of information about the investigation into the worst mass killing in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s history . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The issue of confidential informants has been an undercurrent of the investigation into April&amp;rsquo ; s shooting rampage based on rumours or speculation that the gunman , Gabriel Wortman , was himself an RCMP informant or civilian agent for police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The speculation grew significantly last week when **30;2151;TOOLONG revealed the killer withdraw $475,000 in $100 bills from a Brinks depot 19 days before his murder spree . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The article cited no direct evidence of the reason for the unusual withdrawal but quoted sources in banking and policing saying the transaction is consistent with how the RCMP pays informants and agents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The RCMP later denied the police force was the source of Wortman&amp;rsquo ; s money . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Immediately after the shooting spree , when police searched Wortman&amp;rsquo ; s property at 200 Portapique Beach Rd. , which he set on fire at the start of his rampage , police found an ammunition box containing a burnt $100 bill . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2183;TOOLONG ... of N.S. shooter renounces right to be executor of his $1.2M **27;2218;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2247;TOOLONG ... autopsy of N.S. mass killer reveals an ' injustice collector ' consumed by **28;2282;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... The information on what police found in their search came from documents ordered released by a Nova Scotia judge in response to a court challenge for more information launched by a consortium of news organizations , including Postmedia. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another in the series of court hearings on the media&amp;rsquo ; s challenge for information that the Crown wishes to keep secret was held Tuesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was during Tuesday&amp;rsquo ; s hearing that Mark Covan , a lawyer with the Public Prosecution Service of Canada , which represents the RCMP at the hearings , spoke of the need to protect confidential informants when he argued why the documents need to be assessed by a judge behind closed doors before they are discussed in public . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The judge may be satisfied that some information automatically needs to remain secret and is not open to arguments by the media&amp;rsquo ; s lawyer , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; An example of that is the identity of a confidential informant . There is no possible way that the applicants are going to get to know the identity of a confidential informant unless they meet the &amp;lsquo ; innocence at stake&amp;rsquo ; standard . And they haven&amp;rsquo ; t done that , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;2312;TOOLONG ... title= " RCMP block the entrance to Portapique Beach Road after the search for mass shooter Gabriel Wortman ended , April 19 , 2020. " alt= " RCMP block the entrance to Portapique Beach Road after the search for mass shooter Gabriel Wortman ended , April 19 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " John Morris/Reuters " **28;2347;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78508214 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; ( In 2018 , the Supreme Court of Canada ruled that the secrecy protecting a confidential police informant can only be pierced in a criminal case when someone&amp;rsquo ; s innocence is at stake. ) &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; David Coles , lawyer for the media group , seemed alarmed by the reference to informants. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Several aspects of this trouble me , &amp;rdquo ; Coles told Judge Laurie Halfpenny MacQuarrie. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You have to decide whether there are elements of this that are simply going to be off the table , and he mentions , by way of example , confidential informants . Well , my goodness . I&amp;rsquo ; ve looked at the affidavits that we have in this first batch , &amp;rdquo ; Coles said . Crown lawyers claim several reasons for various redactions of information but confidential informant status is not one of them , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Covan then sought to clarify the Crown&amp;rsquo ; s position . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I know there is members of media on the line , &amp;rdquo ; he said at the hearing , held by teleconference because of COVID-19 restrictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There has been a considerable amount of reporting on this . The Crown is not suggesting there are informers in this case , confidential informants in this case . That was an example of how the law applies in a particular context . We are not suggesting here that the RCMP has utilized confidential informants in creating these court documents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are not suggesting that at all . If we had , as Mr. Coles points out , there would have been a clear statement to that effect in our materials that we are relying on confidential informant privilege and we have not done . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Halfpenny MacQuarrie said she understood : &amp;ldquo ; I took it as an illustration not a specific . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She noted the two sides have &amp;ldquo ; agreed to disagree&amp;rdquo ; on the process for deciding the issues . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are nine additional warrant applications and documents still to be released and each document previously released has had significant portions blacked out by prosecutors . Coles said there are more than 1,000 redactions. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A series of open and closed court dates have been scheduled to hear further arguments for and against release of information contained in court documents relating to the shooting . A public court hearing is scheduled for the end of July . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wortman , 51 , wore an RCMP uniform and drove a replica RCMP cruiser during his rampage that started on April 18 with the assault and confinement of his common-law spouse and ended when he was shot and killed by police the next day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; * Email : &lt;a href= " **31;2377;TOOLONG " **38;2410;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **25;2450;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;ADHumphreys ... &lt;/p&gt; Adrian HumphreysTue , 23 Jun 2020 21:01:59 **45;2477;TOOLONG ... is going to produce cognitive deficits ' : Famed neuroscientist Adrian Owen launches COVID-19 brain studyhttp : **35;2524;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;2561;TOOLONG ... title= " A world renowned neuroscientist has instead launched a study that aims to recruit 50,000 people with a confirmed positive diagnosis of COVID-19 to study the disease 's impact on brain function . " alt= " A world renowned neuroscientist has instead launched a study that aims to recruit 50,000 people with a confirmed positive diagnosis of COVID-19 to study the disease 's impact on brain function . " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images " **28;2596;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576494 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Normally , Adrian Owen would be inside an ICU these days , trying to read the minds of people with severely injured brains . Because of COVID-19 , &amp;ldquo ; I can&amp;rsquo ; t do that right now . I haven&amp;rsquo ; t been able to do that for three or four months . I have a whole team of people sitting in my lab , unable to directly assess patients , &amp;rdquo ; said the renowned Western University neuroscientist who has devoted much of his career searching for signs of consciousness and awareness in unresponsive patients . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But then the crisis started escalating ; reports starting to come in of people suffering neurological consequences , and it became apparent to Owen about six or eight weeks ago &amp;ldquo ; that if we&amp;rsquo ; re ever going to understand this , this is something we need to do , now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What he&amp;rsquo ; s doing now is launching a massive study to explore the impacts of COVID-19 on the brain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The online &lt;a href= " https : **33;2626;TOOLONG ... Brain Study&lt;/a&gt; aims to recruit 50,000 people with a confirmed , positive diagnosis of the virus . Using questionnaires and pop down menus , Owen and his collaborators will collect information , &amp;ldquo ; completely anonymized and secure , &amp;rdquo ; asking participants about their medical history , any underlying heart , lung or other health issues , the extent of their COVID-19 diagnosis and what happened to them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They&amp;rsquo ; ll be asked to perform cognitive games that assess memory , decision-making , planning and problem solving , and results will be compared against a huge database of millions of tests completed by a healthy , normal population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For the study , Owen has partnered with Sunnybrook Hospital stroke neurologist Dr. Rick Swartz . They&amp;rsquo ; re hoping for a large and diverse group ( the study is available in English , French and Spanish ) . They hope that with 50,000 people they&amp;rsquo ; ll have enough statistical power to tease apart &amp;ldquo ; all of these little nuances , &amp;rdquo ; Owen said , and answer questions like , are there certain proportions of the population that are more vulnerable to developing cognitive deficits &amp;mdash ; fuzzy thinking , brain fog , problems concentrating ? Are there differences between men and women ? Older people and young ? Is it only people who were placed on ventilators ? Is this something happening to everybody ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Timing is critical , said Owen , a professor in Western&amp;rsquo ; s Brain and Mind Institute . &amp;ldquo ; We can&amp;rsquo ; t wait a year from now when potentially we&amp;rsquo ; ll have eight million or more people who have survived COVID-19. &amp;rdquo ; His hypothesis ? &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re going to see many , many people with profound cognitive impairment a year from now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have enough experience that we know that this is going to produce cognitive deficits , &amp;rdquo ; and there are several possible reasons why , he said , including from the virus itself , the secondary effects on the respiratory system that might affect the flow of oxygen to the brain , to staying in ICU . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Many different things are likely to contribute to the cognitive profile of people coming out of this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; So far little work has been done on COVID-19&amp;rsquo ; s potential impact on the brain . People were too focused on saving lives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , &lt;a href= " https : **33;2661;TOOLONG ... recent review article&lt;/a&gt; raised the question of whether SARS-CoV-2 , the pandemic virus that has killed nearly half a million globally and sent millions of other lives into free fall , is neurotropic , meaning toxic to brain tissue and to what extent it&amp;rsquo ; s capable of damaging the central nervous system . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;2696;TOOLONG ... title= " In a handout photo of Western University neuroscientist Adrian Owen , who , in collaboration with Dr. Rick Swartz , a stroke neurologist from Sunnybrook Health Sciences Centre and the University of Toronto , has launched a study to look at the possible effects of COVID-19 on the brain . " alt= " In a handout photo of Western University neuroscientist Adrian Owen , who , in collaboration with Dr. Rick Swartz , a stroke neurologist from Sunnybrook Health Sciences Centre and the University of Toronto , has launched a study to look at the possible effects of COVID-19 on the brain . " data-portal-copyright= " Handout " **28;2731;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576503 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; During the SARS pandemic of 2002-03 , many survivors suffered long-term neurological complications . The new virus shares many of the same features as its more lethal predecessor . Both use spike proteins to bind to a protein called ACE2 on the surface of &amp;ldquo ; host , &amp;rdquo ; in this case human host , cells , and some scientists have found ACE2 receptors scattered through the brain , on neurons as well as the star-shaped glial cells that help nourish them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The virus might also invade the brain by several routes , the review article found , including&amp;nbsp ; via the olfactory nerve , which might explain why people with COVID-19 so frequently report loss of taste and smell . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Other common neurologic complaints in COVID-19 include headache , stroke , impaired consciousness , seizure and encephalopathy. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This virus is new ; there&amp;rsquo ; s no long-term data on people who have recovered from COVID-19. &amp;ldquo ; But there are plenty of reports of people reporting what in a clinical context we refer to as delirium , &amp;rdquo ; Owen said . &amp;ldquo ; There are people coming out of the ICU recovering from COVID and saying they don&amp;rsquo ; t feel themselves . They&amp;rsquo ; ve got fuzzy thinking . They can&amp;rsquo ; t really concentrate . And this is sort of the typical profile that we know goes on to produce long-term consequences . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; High doses of some sedatives in the ICU can contribute to delirium , a kind of brain dysfunction that can lead to a serious state of confusion and even paranoid delusions that the brain lays down as &amp;ldquo ; real&amp;rdquo ; memories . Early evidence suggests that one-third of COVID-19 patients of all ages , and two-thirds of those with severe disease , show signs of delirium , &lt;a href= " https : **33;2761;TOOLONG ... to Harvard Medical School researchers . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I do most of my work with ICU clinicians , and they&amp;rsquo ; ll often tell you , no one leaves the ICU in good shape , cognitively , &amp;rdquo ; Owen said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The priority , and this is not specific to COVID , the priority in most ICU&amp;rsquo ; s around the world is to get the patient out the door alive . They&amp;rsquo ; re not trying to get them out making sure they&amp;rsquo ; re cognitively intact and they have no memory impairments . People are typically fighting a battle between life and death . Cognitive deficits might not be a high priority at that point . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2796;TOOLONG ... tinged with anxiety ' : What to expect from the next 100 days of COVID-19 , according to **28;2831;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2861;TOOLONG ... COVID-19 coronavirus is changing . What 's not clear is what it **26;2896;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2924;TOOLONG ... could be last country to cling to two-metre physical distancing **25;2959;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;2986;TOOLONG ... and your brain : Can the coronavirus cause long-lasting neurological damage ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... People who spend time on ventilators generally experience cognitive deficits , the result of uneven delivery of oxygen to the brain , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The pandemic virus can also cause blood clots that can lead to strokes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The hope is to use the study&amp;rsquo ; s data to design therapeutic strategies , &amp;ldquo ; to work out what we need to do to deal with this , &amp;rdquo ; Owen said . &amp;ldquo ; You don&amp;rsquo ; t want to put all your eggs into giving people memory training if it&amp;rsquo ; s not fundamentally a memory problem that people have . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s important we understand exactly what the problems are : Is this something that affects people&amp;rsquo ; s memory ? Their concentration ? Their ability to make high-level decisions ? &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s also important to get a better handle on the magnitude of the problem . &amp;ldquo ; Is this something that&amp;rsquo ; s going to affect 10 million in a year&amp;rsquo ; s time , in which case that is a massive social and economic problem , &amp;rdquo ; Owen said . &amp;ldquo ; Or is this something that&amp;rsquo ; s going to affect 20,000 people a year from now , in which case it&amp;rsquo ; s something much more manageable . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You can not tackle any problem to do with the brain unless you fully understand it . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; Sharon KirkeyTue , 23 Jun 2020 18:41:26 **45;3021;TOOLONG ... Holmes : A little leak can lead to big problemshttp : **32;3068;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;3102;TOOLONG ... title= " Look for signs of leaks around fixtures , faucets , and appliances , Mike Holmes advises . " alt= " Look for signs of leaks around fixtures , faucets , and appliances , Mike Holmes advises . " data-portal-copyright= " Photo Alex Schuldtz , The Holmes Group " **28;3137;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576844 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Often when I talk about leaky homes , I talk about the big stuff : Leaks that come in through your outdoor structure , like the roof , windows , or foundation . While these can cause a major of water damage , sometimes , the leak can be coming from inside the house . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; You&amp;rsquo ; ve got a whole host of pipes and plumbing hiding behind your walls that could be slowly leaking &amp;mdash ; either due to age , improper install , or an unfortunate accident like a burst pipe . Even if you&amp;rsquo ; ve taken every step to prevent water from sneaking in through your building envelope , you&amp;rsquo ; re not totally safe from water damage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Here are a few places to keep an eye out for leaks : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Is it in the kitchen ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A plumbing leak can be a common source of costly water bills . Even a small leak can add up to big bills over time . Where should you be on the hunt for leaks ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Look for standing water at the bottom of your dishwasher . Check where the dishwasher connects to the main drain . You&amp;rsquo ; re looking for signs of discoloration on the hose to indicate a water leak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Faucets and fixtures don&amp;rsquo ; t last forever , especially if you bought cheap ones . If the unit is still dripping after it&amp;rsquo ; s shut off , you&amp;rsquo ; ve got a leak . You may also see discoloration around the base of the fixtures or in the sink cabinetry caused by water damage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If it&amp;rsquo ; s a small leak , you could try fixing it yourself . For compression faucets ( faucets where you tighten the washer to stop water flow ) , the No. 1 cause is a worn-out washer or O-ring . These are pretty simple to replace , but you MUST replace it with an exact fit or you will still have a leak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For cartridge faucets ( which typically use an up and down function , rather than a twist function ) , you can try replacing the cartridge in the unit . Again , you&amp;rsquo ; ll need the exact size to make a successful replacement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Is it in the bathroom ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Everything I&amp;rsquo ; ve already mentioned about faucets and fixtures remains true for the bathroom , though with one note of caution . If the leak is in the shower or tub , it could be a sign of something bigger going on behind the walls . That is a job I&amp;rsquo ; d leave to a pro . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are two types of toilet leaks . It could either be due to the flush valve for the fill valve . If the flush valve doesn&amp;rsquo ; t form a watertight seal , overtime water will begin to leak into the toilet ball . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the fill valve isn&amp;rsquo ; t fitted properly , either it&amp;rsquo ; s placed too high , or doesn&amp;rsquo ; t close properly , the tank will experience constant flowing water into overflow tube . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Silent toilet leaks can waste upwards of 1,000 litres of water per day &amp;mdash ; so if you suspect a leak , call in a plumber quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Is it in the basement ? &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Look around your water heater for signs of leakage . You might see some rust spots , or some pooling water on the floor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are a few reasons a water heater may leak , but the most common is a loose drain valve . You can try tightening it until it&amp;rsquo ; s snug and see if that does the trick . If the temperature was set too high , it could be creating too much pressure in the unit , also causing a leak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finally , the leak could be due to a cracked storage tank . Unfortunately , in that case , it&amp;rsquo ; s time to replace the unit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Generally , if the water heater is leaking , it&amp;rsquo ; s still safe to use , but I recommend calling an expert quickly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A leaking washing machine could be caused by bad tub fittings , insecure supply hoses , or the internal hoses could be faltering . Checking the supply hoses and ensuring they&amp;rsquo ; re still firmly connected is pretty simple for any homeowner . But if you&amp;rsquo ; ve determined the leak is coming from inside the machine , your best bet is to have a pro look at it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A smart automatic water shut-off valve system can help you in the case of big leaks or a burst pipe . If it detects a major water event , it will automatically shut off the water , preventing a bigger problem . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;To find out more about Mike Holmes , visit **25;3167;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Mike HolmesTue , 23 Jun 2020 17:17:20 **45;3194;TOOLONG ... college professor on leave after asking Vietnamese-American student to ' anglicize ' her namehttp : **36;3241;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;3279;TOOLONG ... title= " Laney College , based in Oakland California has put a professor on leave after he told a Vietnamese-American student her name sounded like an ' insult in English . " data-portal-copyright= " Frank Gunn / The Canadian Press " **28;4761;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576649 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thirteen years ago , Dr. Natasha Crowcroft , a Cambridge-trained physician and expert in infectious disease surveillance , moved to Toronto from London to help rebuild Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s public health system after SARS . For 11 years , Crowcroft worked inside the agency known as &lt;a href= " https : **29;4791;TOOLONG " rel= " noopener " target= " blank " &gt;Public Health Ontario&lt;/a&gt; ( PHO ) in a series of progressively more senior roles . During the 2009 H1N1 pandemic , she led the agency&amp;rsquo ; s surveillance epidemiology group . Last year , in April , she was named PHO&amp;rsquo ; s chief science officer and senior scientist . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The public health system Crowcroft found in &lt;a href= " https : **31;4822;TOOLONG " rel= " noopener " target= " blank " &gt;Ontario&lt;/a&gt; was &amp;ldquo ; a bit of a dog&amp;rsquo ; s breakfast , &amp;rdquo ; she said . Over the years , there were constant squabbles between the Ministry of Health and PHO about issues like jurisdiction and independence . Crowcroft wasn&amp;rsquo ; t shocked by any of that . To an extent , it just goes with the territory in her field . &amp;ldquo ; Governments really don&amp;rsquo ; t like independent public health organizations that evaluate what&amp;rsquo ; s going on and tell them when they&amp;rsquo ; re not doing things right , &amp;rdquo ; she said . They&amp;rsquo ; re constantly trying to trim their wings and pull them back into the cage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I sometimes say it&amp;rsquo ; s a bit like the Death Star in Star Wars , &amp;rdquo ; Crowcroft said . &amp;ldquo ; You know you have to do everything you can not to get sucked into the tractor beam . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For all her frustrations , Crowcroft enjoyed working in public health . She thinks it&amp;rsquo ; s vitally important work , and interesting too . But two years ago , after the election of Doug Ford&amp;rsquo ; s Progressive Conservative government , the situation at Public Health Ontario , and in the Ontario public health system at large , began to deteriorate , she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Ford government quickly froze PHO&amp;rsquo ; s budget and signalled that deeper cuts , to PHO and local health units , were on the way . The new administration also proved uninterested in some core public health missions , things like basic science . ? That hostility isn&amp;rsquo ; t entirely new . Some of it predates this government . &amp;ldquo ; But it did get worse under this government , &amp;rdquo ; Crowcroft said . &amp;ldquo ; The government has made it almost impossible for science to function within a public health organization . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It wasn&amp;rsquo ; t just science , either . Public Health Ontario was set up as an independent agency as a response to Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s failures during SARS . Crowcroft was specifically recruited to help with that effort . But the new government didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to hear about that part of PHO&amp;rsquo ; s history and mission , according to Crowcroft. &amp;ldquo ; The other message that was around before this happened was that people were saying , &amp;lsquo ; Don&amp;rsquo ; t talk about SARS anymore . The government doesn&amp;rsquo ; t want to hear about SARS , &amp;rdquo ; she said , &amp;ldquo ; which is supremely ironic right now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hayley Chazan , a spokeswoman for Health Minister Christine Elliott , said in an email that that direction &amp;ldquo ; was never provided by the office of the minister of health . &amp;rdquo ; She also pointed to numerous ways the &lt;a href= " https : **33;4855;TOOLONG ... rel= " noopener " target= " blank " &gt;lessons learned from SARS&lt;/a&gt; have guided the provincial response to &lt;a href= " https : **32;4890;TOOLONG " rel= " noopener " target= " blank " &gt;COVID-19&lt;/a&gt; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last summer , Crowcroft left Public Health Ontario to head up a new Centre for Vaccine Preventable Diseases at the University of Toronto . In June , she joined the World Health Organization . She hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been replaced at PHO . Her old job , chief scientist , no longer appears in the agency&amp;rsquo ; s leadership chart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Crowcroft wasn&amp;rsquo ; t alone , either . In the year before the &lt;a href= " https : **33;4924;TOOLONG ... rel= " noopener " target= " blank " &gt;COVID-19 pandemic&lt;/a&gt; struck , Public Health Ontario lost a host of senior leaders in crucial roles . Some were pushed out due to budget constraints or organizational restructuring . Others took early retirement . Some were replaced . Many were not . &amp;ldquo ; I think there was maybe one person who was probably going to retire anyway , &amp;rdquo ; said Crowcroft. &amp;ldquo ; But my impression from the people I do know is an overwhelming majority were not necessarily planning to go at that time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The loss of all that leadership , expertise and experience left Public Health Ontario undermanned in several areas crucial to pandemic response as the SARS-CoV-2 virus began to sweep the province earlier this year , according to multiple former senior PHO officials . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The province has been repeatedly criticized for its efforts on data collection and sharing during the pandemic . In early June , the government appointed former federal Liberal cabinet minister Dr. Jane Philpott as a special advisor on data in an effort to clean up the problem . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But until last year , Public Health Ontario had its own senior-level expert on data collection , presentation and sharing . Julian Martalog was hired as PHO&amp;rsquo ; s first director of informatics in 2016. &amp;ldquo ; My role was really just to make sure the organization had a way of transforming all the data they were collecting into information and knowledge , &amp;rdquo ; he said . Martalog was laid off last year . He hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been replaced . His position no longer appears on PHO&amp;rsquo ; s leadership chart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think it was a little bit strange that they decided to eliminate that role in particular , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I like to think that with this situation now , I would have been able to continue to make an impact and provide strong leadership , but it is what it is. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Martalog has watched the provincial pandemic response with some unease and confusion . &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s stuff that comes out every day that makes me scratch my head , &amp;rdquo ; he said . He doesn&amp;rsquo ; t understand why critical information on things like deaths , infection rates and locations hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been better presented and easier to access . He believes that , if he was still at PHO , he could have made a difference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I would have been heavily involved in that whole effort just to make sure that the right information is presented in a timely way , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Communication in general has been a sore spot for the province during the pandemic . Premier Ford and Chief Medical Officer of Health David Williams have at times presented conflicting messages in back-to-back to press conferences . Williams in particular has come under fire for his dry and sometimes convoluted delivery . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ironically , Public Health Ontario had one of the country&amp;rsquo ; s leading experts on what&amp;rsquo ; s known as &amp;ldquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;4959;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;risk communication&lt;/a&gt; &amp;rdquo ; on staff until the end of March . Dr. Ray Copes was the agency&amp;rsquo ; s chief of environmental and occupational health . Part of his job was to train other public health units and officials on how to speak to the public about health risks and crises in ways that are coherent , calming and easy to absorb . There is no one left in the agency with that level of expertise , according to one senior official still in public health . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Copes&amp;rsquo ; departure from PHO was planned last year . But his final day on the job wasn&amp;rsquo ; t until the end of March , when the pandemic was already in full flight . He wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t say specifically why he left or if he was asked by PHO to stay on until the crisis waned . But he did say this about the general situation at the top of his former workplace : &amp;ldquo ; There certainly are some folks that aren&amp;rsquo ; t there anymore , who I think really could have made a contribution to the response had that been requested , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not aware that that was done in the case of Public Health Ontario . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked about risk communication in general during the pandemic , Copes was effusive in his praise for British Columbia&amp;rsquo ; s Dr. Bonnie Henry . She benefits from being atop a coherently organized , well-managed public health system , something Ontario lacks , Copes said . But she&amp;rsquo ; s also a great communicator who&amp;rsquo ; s been given the space on most days to be B.C.&amp;rsquo ; s sole voice on scientific , technical and health matters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Ontario , Copes said &amp;mdash ; and this predates the current government &amp;mdash ; politicians have a habit of putting themselves out front , even when that&amp;rsquo ; s not the best strategy . That&amp;rsquo ; s something , if he was still on the job , he would have advised against . &amp;ldquo ; How credible , ultimately , is a lay elected representative speaking about medical or scientific matters ? &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s true Williams himself isn&amp;rsquo ; t much of a speaker . But there&amp;rsquo ; s no reason Williams had to be the province&amp;rsquo ; s only , or even lead , expert voice on the pandemic . PHO used to have a deep roster of scientists who were both experts and experienced crisis communicators . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That hasn&amp;rsquo ; t been the case with COVID-19 . Many of those experts , including Crowcroft , have since left the agency . &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve done tons of this kind of stuff . If I would have been there , I&amp;rsquo ; m sure I would have been pulled into it , &amp;rdquo ; Crowcroft said . &amp;ldquo ; But it&amp;rsquo ; s not just about having the right person . You have to have the environment and the vision and the commitment to meet the mandate of the organization , which is protecting the health of Ontarians. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several other senior leaders &amp;mdash ; in addition to Crowcroft , Martalog and Copes &amp;mdash ; have left the agency in the past year . PHO&amp;rsquo ; s former chief of health promotion , Dr. Brent Moloughney , is now Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s Associate Medical Officer of Health . His departure was a personal decision , he said . He wanted to be in Ottawa to be closer to his aging parents . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nicholas Paul , who as recently as January of this year , was PHO&amp;rsquo ; s laboratory operations director , was removed from the agency&amp;rsquo ; s online leadership chart sometime before March . He couldn&amp;rsquo ; t be reached for comment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; John McLaughlin , Crowcroft&amp;rsquo ; s predecessor as chief scientist , retired from PHO last year , on schedule , after turning 65. &amp;ldquo ; I regret seeing that other people have left , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; Sometimes there was illness , sometimes there was opportunity , but it seemed that just a coincidence of things has led to a lot of senior people not being there at a time like this . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There are other , temporary , absences , too . Mike Mendaglio , the agency&amp;rsquo ; s chief laboratory operations officer , is on medical leave , as is Peter Donnelly , PHO&amp;rsquo ; s president and CEO . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall , according to PHO&amp;rsquo ; s organizational charts , the agency eliminated six leadership positions between June 2019 and January 2020 . Since then , at least two more senior leaders , Copes and Moloughney , have left the agency , while a third , Paul , no longer appears on the online version of the chart . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In an email , Janet Wong , a spokeswoman for the agency , said that , as &amp;ldquo ; with the majority of publicly funded organizations in Ontario , Public Health Ontario has had to streamline our operations to align with fiscal realities . &amp;rdquo ; That restructuring has led to the departure of some staff , she added . &amp;ldquo ; However , these staff changes have not impacted PHO&amp;rsquo ; s ability to respond to the pandemic. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Elliott&amp;rsquo ; s spokeswoman , Hayley Chazan , said PHO has served &amp;ldquo ; as a valued partner&amp;rdquo ; throughout the COVID-19 outbreak . &amp;ldquo ; We have benefited immensely from the advice and counsel from many of the organization&amp;rsquo ; s leaders , including Dr. Peter Donnelly , who helped lead the release of initial modelling , Dr. Shelley Deeks , who has helped develop our case and contact management plan , and Dr. Vanessa Allen , who is playing a key role in guiding Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s testing strategy and has also played a critical role in setting up our provincial laboratory network . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But that appreciation for the agency did not predate COVID-19 , according to multiple sources both inside and outside PHO. &amp;ldquo ; Certainly there was no love shown to public health in Ontario by the government , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Bob Bell , a medical doctor and health care administrator who served as Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s deputy minister of health and long-term care between 2014 and 2018. &amp;ldquo ; The funding cuts at Public Health Ontario can not have been useful to the response to COVID-19 . And when you look at the pennies saved compared to the dollars cost &amp;mdash ; that Ontario is going to suffer by delaying its reopening because of a lack of organized public health effectiveness &amp;mdash ; it&amp;rsquo ; s like , &amp;lsquo ; boy , was that ever stupid . &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some who have left the agency , meanwhile , believe the funding freeze and the institutional changes in the first years of the Ford administration may have compounded existing problems within the agency to such a degree that , in a year&amp;rsquo ; s time , PHO may not exist at all . &amp;ldquo ; Which would be a real shame because of all the effort that went in to creating it , &amp;rdquo ; said Crowcroft. &amp;ldquo ; And the reasons for needing it are still there . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On a certain level , the COVID pandemic is the just the bottom swing of a public health yo-yo that has happened over and over again in Ontario . &amp;ldquo ; You run in these circles where public health advocates get silenced gradually , and then there&amp;rsquo ; s a crisis . And then everyone goes &amp;lsquo ; Oh my God , what happened ? Why didn&amp;rsquo ; t we have the lab testing we needed ? Why didn&amp;rsquo ; t we have the right scientific advice ? Why was there no good public health leadership ? And it&amp;rsquo ; s like , &amp;lsquo ; Oh yeah , we shut that down , remember ? &amp;rsquo ; &amp;rdquo ; Crowcroft said . &amp;ldquo ; These aren&amp;rsquo ; t new stories . It just seems like Ontario is doomed to relive its history again and again . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; Richard WarnicaTue , 23 Jun 2020 15:32:51 **45;4994;TOOLONG ... has power to end Meng extradition , bring Canadians home from China , Kovrig 's wife sayshttp : **36;5041;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;5079;TOOLONG ... title= " Vina Nadjibulla with her husband Michael Kovrig , who is being detained in China . " " data-portal-copyright= " Sean Kilpatrick/The Canadian Press " **28;5425;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576146 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;mdash ; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s new auditor general says many audits have been cancelled or delayed &amp;ldquo ; indefinitely&amp;rdquo ; as her office faces budgetary constraints , as well as an increased workload due to COVID-19 and infrastructure spending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have some audits that are ongoing that were expected to be tabled in the House in the fall of 2020 . We have delayed those into 2021 , &amp;rdquo ; Auditor General Karen Hogan told members of the federal finance committee on Monday . &amp;ldquo ; All other audits unfortunately at this time other than one audit , under the commissioner of the environment , have been put on hold , cancelled or delayed indefinitely so that we can focus on Investing in Canada and COVID-19. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The reasons for the setbacks are essentially twofold . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Firstly , the Office of the Auditor General ( OAG ) needs more money in order to fulfil its consistently growing mandate , said Hogan , who was appointed June 8 , to replace her predecessor Michael Ferguson , who died in February 2019. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Last year , Ferguson told MPs that the office needed an extra $10.8 million in order to both hire new auditors and update the OAG&amp;rsquo ; s obsolete IT infrastructure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Now that the OAG has to audit Crown corporations as well as the federal government&amp;rsquo ; s $187-billion Investing in Canada infrastructure plan and COVID-19 aid measures , Hogan says she&amp;rsquo ; s going to need much more than that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The $10.8 million is an outdated request&amp;hellip ; I think it&amp;rsquo ; s fair to even say it&amp;rsquo ; s a significantly outdated request , &amp;rdquo ; she explained , adding that her office is still calculating the new budget request and would submit it to parliamentarians soon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;5455;TOOLONG ... taps Karen Hogan to replace the late Michael Ferguson as auditor **28;5490;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;5520;TOOLONG ... auditor general says lack of funds leaving him with outdated technology , not enough **27;5555;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p ... Secondly , the aforementioned Investing in Canada and COVID-19 audits are so large in scope , they will likely take years to complete fully and will consume much of the OAG&amp;rsquo ; s resources , Hogan warned . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A unanimous motion adopted by members of the finance committee earlier this month asked the OAG to audit all federal programs association to the pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Auditing all of the COVID programs would be astronomical , &amp;rdquo ; Hogan said , adding it would take many years and wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be in the best interests of Parliament or Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Instead , Hogan said her office is going to prioritize programs according to &amp;ldquo ; risk&amp;rdquo ; and where she thinks audits will have &amp;ldquo ; the best impact and value . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The first parts of the government&amp;rsquo ; s pandemic response plan being audited are the availability of personal protective equipment , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s food supply , and the Canada Emergency Response Benefit , a subsidy to help Canadians who lost their jobs or had their hours cut by the pandemic . The subsidy , which provides $2,000-a-month to individuals , is budgeted to cost the government $60 billion . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative finance critic Pierre Poilievre said Parliament is eager for answers , but MPs also want the auditor general to do a thorough job . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- With files from Canadian Press&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **27;5584;TOOLONG " **34;5613;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **25;5649;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;ChrisGNardi &; ... &lt;/p&gt; Christopher NardiTue , 23 Jun 2020 01:09:55 **45;5676;TOOLONG ... " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;5793;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576115 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The victim of a home invasion murder in London , Ont. , is reportedly a former general in a West African rebel militia who once was the subject of intense investigation by Canadian war crimes investigators . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bill Horace , 44 , was killed in the early morning around 4:40 am Sunday when four men , three of them in hooded sweatshirts and hospital-style facemasks , forced their way into a house . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The &lt;em&gt;London Free Press&lt;/em&gt; reported a neighbour saw two go to the front door and two to the back , before hearing breaking glass , a struggle , and seeing Horace run out the front door where he was shot . Police said Horace&amp;rsquo ; s family was in the home at the time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; CTV quoted a neighbour who heard one gun shot and saw the men leave in two cars . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Paramedics took the victim to hospital , where he was pronounced dead . Police in London said they do not believe this was a random incident , but said nothing about why Horace , who lived in Toronto , may have been specifically targeted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The London Police Service has received several media inquiries about the identity of the deceased and possible historical association to National Patriotic Front in Liberia . &amp;nbsp ; The London Police Service is aware of these inquiries , but can not confirm this fact at this time , &amp;rdquo ; a statement said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several media outlets , in Canada and Africa , identified Horace as the former leader of a rebel militia fighting the brutal war in Liberia and Sierra Leone in the 1990s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian journalist and historian Michael Petrou was among those who drew the link on Twitter . His reporting a decade ago in **30;5823;TOOLONG revealed Horace was a former general in the National Patriotic Front of Liberia ( NPFL ) living freely in Toronto despite being accused by witnesses and former associates of conducting and commanding &amp;ldquo ; atrocities on a massive scale . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Everyone around here used to go to the big palm nut farm to cut palm and make oil to eat and sell , &amp;rdquo ; according to a witness at Liberia&amp;rsquo ; s Truth and Reconciliation , quoted by Petrou. &amp;ldquo ; Gen. Bill Horace and his men were passing . They entered the plantation and accused us of looting the place . He then ordered his men to arrest people . They started chasing us , and everybody was running all over the place . They then started firing at us . I first saw one woman fall . The bullet hit her on the head . Her husband was crying . Then one of the other fighters shot him also . Both of them died instantly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Two years later , in 2012 , it emerged Canada was formally investigating Horace for war crimes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That same year , the former president of Liberia and leader of the NPFL , Charles Taylor , was convicted at the United Nations-backed Special Court for Sierra Leone for planning , aiding and abetting war crimes , some of the worst in recorded history . He is now serving a 50 year sentence in a British prison . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;5855;TOOLONG ... title= " Firefighters at the scene of a fatal home shooting in London , Ont. , on June 22 , 2020. " alt= " Firefighters at the scene of a fatal home shooting in London , Ont. , on June 22 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;5890;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576114 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In his book &lt;em&gt;American Warlord&lt;/em&gt; , based on the life of Taylor&amp;rsquo ; s American son , Chucky , author Johnny Dwyer reports that by 1992 Horace was in fact the boyfriend of Taylor&amp;rsquo ; s daughter , Zoe . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Horace had a reputation for brutality that stood out even among Taylor&amp;rsquo ; s rebels , &amp;rdquo ; Dwyer writes . &amp;ldquo ; He served as a commander of a contingent of Taylor fighters called the &amp;lsquo ; Marine Division , &amp;rsquo ; overseeing a sprawling area of operations in Grand Bassa and Maryland County surrounding the port city of Buchanan . &amp;nbsp ; In a civil war characterized by atrocities , the Marine Division carried a singular reputation for depredations. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As Dwyer reports , one of Taylor&amp;rsquo ; s generals would , years later , point out to a truth commission : &amp;ldquo ; If the Marine pass somewhere you will know &amp;hellip ; because of the flies &amp;hellip ; the whole NPFL was afraid of them , even the ( sic ) Charles Taylor was afraid of them . The Marine was the ones that brought the wickedness to the people . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A Marine member told the commission that the unit had a licence from Taylor to &amp;ldquo ; kill anybody and destroy anything&amp;rdquo ; and were told not to take any prisoners . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another fighter in the war told Dwyer that Horace was known for a particularly gruesome method of killing : &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He was in the habit of killing the crucifix way , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; the&amp;nbsp ; man said . Horace , he said , would mount his victims onto wooden crosses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Many of us ( Liberians ) are here in Canada as a result of that war , &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; Leo Johnson , president of the Liberian Association of Canada , told the &lt;em&gt; London Free Press . &lt;/em&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We became refugees directly as a result of that war&amp;hellip ; It has become a reality in the Liberian community that our community is a mix of victims of war crimes and the perpetrators at the same time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s integrated War Crimes Program was launched in 1988 when Canada generally stopped trying to prosecute war crimes and focused instead on excluding and deporting war criminals . Canada has since adopted laws to help cooperate with international tribunals , such as the one that convicted Taylor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This was the second homicide in 2020 in London , Ont. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- With files from London Free Press&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; National Post StaffTue , 23 Jun 2020 00:21:30 **45;5920;TOOLONG ... ca n't wait years ' : Airlines tell MPs they want COVID-19 rules eased so their business can restarthttp : **33;5967;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;6002;TOOLONG ... title= " While they are calling on the government to ease restrictions , he said they still expect it will be a long way before things return to normal . " data-portal-copyright= " Jack Boland/Postmedia/File " **28;6037;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576084 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;ndash ; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s airlines want the government to ease travel restrictions , border closures and mandatory quarantines so their businesses can take off again . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Representatives from Air Canada , WestJet and Air Transat all testified at a House of Commons health committee , with a unified message that it&amp;rsquo ; s time for Canada to gradually open up to the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ferio Pugliese , a senior vice president of government relations for Air Canada , told MPs that other countries are moving to reopen quicker . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada needs to move at a better pace than it has thus far , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; Canada we believe now needs to work urgently with stakeholder to reopen its borders . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pugliese said Air Canada has cut hundreds of flights and thousands of jobs and is working at five per cent of its usual capacity . He said , long-term , airlines still have fixed costs and they will become more and more unmanageable if planes stay grounded . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As long as these restrictions stay in place and we are not looking at balanced ways of reopening the economy , it will have a tremendous impact on the financial viability of these businesses . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Currently , with a few exceptions , only Canadian citizens and permanent residents are allowed to enter the country . Canadians returning from overseas have to quarantine for 14 days upon their arrival and some provinces are even requiring quarantines for people arriving from other parts of Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Government relations director Howard Liebman , with Air Transat , said his company can&amp;rsquo ; t wait until the virus goes away . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We can&amp;rsquo ; t wait years until there is a widely available vaccine to get back in the air . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said the company has tentative plans to open up this summer , but they know the flying public won&amp;rsquo ; t be returning in waves . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Liebman said Transat , as a leisure operator , has been completely grounded since the pandemic began and is feeling the strain . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have not operated or sold tickets in more than three months , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; There is no business or industry that can go for three months without revenue or operations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jared Mikoch-Gerke , WestJet&amp;rsquo ; s manager of aviation security , said the Calgary-based airline had grounded two-thirds of its fleet and cut thousands of jobs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While they are calling on the government to ease restrictions , he said they still expect it will be a long way before things return to normal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t anticipate returning to pre-COVID levels until 2022 , &amp;rdquo ; he told the committee . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of the panelists said Canada should move gradually , opening up to specific countries with low levels of COVID-19 transmission , safe corridors that would prevent a wider outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These would be routes between international locations that have done a good job , &amp;rdquo ; said Mikoch-Gerke. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The airlines said they are using multiple safety measures , including enhanced cleaning , requiring masks and screening at airports , but said the commercial aviation businesses needs to resume for the Canadian economy to take off . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Commercial aviation is an essential service and an element of critical infrastructure , &amp;rdquo ; said Mikoch-Gerke. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before the committee met Monday , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau was asked about easing restrictions to help the tourism industry . He said he feels for the industry , but Canada can&amp;rsquo ; t take any chances . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We know that reopening too quickly or carelessly would lead us to a resurgence that might well force us to go back into lockdown , to shut down the economy once again and nobody wants that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said the government had offered financial support for businesses specifically so they wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have to take unnecessary risks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have significant supports out for industries , for businesses large and small and for Canadians to be able to make it through the coming months , but we are going to be very , very careful about when and how we start reopening international borders . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several MPs were also skeptical of allowing airlines to reopen or removing the current restrictions &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; With cases rising in many parts of the world , NDP MP Don Davies asked the airlines if they were worried opening the borders could be a terrible decision . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We might be walking into a second wave , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Are you not concerned we may be walking into another flare-up , &amp;rdquo ; he asked the airlines . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of the airlines admitted reopening the borders comes with some risk , but they said they are confident it can be done safely . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are in the safety business and we are in the risk management business , that&amp;rsquo ; s what we do , &amp;rdquo ; said Pugliese. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several MPs also grilled the airlines about refunds for passengers . Most airlines have offered travel credits or vouchers for the thousands of cancelled flights that resulted from COVID-19. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative MP Matt Jeneroux said it is unfair to consumers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s unfathomable to me that if someone paid for a service , but didn&amp;rsquo ; t receive that service that they would still be charged for that service . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Pugliese said Air Canada had refunded almost $1 billion in flights for customers who bought tickets that included the option of a cash refund . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said the current rules don&amp;rsquo ; t require cash refunds in cases like this and they have extended the travel vouchers they are offering so they won&amp;rsquo ; t expire as quickly . More than 30,000 Canadians have signed a petition , submitted to the House of Commons requiring cash refunds for cancelled flights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mikoch-Gerke with WestJet said the current rules allow airlines to offer credits when the cause for a cancellation is out of their control . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We do believe that refunding and reimbursing with travel credits is an appropriate response in exceptional circumstances . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **29;6067;TOOLONG " **36;6098;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a class= " twitter-follow-button " href= " https : **25;6136;TOOLONG " &gt; **32;6163;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Ryan TumiltyMon , 22 Jun 2020 23:43:25 **45;6197;TOOLONG ...
