" data-portal-copyright= " Fran ? ois Ollivier/Getty Images/File " **28;6315;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576057 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; EDMONTON &amp;mdash ; A Saudi dissident living in Canada said he has been informed by the Royal Canadian Mounted Police that there have been threats against him made by Saudi Arabia &amp;mdash ; but that won&amp;rsquo ; t stop his popular YouTube broadcasts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Because of the fear of the threats I am facing , I decided to stop doing the weekly broadcasts , &amp;rdquo ; Omar Abdulaziz wrote on Twitter . &amp;ldquo ; So it will be a daily broadcast , God willing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The news comes just weeks after the &lt;em&gt;National Post&lt;/em&gt; reported the children of another exiled Saudi , &lt;a href= " https : **33;6345;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Saad Aljabri&lt;/a&gt; , a former intelligence official , had been apparently abducted in Saudi , an attempt , his family believes , to force Aljabri to return to the kingdom . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aljabri has been living in Canada since 2017. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In recent years , other Saudi refugees and dissidents have also made their way to Canada . After fleeing her family while on vacation in Kuwait in 2019 , Rahaf Mohammed , who was then 18 years old , was held up in Bangkok , Thailand , before being granted asylum &lt;a href= " https : **33;6380;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;in Canada&lt;/a&gt; . Mohammed said she&amp;rsquo ; d been abused by her family , and was one of the &amp;ldquo ; lucky ones&amp;rdquo ; in managing to escape . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;6415;TOOLONG ... of ex-Saudi intelligence official living in Canada disappear amid Saudi efforts to force him **25;6450;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;6477;TOOLONG ... friend of Khashoggi living in Canada thinks their texts led to journalist 's **28;6512;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... In 2019 , 312 Saudis applied for asylum in Canada , and 282 had their claims granted . &amp;nbsp ; The top countries of origin for asylum seekers in 2019 were Iran , with 2,744 accepted claims and Turkey , with 2,043 accepted claims . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Thomas Juneau , a professor in the faculty of Public and International Affairs at the University of Ottawa , said while Canada is a haven sought by Saudi dissidents , they&amp;rsquo ; re found all around the world . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s important not to overstate it . It&amp;rsquo ; s a destination , it&amp;rsquo ; s not &lt;em&gt;the&lt;/em&gt; destination , &amp; ... Juneau said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abdulaziz , 29 , has been in Canada since he came as a university student in 2009 . In 2014 , he claimed asylum after his scholarship to study in Canada was revoked . Over the years , there have been various pressure campaigns mounted against him &amp;mdash ; and against friends and family back home &amp;mdash ; to stop his criticisms of the Saudi government . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a report in&amp;nbsp ; &lt;em&gt;Vanity Fair&lt;/em&gt; in September 2019 , Abdulaziz said two Saudi representatives , accompanied by Abdulaziz&amp;rsquo ; s brother , came to Montreal . &amp;ldquo ; They encouraged him to stop his activism and return home , urging him to visit the Saudi Embassy to renew his passport , &amp;rdquo ; the &lt;em&gt;Vanity Fair&lt;/em&gt; story says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abdulaziz rejected the offer . His understanding , &lt;em&gt;Vanity Fair&lt;/em&gt; reported , was that his brother was jailed after that . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While pressure by the regime against dissidents abroad isn&amp;rsquo ; t especially unique , what&amp;rsquo ; s unusual in this case is there appears to have been real and serious threats made , said Juneau. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Abdulaziz , who could not be reached for comment Monday , told &lt;em&gt;The Guardian&lt;/em&gt; the kingdom wants &amp;ldquo ; to do something&amp;rdquo ; to him . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But I don&amp;rsquo ; t know whether it&amp;rsquo ; s assassination , kidnapping , I don&amp;rsquo ; t know &amp;ndash ; but something not OK for sure . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saudi Arabia&amp;rsquo ; s embassy in Ottawa did not respond to a request for comment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 2018 , the University of Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s CitizenLab , a research group on &amp;ldquo ; digital threats to civil society and high-level policy engagement , &amp;rdquo ; published a report detailing how Abdulaziz , who produces &lt;a href= " https : **33;6542;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;a YouTube show&lt;/a&gt; and is a vocal online critic of the Saudi regime , was being monitored through spyware on his iPhone , a program called &amp;ldquo ; Pegasus , &amp;rdquo ; which is produced by the Israel-based cyber warfare firm NSO Group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a boilerplate statement , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s foreign affairs ministry said : &amp;ldquo ; The promotion and protection of human rights , including the right to freedom of thought , conscience , and religion , are an integral part of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s foreign policy . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The RCMP said they could not confirm an investigation , charges laid or the identities involved , because that would happen &amp;ldquo ; only in the event that an investigation results in the laying of criminal charges . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saudi Arabia is known for aggressively pursuing dissidents at home &amp;mdash ; and abroad &amp;mdash ; with tactics including assassination , such as the 2018 murder of &lt;em&gt;Washington Post&lt;/em&gt; columnist Jamal Khashoggi in the Saudi consulate in Istanbul . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s relations with Saudi Arabia have been tense in recent years . Foreign affairs minister Chrystia Freeland caused a diplomatic dust-up in 2018 by calling for the release from imprisonment of Samar Badawi and Raif Badawi , dissidents who have ties to Canada . In response , Saudi Arabia recalled its ambassador and accused Canada of &amp;ldquo ; blatant interference in the Kingdom&amp;rsquo ; s domestic affairs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Juneau described the threats against Abdulaziz as another example of the issues making it&amp;nbsp ; difficult for relations to improve . &amp;ldquo ; The issue of Saudi pressure on dissidents and opponents is one sore point , it absolutely is , but it&amp;rsquo ; s important to remember there are other issues around that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;6577;TOOLONG " **35;6607;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **26;6644;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;tylerrdawson ... &lt;/p&gt; Tyler DawsonMon , 22 Jun 2020 23:17:53 **45;6672;TOOLONG ... goes back to the future with idea of provincial police force to replace RCMPhttp : **36;6719;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;6757;TOOLONG ... title= " An Alberta Provincial Police mounted troop . " alt= " An Alberta Provincial Police mounted troop . " data-portal-copyright= " Provincial Archives of Alberta " **28;6792;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576020 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; EDMONTON &amp;mdash ; Alberta is considering severing its contract with the Royal Canadian Mounted Police , a move that would require the creation &amp;mdash ; or , rather , re-creation &amp;mdash ; of a provincial police force . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the province&amp;rsquo ; s 115-year history until right now , the RCMP &amp;mdash ; or its precursor , the North-West Mounted Police &amp;mdash ; has provided policing services for much of Alberta , particularly rural areas and small towns . But in the wake of recommendations from a panel seeking out ideas on a &amp;ldquo ; fair deal&amp;rdquo ; for Alberta within confederation , the province may consider creating its own police force , as it did for roughly 15 years around the time of the First World War . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Between 1917 and 1932 , Alberta was policed by the Alberta Provincial Police . The force came to be mainly because the RCMP&amp;rsquo ; s ranks were being drained by the war effort . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But , according to the work of Sean Moir , an Edmonton historian cited in a 2007 &lt;em&gt;Edmonton Journal&lt;/em&gt; article , and corroborated from historical newspaper clippings of the time , there was another reason : The RCMP didn&amp;rsquo ; t want to police Prohibition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;6822;TOOLONG ... says Alberta should consider ditching CPP , RCMP ; Kenney considers **28;6857;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;6887;TOOLONG ... video shows RCMP tackling and punching Alberta chief Allan Adam in **27;6922;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p ... Prohibition had come into effect across the country by 1918 . But provinces had put in their own liquor bans prior to that , along with some municipalities and counties under the 1878 Canada Temperance Act . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My argument is that these guys had gone through it before and they knew what it was going to be like to enforce very unpopular liquor legislation . They knew it was going to be a no-win situation , &amp;rdquo ; Moir told the **28;6951;TOOLONG . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An April 1918 &lt;a href= " http : **34;6981;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;newspaper clipping&lt;/a&gt; from the Edmonton Bulletin newspaper , on the debate over a provincial police force , cites Alberta Premier Charles Stewart as saying the &amp;ldquo ; Red Coats&amp;rdquo ; had no desire to enforce Prohibition . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even when Prohibition ended &amp;mdash ; the first day the taps ran in Alberta was May 12 , 1924 &amp;mdash ; the Alberta Provincial Police stuck around . Until the Great Depression hit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;7017;TOOLONG ... title= " In the province 's 115-year history until right now , the RCMP -- or its precursor , the North-West Mounted Police -- has provided policing services for much of Alberta . " alt= " In the province 's 115-year history until right now , the RCMP -- or its precursor , the North-West Mounted Police -- has provided policing services for much of Alberta . " data-portal-copyright= " Global News via Postmedia/File " **28;7052;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78576031 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; In 1930 , Premier John Brownlee asked politicians for ideas on how to save money that would not affect services . The proposal made by Conservative leader David Duggan was to scrap the police and bring in the RCMP , for savings of $250,000 per year ( about $3.7 million in today&amp;rsquo ; s dollars ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Duggan&amp;rsquo ; s proposal failed , but by 1932 the economics had deteriorated sufficiently , and legislators reinstated the Dominion government&amp;rsquo ; s police force . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The current 20-year contract , signed in 2011 under then-Alberta Premier Ed Stelmach , goes until 2032 . The province can still withdraw from the contract , provided it gives the federal government two year&amp;rsquo ; s notice . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The argument today for a provincial force are rising crime rates in rural areas of the province , lengthy response times to 911 calls , staffing and western alienation issues . The Fair Deal panel says a local force would be better positioned to serve Albertans. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; A stable police service that allowed members to stay grounded in local communities would be attractive to men and women considering law enforcement as a career , &amp;rdquo ; the report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , the panel warned that there would be cost issues associated with taking over the policing duties of the RCMP &amp;mdash ; just as there were a century ago . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Alberta pays roughly $262 million per year for the RCMP , with the federal government contributing $112 million per year . The latter figure would need to be absorbed by Alberta taxpayers in order to run a provincial force . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;With files from The Canadian Press&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;7082;TOOLONG " **35;7112;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **26;7149;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;tylerrdawson ... &lt;/p&gt; Tyler DawsonMon , 22 Jun 2020 22:26:40 **45;7177;TOOLONG ... campaign believes alleged hack came from MP 's office , says it has received confession letterhttp : **34;7224;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;7260;TOOLONG ... title= " Conservative leadership candidate Erin O'Toole ( left ) is alleging that someone passed sensitive login information to rival Peter MacKay 's campaign . " alt= " Conservative leadership candidate Erin O'Toole ( left ) is alleging that someone passed sensitive login information to rival Peter MacKay 's campaign . " data-portal-copyright= " " **28;7295;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575494 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Erin O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s Conservative leadership campaign is alleging that someone in a Member of Parliament&amp;rsquo ; s office passed sensitive login information to rival Peter MacKay&amp;rsquo ; s campaign , giving his chief competitor access to virtually all high-level campaign activity , according to a letter sent to police and obtained by the National Post . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The O&amp;rsquo ; Toole campaign believes at least 138 Zoom meetings in English were accessed and at least seven meetings in French . Some of the meetings were high-level strategy discussions among senior campaign figures . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The version of the letter obtained by the Post redacts the name of the person in the MP&amp;rsquo ; s office who is alleged to have taken the login credentials from the MP&amp;rsquo ; s email account . The O&amp;rsquo ; Toole campaign has obtained a confession letter from the person , according to a senior campaign source . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The campaign wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t identify the MP , but did acknowledge the person was an O&amp;rsquo ; Toole supporter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An O&amp;rsquo ; Toole staffer , who asked not to be identified in order to describe the inner workings of a campaign , said that if the allegations were true , then the MacKay campaign was able to &amp;ldquo ; effectively bug the virtual boardroom&amp;rdquo ; of its rival camp . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Campaigning in the time of COVID-19 , there are internal video conferences to replace boardroom meetings about the platform , the message-of-the-day , debate prep , issues management , strategic planning , fundraising . Mulroney later apologized , although that statement was undercut by her threat to sue for &amp;ldquo ; liable ( sic ) , &amp;rdquo ; a remark she later walked back . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ben Mulroney spoke only obliquely of &amp;ldquo ; the situation surrounding my wife&amp;rdquo ; in a statement he made Monday on CTV&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;em&gt;Your Morning&lt;/em&gt; program . &amp;ldquo ; I love my wife , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; However , it is not my place to speak for her , and today , together , we are committed to doing the work to both learn and understand more about anti-Black racism as well as learn and understand more about our blind spots . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He went on to note that &amp;ldquo ; my privilege has benefited me greatly , &amp;rdquo ; and that &amp;ldquo ; we need more Black voices , more Indigenous voices , more people of colour in the media as well as every other profession . &amp;rdquo ; He said he was stepping away &amp;ldquo ; to create space for a new perspective and a new voice , &amp;rdquo ; which he hoped might come from one of those groups . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Mulroney , 44 , has been part of **26;7764;TOOLONG since its current inception in 2002 . " data-portal-copyright= " Ernest Doroszuk/Toronto Sun/Postmedia " **28;7940;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575742 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A corrupt Toronto police officer was part of a criminal organization that used stolen encrypted police radios to unfairly profit in southern Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s lucrative and dangerous tow truck wars , police said Monday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Eleven people were arrested in the anti-corruption and organized crime probe , including a police constable and seven tow truck drivers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The constable was so entrenched in a towing cartel he not only profited by sharing police equipment and information , he also owned a car rental service and two tow trucks being operated by members of the group , police alleged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A police radio decrypts private communications between officers , allowing users to listen in . A consortium of tow truck drivers could then be the first to learn of traffic collisions and arrive at the scene to solicit towing business and other services , police said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several tow truck employees from three different towing companies had access to three stolen Toronto police radios , at least one of which was stolen from police by a police officer , Supt. Domenic Sinopoli alleged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Three radios were recovered &amp;mdash ; one of which went missing in February 2018 , suggesting the scheme may have operated for more than two years . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Highlighting the organization of the towing consortium , collision information from the radios was distributed using an internet app to other tow drivers who paid a monthly subscription fee . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The industry is rife with corruption , &amp;rdquo ; Sinopoli said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toronto police&amp;rsquo ; s Professional Standards Unit , which internally monitors the ethics and integrity of its members , started investigating theft of police radios last August . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As the investigation took on some life , the corruption revealed itself , &amp;rdquo ; said Sinopoli. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What we learned was a consortium of tow trucks were in possession of stolen police radios and were utilizing the encrypted transmissions to facilitate their business interests . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The arrested officer , Const . Ronald Joseph , 47 , of Toronto , who has been with Toronto police for 11 years , allegedly helped members of the consortium get a genuine police radio , which was then cloned and the cloned version checked back in with police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He used his position to steal and transfer a police radio that would give advantage to the group with respect to the ability to intercept police communication , attend more accidents or perhaps evade police detection , &amp;rdquo ; Sinopoli alleged . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;7970;TOOLONG ... title= " A policeman executes a search warrant in relation to Toronto 's tow truck war . " alt= " A policeman executes a search warrant in relation to Toronto 's tow truck war . " data-portal-copyright= " York Regional Police " **28;8005;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575749 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joseph is also accused of being paid for telling the group of accident locations as well as profiting from tows and rentals from his own business interests . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The officer would receive monetary compensation for the tow trucks , he would receive kickbacks for the tips he provided and he would receive referrals to his car rental agency , &amp;rdquo ; said Sinopoli. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toronto police knew Joseph owned a car rental service but were unaware of his ownership of tow trucks , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The scheme started to unravel in May , when officers stopped a tow truck driver on Highway 400 . The officers found a genuine Toronto police radio in the tow truck , Sinopoli said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The driver , Kevin Lima , 27 , of Barrie , Ont. , was charged with dangerous driving and released at the scene . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Police discovered the radio had the same identification as a radio at 22 Division . When officers examined the radio still being used by police officers , it was found to be a clone , meaning a replica that looks and acts like the real thing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A few weeks later , police searched several homes in Toronto , Barrie and Brampton and found a second Toronto police radio , reported lost or stolen from 14 Division . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also found were numerous other radios that appear to be from other organizations , possibly police agencies , and parts used for cloning radios , police said . Sinopoli said organizations will need to come in and try to identify their radios . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then , on June 9 , three tow truck drivers arrived at the Costco parking lot near the busy highway intersection of Highway 401 and Allen Rd. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Police found a third Toronto police radio and a loaded 9mm handgun , Sinopoli said . All three were arrested . Lima was one of them , along with Jacob Villeneuve Portela , 21 , of Toronto , and Giuseppe Carfora , 27 , of Guelph. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More police searches came on Thursday , at five homes and seven businesses in southern and northern Ontario . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Along with the 11 arrests police seized six tow trucks , a loaded handgun and $35,000 cash , police said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joseph worked in 22 Division , in the city&amp;rsquo ; s west-end , that includes a stretch of the busy Gardiner Expressway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sinopoli said Joseph&amp;rsquo ; s link to the group appears to be through a business relationship with Lima . He said he was uncertain how their relation was first built . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;8035;TOOLONG ... title= " A tow truck suspected to have been part of Toronto 's tow truck wars is pictured . " alt= " A tow truck suspected to have been part of Toronto 's tow truck wars is pictured . " data-portal-copyright= " York Regional Police " **28;8070;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575754 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Joseph was first arrested earlier this month as part of the probe and charged with breach of trust and theft . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He was arrested again on Thursday and charged with gangsterism &amp;mdash ; specifically participating in the activities of a criminal organization and commission of an offence for a criminal organization . He also faces charges of taking secret commissions , attempt fraud , breach of trust , possession of device to intercept private communications , trafficking property obtained by crime , counseling forgery and counseling breach of trust . He is suspended with pay pending the outcome of court proceedings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sinopoli said the alleged organized crime group was the towing consortium itself , rather than a previously known group such as a gang , biker club or Mafia clan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toronto police Chief Mark Saunders said the towing industry needs attention . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ll do everything we can , especially internally , to see what gaps may exist within our organization and tighten up on those , &amp;rdquo ; Saunders said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s not just up to the Toronto Police Service . I think what we need to have is a multi-layered approach involving different layers of government and also looking at the regulatory processes that exist . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lima faces the same gangsterism charges as Joseph , as well as of intercepting private communications , interception of radio-based communications , possession of property obtained by crime , possession of a device to intercept private communications , trafficking in property obtained by crime . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Also facing gangsterism and various other charges are : Francisco Mike Portela , 41 , of Toronto ; Mark Wolfe , 50 , of Toronto ; Leonardo Ciulla , 21 , of Mississauga ; Hernani Capela , 32 , of Vaughan ; Michael Carreiro , 28 , of Mississauga ; and Francesco Caruso , 49 , of Toronto . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Charged but not facing organized crime charges is Tyson Plourde , 39 , of Brampton . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They have a court appearance scheduled for Sept. 4. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; * Email : &lt;a href= " **31;8100;TOOLONG " **38;8133;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **25;8173;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;ADHumphreys ... &lt;/p&gt; Adrian HumphreysMon , 22 Jun 2020 20:24:18 **45;8200;TOOLONG ... some cities get ravaged by COVID-19 while others don'thttp : **35;8247;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;8284;TOOLONG ... title= " The Toronto skyline on June 2. Thompson / National Post " **28;8319;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575424 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s &lt;a href= " https : **32;8349;TOOLONG " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;COVID-19&lt;/a&gt; outbreak has so far been mostly confined to its two largest cities , with two-thirds of confirmed cases in Toronto and Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The question that consumes policymakers is whether that trend will continue or if the virus will someday sweep into the rest of the country with the same ferocity . The answer will sketch the outline of Canadian life in the months ahead . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; New research suggests , though , that certain cities are far more susceptible to the virus than others . The findings could be crucial for public health officials planning for a possible resurgence of the virus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think we can safely say that certain drivers , such as a dense way of living , as well as age structure , in particular , the share of the population living in nursing homes , will strongly continue to predict worse outcomes , &amp;rdquo ; said Klaus Desmet , an economist at Southern Methodist University , Texas , in an interview with the National Post . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For months , experts have been tracking the capricious path of the new coronavirus . It left an appalling trail of destruction in Italy , killing nearly 35,000 people , and then hit Germany with a glancing blow and a quarter of the deaths that Italy experienced . New York City suffered one of the worst outbreaks in the world while Los Angeles suffered a death toll one-sixth the size . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Canada , greater Toronto and Montreal account for more than 66,000 of the roughly 100,000 cases , while the rest of Canada accounts for the rest . Desmet says it&amp;rsquo ; s likely that Canada&amp;rsquo ; s largest two cities fell victim to the same characteristics as New York City . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What we find is that large metropolis are vulnerable mainly for two reasons . First , they are places with a dense way of life . Second , in large urban areas it is very hard to create a disease-free bubble because there is always inflow and outflow , &amp;rdquo ; said Desmet. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;8383;TOOLONG ... pandemic prompts urbanites to rethink ' grand bargain ' of dense city **27;8418;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;8447;TOOLONG ... in one day : Sunday saw the biggest-ever daily increase in new COVID-19 **26;8482;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... By isolating each variable , Desmet and Romain Wacziarg , an economist at the University of California , Los Angeles , have found that a city&amp;rsquo ; s density is a huge factor , along with the age of the population and its distance to an international airport . Most importantly , the researchers believe that these characteristics are likely to define the shape of the outbreak in the future . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The evidence so far suggests the severity of the disease will not equal out across cities , but remain uneven and with some cities grappling with persistent outbreaks while others remain relatively calm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; If this trend continues , it means officials can concentrate resources in areas with the highest susceptibility without fearing that other areas will be left vulnerable to their own outbreak . It also means certain places can have less stringent lockdowns and earlier reopenings without the risk of overwhelming the health care systems . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;8510;TOOLONG ... title= " Passengers wearing protective masks ride a streetcar in Toronto on June 18. " alt= " Passengers wearing protective masks ride a streetcar in Toronto on June 18. " data-portal-copyright= " Stephanie Foden/Bloomberg " **28;8545;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575428 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Density and public **25;8575;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The most obvious guess about what drives the severity of an outbreak is density . Anyone skimming the list of cities particularly affected by COVID-19 will see a list of North America&amp;rsquo ; s most dense cities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This should come as no surprise : as with any other infectious disease , contact between susceptible and infected individuals is a key determinant of the spread of the disease , &amp;rdquo ; reads a study from Desmet and Wacziarg on the differences in COVID-19 outbreaks across the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study considers several features of density , including the share of individuals living in multi-unit housing structures and the number of people per household . Another factor measures the amount of density a random person in the city or county will encounter in a square kilometre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The researchers found multi-unit housing and the size of households are both positively associated with cases and deaths , and they expect this to be a feature of any future outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The research also found that places with more people using public transit have much worse COVID-19 outbreaks , particularly in terms of deaths . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Any epidemiological model of infectious disease will tell you that one of the most important drivers of the spread of the disease is the infection rate . Obviously that infection rate depends on how many other people an infected individual bumps into every day ( and ) that&amp;rsquo ; ll be much larger if I use the metro than if I live on an isolated farm on the countryside , &amp;rdquo ; said Desmet. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;8602;TOOLONG ... title= " Terminal 1 at Toronto 's Pearson Airport on June 18. " alt= " Terminal 1 at Toronto 's Pearson Airport on June 18. " data-portal-copyright= " Peter J. Thompson / National Post " **28;8637;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575422 " /&gt; **29;8667;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau announced in March that international flights would only be arriving in Vancouver , Calgary , Toronto and Montreal , which provided laboratory conditions for measuring the effects of international travel on the pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the United States , the researchers also found a clear correlation between a city&amp;rsquo ; s distance to an international airport and an early outbreak of COVID-19. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a randomness in locations that got the virus early . One important exception is the variable capturing distance to international airports with connections to the top-5 COVID incidence countries as of March 15 , &amp;rdquo ; the study reports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In Toronto , the higher infection rates clustered around the airport . Peel Region , which is home to Pearson International Airport , has a rate of 328 cases per 100,000 people . That&amp;rsquo ; s one of the highest rates in the province , second only to the city of Toronto itself , which has 394 cases per 100,000 people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This could also be what drove some small differences in case numbers in Alberta . In Calgary , where the airport remained open to international flights , the infection rates surged ahead of Edmonton , where the airport is smaller , farther away from the city and closed to international travel . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s almost certain that cities close to international airports get infected earlier than others , but Desmet said it&amp;rsquo ; s less clear whether they will be worse off in the long run . The evidence does seem to be pointing in that direction , he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Age of population&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; Interestingly , the researchers found that if a county has more people over the age of 75 , it is likely to have fewer cases . This is likely because younger people are more likely to take part in activities that spread the virus , like large social gatherings and events . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Places with a large share of retired individuals may feature fewer places ( bars , stadiums ) where the disease spreads rapidly , &amp;rdquo ; the study says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And although an older population tends to be associated with lower transmission , in some situations it can lead to more deaths . This could also be driven by outbreaks at nursing homes , which have seen horrifying death rates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This finding is consistent with the idea that once a county is affected by the pandemic , its nursing homes can quickly become powderkegs , and account for larger shares of countywide deaths . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;8698;TOOLONG ... title= " The line for Zara 's Queen Street West location in Toronto on June 15. " alt= " The line for Zara 's Queen Street West location in Toronto on June 15. " data-portal-copyright= " Peter J. Thompson / National Post " **28;8733;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78575423 " /&gt; &lt;h3&gt;Other factors&lt;/h3&gt; &lt;p&gt; The researchers found that some widely hypothesized risk factors for COVID-19 may not be that important . For example , the share of smokers and obese people does not seem to be a big driver of high mortality rates . Even the quality of health care infrastructure seems to be much less important than other factors like the density of the city and the age of the population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Although a person&amp;rsquo ; s level of education itself seems to have little bearing on the severity of an outbreak , poverty and inequality seem to be big drivers . Poverty has a strong bearing on the mortality rate and inequality correlates with more cases of COVID-19. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the United States , race also matters a great deal . Outbreaks are more severe in areas with more Black and Hispanic people , both in terms of the number of cases and deaths . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Overall , these results confirm concerns that the COVID-19 pandemic has a disparate effect on various racial groups , &amp;rdquo ; the paper reads . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of these trends are important , but they aren&amp;rsquo ; t the final story . For instance , the fact that New Jersey is right beside New York is far more influential to its outbreak potential than any state-specific characteristics . Likewise , the fact that Hawaii is a remote island is a much better predictor than any other characteristic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study also emphasizes one of the most vexing features of a virus outbreak , that so many of the activities that make for a happy life now put people at an increased risk . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Taken together , being connected to many other people makes you vulnerable . Ironically , what is very beneficial in normal times for the economy , makes you vulnerable in these abnormal times , &amp;rdquo ; said Desmet. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **30;8763;TOOLONG " **37;8795;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **28;8834;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;stuartxthoms ... &lt;/p&gt; Stuart ThomsonMon , 22 Jun 2020 18:50:14 **45;8864;TOOLONG ... **30;8911;TOOLONG has failed for months to serve plane crash lawsuit on Iran , as legally required , lawyer sayshttp : **36;8943;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;8981;TOOLONG ... title= " Ryan Pourjam , 13 , son of Mansour Pourjam , is embraced by family friend Mahmoud Rastgou , after a ceremony at Carleton University to honour Pourjam , a biology alumnus on Jan. 15 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " GUILLERMO ARIAS/AFP via Getty Images " **28;9506;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78574652 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;ndash ; Canadian experts looking at a surge of new COVID-19 cases in several U.S. states say it should be a warning to Canada , but not a cause for alarm . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Several U.S. states have seen huge spikes in COVID-19 cases in the past weeks . On Friday , Arizona reported 3,217 new cases of the disease and Florida saw 4,049 . In California , there were 4,351 cases , Texas saw 4,189 and Georgia saw 1,800 new cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; All of these states have seen numbers higher than they have seen at any point in the pandemic , which is coming weeks after they allowed restaurants , businesses and even theatres to open up again . In many cases those businesses opened without the same distance requirements that are common in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Theresa Tam , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s chief public health officer , said they are following the situation south of the border closely and it should serve as a cautionary tale . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We do see all of these kinds of trends as a warning signal for us again , reminding Canadians the virus is still around , very much around in many parts of the world including the U.S , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She said failing to maintain high standards around hand-washing and social distancing could quickly cause a surge in new infections . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need to keep up and the moment we let go this virus could find some way of accelerating in our community . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The provincial and federal governments came to an agreed set of guidelines for reopening that ensured cases were on a downward trend before a province reopened and that contact tracing and testing were available . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Georgia , one of the states now seeing a surge , was one of the first U.S. states to open its economy on April 24 . On that day , when businesses reopened in the state there were 608 new cases reported , a month ago it had risen to 946 and as of Friday there were 1,800 cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Florida , Arizona , California , and Texas all began their reopening efforts in early May and are now seeing daily case counts that are two or three times as high as at any point in the pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario and Quebec , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s worst hit provinces , have started their reopening plans , but they have done so regionally and gradually compared to the U.S. states . Toronto and Montreal , which were particularly hard hit , still have many restrictions in place . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ontario saw 175 new cases on Sunday , while Quebec saw just 92. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. Zain Chagla , a specialist in infectious diseases and an assistant professor at McMaster University , said everything he has seen from the U.S. indicates they simply opened up too fast . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;9536;TOOLONG ... class= " alignleft size-full wp-image-78575512 " src= " https : **33;9571;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 1001 " height= " 583 " srcset= " https : **31;9606;TOOLONG ... 1001w , https : **39;9639;TOOLONG ... 150w , https : **39;9680;TOOLONG ... 300w , https : **39;9721;TOOLONG ... 768w " sizes= " ( max-width : 1001px ) 100vw , 1001px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We saw a preview of what not to do in the United States is not just wait till you get a peak , let the cases drop a little bit and then open the door . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said there was clearly still community transmission in these states when the decision was made to reopen and the virus has been able to rapidly spread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Moving slower is probably a whole lot better than moving faster , as we&amp;rsquo ; re seeing in the United States . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chagla said Canadian provinces have moved from complete lockdowns to gradual reopenings , which has given them the opportunity to address new outbreaks and clusters before they spread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re opening the door much later into our downward curve . " " data-portal-copyright= " Getty Images " **28;10432;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573914 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; " What 's keeping you awake at night ? " That 's the question we put to doctors , scientists , philosophers , psychologists , futurists , microbiologists and bioethicists , 100 days after the World Health Organization officially declared COVID-19 a global pandemic . We asked the experts what they 're anticipating for the next 100 days , what we should do differently , what worries them most . " Things have changed at such manic speed in the past three months , it 's difficult to predict what the next 100 days will yield , " said McGill University 's Samuel Veissi ? re . Below , Veissi ? re and others reflect on the challenges ahead . Their responses have been edited for clarity and length . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On compliance fatigue , social isolation and the politics of a pandemic : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m worried about future waves being imminent , maybe not even waves , but just this steady , persistent ( viral activity ) . &amp;nbsp ; How do we sustain what we&amp;rsquo ; re doing ? It&amp;rsquo ; s so important for mental health to have livelihoods . To continue to function . I don&amp;rsquo ; t know what the answer is . That kind of keeps me awake . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em ... Samira Mubareka , virologist and infectious diseases doctor at Sunnybrook Health Sciences Centre in Toronto&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As summer arrives , lockdown-weary people will increasingly flock to parks , beaches . Protest rallies and , south of the border , political rallies will bring people together in large numbers . This could prolong the current wave of infection or even spur a second wave . Paradoxically , efforts to combat one social problem ( protest rallies to combat racism ) could exacerbate another social problem ( the spread of COVID-19 ) &amp;hellip ; . Unlike the 1918 flu pandemic , in which the sight of coffins was an everyday occurrence , COVID-19 is largely a hidden pandemic , where the sick and dying are sequestered in hospitals , and coffins are nowhere to be seen . This can lead people to underestimate the seriousness of the current pandemic. &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Dr . Steven Taylor , professor and clinical psychologist at the University of British Columbia&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Politics and social polarization is what worries me the most . Before the pandemic some of us had predicted that 2020 would be the year of reason and reconciliation in the culture wars . We couldn&amp;rsquo ; t have been more wrong . For a few weeks , as the world ground to a halt , trying to make sense of the new threat posed by the virus , people everywhere appeared to be uniting in ways never seen before . This sadly didn&amp;rsquo ; t last . As everything from epidemiology , mathematical modelling , mask wearing , treatment options and whether or not to lock down became politicized , the already-polarized left-right divide took a sharper turn toward irreconcilable divisions . The recent wave of protests condemning racism and police brutality gave millions of young people worldwide a much-needed sense of purpose and justice after months of terrifying isolation , but it also reinforced the tribalization of politics that had been underway since the Trump election . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Samuel Veissi ? re , co-director , Culture , Mind and Brain Program at McGill University&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10462;TOOLONG ... just passed 100,000 COVID-19 cases . 8,257 of them have been **26;10497;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10525;TOOLONG ... Gurney : 100 days in , the future is still hard to **28;10560;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;10590;TOOLONG ... pandemic has boosted some premiers ' approval ratings 30 percent , Legault benefitting most : **25;10625;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &g; ... &lt;strong&gt;On bringing patients back : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I worry about all the triaging of non-COVID activity that we&amp;rsquo ; ve done , shutting down all sorts of cardiac and cancer and transplant care in order to potentially look after COVID patients , and the adverse effects patients have suffered because of that . I worry about this balance of trying to ramp up and catch up with all the non-emergency activity that we shut down , with the ongoing burden of COVID disease in our ICU . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Dr . Niall Ferguson , head of critical care at Toronto 's University Health Network&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On a dreaded second wave : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I worry about big , rebounding second waves if all these physical distancing things are taken less seriously , or&amp;nbsp ; when kids go back to daycare or school . Nobody knows how important a vector they might be . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; **29;10652;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I look at the next 100 days with optimism tinged with anxiety . What keeps me up at night is that , as the memory of the catastrophic events in Spain , Italy and N.Y.C. fades , people will take this disease lightly . We are seeing resurgences of the virus in places like Beijing and some U.S. states , and Canada is not immune from that . But until we have a vaccine we have to be vigilant as a population . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Dr . Wael Haddara , chair/chief of critical care medicine at London Health Sciences Centre&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;10683;TOOLONG ... class= " alignnone size-large wp-image-78573911 " src= " https : **33;10718;TOOLONG ... alt= " " width= " 640 " height= " 554 " srcset= " https : **31;10753;TOOLONG ... 640w , https : **39;10786;TOOLONG ... 150w , https : **39;10827;TOOLONG ... 300w , https : **39;10868;TOOLONG ... 768w , https : **39;10909;TOOLONG ... 1000w " sizes= " ( max-width : 640px ) 100vw , 640px " &gt;&lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On long-term care homes : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m looking ahead to how institutional custodial care will change for the better . But that won&amp;rsquo ; t happen in the next 100 days . What is happening is reintroducing family visits in Ontario&amp;rsquo ; s LTC&amp;rsquo ; s after 100 days of isolation . This hard lockdown on already isolated people has had a devastating impact &amp;mdash ; even in homes where basic care needs were still provided adequately . Many residents have declined significantly . New family visits will require face masks and keeping a two-metre distance . Many elderly residents can not see their family at two metres away , can not hear what they are saying through a mask , and often have dementia , making the strange meetings very distressing . There is evidence of successful closer encounters with screening and testing in place coming from the Netherlands . I hope our government will explore kinder options . Our elderly have been through a lot and deserve the comfort of family . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Maya Goldenberg , associate professor of philosophy at the University of Guelph&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On the anxiety of the unknown : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I can tell you exactly what keeps me up at night : Neurology resident Dr. Chris Freehan and I have been talking and writing about &amp;lsquo ; pre-traumatic&amp;rsquo ; stress disorder , the stress of waiting for the next surge in the pandemic , preparing for the unknown , anticipating the morally challenging decisions that will accompany a resurgence , where it will be , why , how do we keep frontline healthcare workers mentally healthy when they are already fragile &amp;hellip ; how do we keep growing individual and societal anxiety under control , while tensions and frustrations about the continued need to distance ourselves from loved ones and others continue . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Judy Illes , professor of neurology and Canada Research Chair in Neuroethics at UBC&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On the likelihood of reverse zoonosis : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The other thing that keeps me awake now is , humans are the most abundant species on the planet . This virus is circulating broadly amongst us . The likelihood of a reverse zoonosis , or spill over back into wildlife is a concern . ( In June , Dutch mink farms began a government-ordered cull over concerns minks infected with coronavirus could transmit it to humans . ) If the virus establishes itself in a wildlife reservoir , it can change enough that if it spills back over into humans , we can have resurgent disease . We&amp;rsquo ; re not really doing much in the way of surveillance of wildlife or protection of wildlife to make sure that doesn&amp;rsquo ; t happen . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;em&gt;Mubareka&lt;/ ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On the hunt for a vaccine : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; My biggest concern relates to our collective ability in the coming months to a year to determine what is the most effective vaccine , how will we be able to produce sufficient quantities for Canada and across the world , and how we can push corporate and government interests aside for collaboration and cooperation for testing and production&amp;hellip ; . As the numbers of cases decrease around most of the high income countries , we will need to turn for support to the lower income countries to test these vaccines in a safe and ethical manner for efficacy , and ensure that the vaccine will then be distributed across the globe&amp;hellip ; . We can not give up this time like we have with SARS and MERS in the past . This virus will be with us for a long time . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;Eric Arts , professor of microbiology and immunology at Western University in London&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There is a growing anti-vaccination sentiment in Canada and the U.S. Our most recent research indicates 20 per cent of Canadians , and 25 per cent of Americans would not get vaccinated . If those numbers continue to grow , that will seriously undermine the effects of vaccination for battling the coronavirus. &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; &lt;em&gt;Taylor&lt;/em ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;On what we need to do now : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; m not a public health doctor , but I&amp;rsquo ; m certainly supportive of everyone wearing a mask when they&amp;rsquo ; re out in public , especially in a closed indoor environment . Universal masking would be very helpful . Ventilators and drugs and PPE , and lack therefore , are other things that keep me up at night . Hopefully we&amp;rsquo ; ll have all those things somewhat better sorted out if things get worse again in the coming months . The burn rate through ( PPE ) is constant . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; **29;10950;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The next 100 days will bring us up to the start of the school year . While ( Ontario Education Minister Stephen ) Lecce has signalled that Ontario kids will be back in the classroom in September , school boards have not been given direction on how to prepare , teachers have not been consulted . Lecce has promised for a late June update , but this is not giving school boards enough time to plan for challenges . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;nbsp ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt; Goldenberg&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;10981;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;11017;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What we will or should do differently is start waking up to the fact that we are all connected to the point that our individual and collective fate &amp;mdash ; pandemics , global warming , nuclear weapons , artificial intelligence &amp;mdash ; is inseparable from that of those living on the other end of the world . And this is where we can find an opportunity to start developing what the Dalai Lama calls &amp;lsquo ; a sense of oneness . &amp;rsquo ; Because the major 21st century problems can not be solved within borders with the traditional me vs. you , or us vs. them mindset , but require a unified global approach that can come only after we stop seeing ourselves as different . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;mdash ; &lt;em&gt;futurist Nikola Danaylov&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s difficult to predict what the next 100 days will yield . PoliticsNewsGovernment COVID-19 emergency measures are ' death of civil liberties by a thousand cuts ' : reporthttp : **34;11540;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;11576;TOOLONG ... title= " Park-goers at Trinity-Bellwoods Park in Toronto , after the city painted it with more than a hundred social distancing circles . " alt= " Park-goers at Trinity-Bellwoods Park in Toronto , after the city painted it with more than a hundred social distancing circles . " data-portal-copyright= " Jack Boland/Postmedia/File " **28;11611;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573829 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;mdash ; The many emergency laws and regulations introduced to target the COVID-19 pandemic have amounted to the &amp;ldquo ; death of civil liberties by a thousand cuts , &amp;rdquo ; a new report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The non-profit Canadian Civil Liberties Association ( CCLA ) on Friday released a lengthy document that reviews emergency measures introduced by federal , provincial and municipal levels of government in recent months , which authorized everything from unlimited spending powers to hefty fines for people who fail to practise social distancing . The organization asserts that those measures amount to a broad overreach of government powers that , left unchecked , threatens to become permanent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The pandemic has led to a thousand impositions on civil liberties that might feel minor alone , but which taken together represent an extraordinary change to civil liberties in Canada , &amp;rdquo ; the report said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Limits to civil liberties include government-led initiatives to curb &amp;ldquo ; misinformation , &amp;rdquo ; mandatory masks or temperature checks in private settings , restricted access to public settings , or even attempts to hinder &amp;ldquo ; drive-in religious services , &amp;rdquo ; the report said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Each of these constrain freedoms in what some might consider small ways . But taken together , they indicate a major shift in Canada towards a more designated , circumscribed , and government-ordered way of doing things . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study underscores the deep divisions that have surfaced in recent months , as the public grapples with the extent to which government should control public life in the name of safety . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hefty fines issued by municipal police to people walking their dogs , for example , has been called an unnecessary overreach by some , while others argue that those fines might be seen as part of a broader need for compliance in order to curb the spread of the virus . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;11641;TOOLONG ... just passed 100,000 COVID-19 cases . 8,257 of those have been **26;11676;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;11704;TOOLONG ... contact tracing app ready to be tested in Ontario : **28;11739;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... The CCLA report does not outright dismiss the need for emergency measures to combat COVID-19 , but said the inconsistent and sometimes illogical nature of those policies likely reduced their effectiveness , while also restricting civil liberties . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Bill 10 , introduced by Alberta Premier Jason Kenney in response to the pandemic , &amp;ldquo ; allows a single minister to exercise the awesome lawmaking power of a parliamentary majority for up to 6 months after the end of the emergency , &amp;rdquo ; for example , while Ontario and Quebec &amp;ldquo ; wove a dragnet of social distancing rules&amp;rdquo ; enforced through punitive fines. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But the effectiveness and necessity of those sweeping powers remains unclear , the report said , while blatant policy shortfalls caused a high rate of deaths in seniors&amp;rsquo ; homes and in some cases a failure to provide safe haven for vulnerable populations . The city of Toronto , the report said , &amp;ldquo ; dragged its feet on ensuring that there were adequate and safe housing solutions for people experiencing homelessness. &amp;rdquo ; Homeless people were &amp;rdquo ; always an afterthought for leadership , at best , &amp;rdquo ; the CCLA said , as were minority populations like Indigenous . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The CCLA rated a number of measures introduced by the various orders of government , giving an &amp;ldquo ; F&amp;rdquo ; grade to Alberta&amp;rsquo ; s B-10 for being &amp;ldquo ; radically **26;11769;TOOLONG ; to the threat of the pandemic . Nunavut&amp;rsquo ; s initial public restrictions received a &amp;ldquo ; D&amp;rdquo ; but was later raised to a &amp;ldquo ; B+&amp;rdquo ; after the territorial government scaled back some of those limits . A decision by the Newfoundland government to close its border to non-residents received an &amp;ldquo ; F&amp;rdquo ; for its direct infringement on rights to movement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The study&amp;rsquo ; s conclusion calls on governments to lift emergency restrictions as soon as possible . It also argues that the public must begin to contemplate the way in which civil liberties improve public life overall , even if it comes at the expense of convenience or health in some cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We can not pretend anymore that there is some elusive balance between civil liberties and public health to be found , &amp;rdquo ; it said . &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; Our civil liberties inform our public health , as much as antibody levels and body temperature . That China may enjoy a lower COVID infection rate today does not make it a society healthier than Canada , whose residents are legally entitled to enjoy freedoms unknown to that state . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;11797;TOOLONG " **35;11827;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **32;11864;TOOLONG ... class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;jessesnyder ... &lt;/p&gt; Jesse SnyderSat , 20 Jun 2020 00:17:41 **45;11898;TOOLONG ... PoliticsNewsEspionage charges against two Canadians proof soft diplomacy with China is n't working : expertshttp : **33;11945;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;11980;TOOLONG ... title= " Canadians Michael Kovrig and Michael Spavor will be charged with espionage , China has announced . " data-portal-copyright= " Photo illustration " **28;12015;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573770 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;ndash ; Former diplomats say China&amp;rsquo ; s decision to charge two Canadians with espionage , after detaining them for more than 18 months , should be a wake-up call to the Trudeau government to stop the soft diplomacy and play hardball. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chinese prosecutors announced Friday that Canadians Michael Kovrig and Michael Spavor would be charged with espionage . Their arrests in 2018 came shortly after Canadian officials detained Huawei executive , Meng Wanzhou in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau said the arrests and charges remain arbitrary and without merit and he is disappointed China continues to detain the two men . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have continued to express our disappointment with the Chinese decision , with the Chinese detention of these two Canadians . We will continue to advocate for their release , for their return to Canada , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson Zhao Lijian said each is charged with &amp;ldquo ; secretly gathering state secrets for overseas forces with particularly serious consequences . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s former ambassador to China , Guy Saint-Jacques , said the soft diplomacy strategy the Trudeau government has taken has failed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The federal government has to come to the conclusion that the appeasement strategy that has been followed so far , has produced no result . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saint-Jacques said the government should consider trade sanctions and being more aggressive with Chinese interference in Canada . It could also finally announce Huawei won&amp;rsquo ; t have access to Canada&amp;rsquo ; s 5G telecom network . Canada should also advocate other countries&amp;nbsp ; adopt such measures to leverage the pressure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s the kind of behaviour we have to prevent going into the future . We are a small player for China , but I think we can exact a price especially when we&amp;rsquo ; re working with others . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kovrig and Spavor have been held with very limited consular contacts in a Chinese prison since their arrest in late 2018 . Chinese Ambassador to Canada Cong Peiwu said in an interview with Reuters last week that consular services for Kovrig and Spavor had been suspended due to coronavirus-control measures earlier this year . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meng by contrast has been on a form of house arrest since her detention began , allowed to move between two Vancouver mansions while wearing an ankle bracelet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;12045;TOOLONG ... title= " Huawei Chief Financial Officer , Meng Wanzhou , leaves B.C.
