" data-portal-copyright= " Don MacKinnon/AFP via Getty Images " **28;12080;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78545470 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Meng recently lost a court decision in her extradition case . She faces fraud charges in the United States and was arrested at the American&amp;rsquo ; s request while transiting through the Vancouver airport . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Saint-Jacques said the recent loss in Canadian courts was likely the catalyst for the criminal charges against Kovrig and Spavor. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Chinese must come to the conclusion that the extraction process will drag on for years and they decided to up the ante by launching the formal trial , &amp;rdquo ; said Saint-Jacques. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Robert Malley , president of the International Crisis Group , the conflict resolution organization Kovrig works for , said the charges against him were &amp;ldquo ; completely baseless&amp;rdquo ; and &amp;ldquo ; groundless. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said that Kovrig was &amp;ldquo ; open and transparent&amp;rdquo ; in his work , and Chinese officials have not provided any evidence to suggest otherwise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s deeply disappointing , but it&amp;rsquo ; s wholly unsurprising , &amp;rdquo ; Malley said . &amp;ldquo ; From the very beginning , this has been a political case , not a legal one . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;12110;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;12146;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Trudeau was asked if he viewed the two men as hostages , which he did not answer . He also didn&amp;rsquo ; t outline any more aggressive measures to garner their release . He said a lot was being done behind the scenes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are using a wide range of public and private measures to ensure that everything is being done to get the Michaels home . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said Chinese officials clearly believe the Meng case is politically motivated , but his government has been clear that the judicial system in Canada is independent . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Chinese authorities have directly linked the case of the two Michaels to the case of Ms. Meng , which is extremely disappointing because for us it is not , there are no links . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Earlier this week , Zhao , the Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesman , denounced the legal proceedings against Meng as an &amp;ldquo ; outright political case with grave consequences , &amp;rdquo ; adding that Canada had been acting as a U.S. &amp;ldquo ; accomplice&amp;rdquo ; to oppress Chinese technology companies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked Friday for the Chinese government&amp;rsquo ; s position on &amp;ldquo ; hostage diplomacy , &amp;rdquo ; Zhao rejected the question as &amp;ldquo ; vicious . &amp;rdquo ; &amp;ldquo ; Ask the Canadian government what &amp;lsquo ; hostage diplomacy&amp;rsquo ; is , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Charles Burton , an associate professor at Brock University and former counsellor at the Canadian Embassy in China , said Kovrig and Spavor should not expect a fair trial . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The case has already been developed , it&amp;rsquo ; s very likely that they will be found guilty of these serious charges , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; The trial itself , because it impinges on national security matters , would probably only amount to half a day . And we wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t have any access to the proceedings . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What China calls the People&amp;rsquo ; s Procuratorate investigated and will prosecute the case , which Burton said effectively means the government will decide the men&amp;rsquo ; s guilt and their sentence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They don&amp;rsquo ; t have the principle of judicial independence . And therefore these decisions are made by the Chinese Communist Party . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Burton said the government approach has been to not antagonize China , but that clearly isn&amp;rsquo ; t delivering . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Our policy has been to make concessions to the Chinese regime , in hope that it puts them favourably disposed to listen to our increasingly desperate pleas for release of Kovrig and Spavor from the hell that they&amp;rsquo ; ve been experiencing now for 557 days . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He said taking a tougher stance won&amp;rsquo ; t necessarily mean retaliation from China , especially if the government builds up a coalition of countries prepared to take a unified stance . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If there&amp;rsquo ; s political will to really stand up and make a strong indication to China that violations of the international rules based order , through their hostage diplomacy tactics are simply unacceptable to Canada , then that might lead to China rethinking how they&amp;rsquo ; re approaching Canada . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from Reuters and Washington Post&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **29;12182;TOOLONG " **36;12213;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a class= " twitter-follow-button " href= " https : **25;12251;TOOLONG " &gt; **32;12278;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Ryan TumiltyFri , 19 Jun 2020 23:41:48 **45;12312;TOOLONG ... PoliticsNewsWorldDozens of puppies found dead in cargo hold of Ukraine plane that landed in Torontohttp : **33;12359;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;12394;TOOLONG ... title= " Planes of the Ukrainian International airlines at the Kyiv Boryspil International Airport in Ukraine . " alt= " Planes of the Ukrainian International airlines at the Kyiv Boryspil International Airport in Ukraine . " data-portal-copyright= " Valentyn Ogirenko/Reuters/File " **28;12429;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573685 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;By Corn ? Van Hoepen&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A grisly scene unfolded Saturday as a Ukraine International Airlines flight cargo hold was being emptied in Toronto ; 38 puppies dead from apparent suffocation. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ukraine Airlines flight PS 1241 landed at Pearson International Airport on June 13 , carrying 500 puppies in the cargo hold that were meant to be sold in the Toronto area , according to a statement by the &lt;a href= " https : **33;12459;TOOLONG ... Food Inspection Agency&lt;/a&gt; ( CFIA ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A source recalls seeing the puppies in Ukraine . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There were around 500 total , 200 French bulldogs included , that were taken for transportation on the &lt;a href= " https : //kbp.aero/en/ " &gt;Borispol&lt;/a ... flight , including 300 cages , which meant some cages contained two dogs , &amp;rdquo ; the source said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The temperatures at the time of loading was over 30 C , and the puppies were visibly suffering according to eyewitness accounts , with multiple crates being placed on top of each other , and some even being encased in plastic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;12494;TOOLONG ... title= " Screen grab from a video taken by a freight worker at Kyiv Boryspil International Airport showing crates containing live animals stacked high awaiting to be loaded in the airplane. " alt= " Screen grab from a video taken by a freight worker at Kyiv Boryspil International Airport showing crates containing live animals stacked high awaiting to be loaded in the airplane. " data-portal-copyright= " Supplied " **28;12529;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573743 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; A handler loading the aircraft in Ukraine said that many of the dogs were put into cages without the ability to even move , and without air circulation . The pallets were then transported into the lower compartment of the aircraft , where air circulates very poorly . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; A video shared by a baggage handler at the time of loading shows carts with a large pile of crates containing live animals stacked high . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This would be in direct violation of the International Air Transport Association guidelines which state that each animal should have enough space to turn around while standing , to sit and lie in a natural position . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Animals which share the same household may become stressed and aggressive towards each other when traveling by air , &amp;rdquo ; the guideline reads . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Toronto resident Abby Lorenzen , who went to the airport that day to pick up a friend&amp;rsquo ; s cat , was told at customs that there would be a significant delay due to the large amount of pets on board the flight . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You could tell on their faces that this was a situation that did not happen very often , &amp;rdquo ; Lorenzen said . CFIA officers also refused Lorenzen access to the warehouse , telling her &amp;ldquo ; we will bring your pet out . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lorenzen , who has picked up pets from the cargo company says this is the first time she was denied access to the warehouse . &amp;ldquo ; Usually , you just walk right in , and pick up your pet , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She received the cat , which appeared to be in good health , and headed home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lorenzen had to be back at the cargo warehouse at early Monday morning for the cat&amp;rsquo ; s final flight to the west coast . This time , she was allowed to enter the warehouse . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; As soon as I entered the warehouse , you could smell death . It was just very eerie , &amp;rdquo ; said Lorenzen. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After checking the cat in , Lorenzen happened to pass a garbage can , and the bodies of several puppies were clearly visible . Shocked , her first instinct was to snap a photo for evidence . She approached an officer with what she saw , and his response was that they had been instructed to dispose of the bodies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; April Byers of Hanover , Ont. , called the CFIA vet line Sunday , a part of the process in finalizing paperwork to pick up a live animal from the cargo centre . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Byers said that that the vet acknowledged that they were handling a situation and told her &amp;ldquo ; Some idiot on the other end shrink wrapped several crates containing animals causing them to suffocate . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; CFIA officials have launched an investigation and will determine further steps once completed . The organization is responsible for regulating the importation of animals , including dogs into Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a statement that was emailed , the CFIA states they have rigorous standards for animal imports and wrote &amp;ldquo ; Carriers can not load , transport or unload animals in a way that would cause injury or undue suffering . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ukraine International Airlines has yet to comment , however , an email by the airline was sent to freight forwarding agents read &amp;ldquo ; Be informed that airline UIA , starting from 19.06 , 2020 , have temporary embargo for live animals on aircrafts B767 until further notification . &amp;rdquo ; This is the same aircraft type that landed in Toronto . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The airlines has also put out &lt;a href= " https : **33;12559;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;a statement&lt;/a&gt; on their Facebook page on Friday offering their condolences for the tragic loss of life on their flight . &amp;ldquo ; UIA is working with local authorities to determine what happened , and make any changes necessary to prevent such a situation from occurring again , &amp;rdquo ; the post reads . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;12594;TOOLONG ... title= " Statement by the Canadian Food Inspection Agency on the incident . " alt= " Statement by the Canadian Food Inspection Agency on the incident . " data-portal-copyright= " CFIA " **28;12629;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573682 " /&gt; National PostFri , 19 Jun 2020 23:04:16 **45;12659;TOOLONG ... are Michael Kovrig and Michael Spavor , the two Canadians held by China ? http : **34;12706;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;12742;TOOLONG ... title= " Michael Kovrig ( left ) and Michael Spavor , the two Canadians detained in China , are shown in these 2018 images taken from video . " alt= " Michael Kovrig ( left ) and Michael Spavor , the two Canadians detained in China , are shown in these 2018 images taken from video . " data-portal-copyright= " THE CANADIAN PRESS/AP " **28;12777;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573338 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; Chinese prosecutors said Friday they would formally bring espionage charges against two Canadians , Michael Spavor and Michael Kovrig , in cases widely seen as retribution for Canada&amp;rsquo ; s role in helping U.S. law enforcement to pursue senior Huawei executive Meng Wanzhou. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The announcement by the Supreme People&amp;rsquo ; s Procuratorate almost certainly foreshadows lengthy prison sentences for Spavor , previously a businessman based on the China-North Korea border , and Kovrig , a former diplomat who worked as a researcher in Beijing for the International Crisis Group . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The two Canadians , who Prime Minister Justin Trudeau says have been &amp;ldquo ; arbitrarily&amp;rdquo ; detained by the Chinese , had been enjoying success in very different fields when apprehended . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At the time of his arrest , Kovrig worked full-time for the International Crisis Group in North East Asia . Working from Hong Kong , he was tasked with defusing tensions between China and nearby states , and with giving a fresh , independent appraisal of China&amp;rsquo ; s growing role in the world , according the the organization&amp;rsquo ; s website. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before his arrest , he reported on China&amp;rsquo ; s role in global affairs , such as the country&amp;rsquo ; s growing presence in Africa and its relationship with North Korea , Global News has &lt;a href= " https : **33;12807;TOOLONG ... On social media , he would frequently comment on China&amp;rsquo ; s foreign policy and on Huawei , the Chinese telecoms giant caught up in the case against Meng. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He had previously worked for Global Affairs Canada in a China-facing role , stationed between Beijing and Hong Kong from 2012 to 2016 . In 2016 , he worked as the political lead for Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s visit to Hong Kong . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kovrig was the first of the so-called &amp;ldquo ; two Michaels&amp;rdquo ; to be arbitrarily detained by Chinese authorities . Despite his former role , he had no diplomatic immunity at the time of his arrest because he was on leave without pay . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;12842;TOOLONG ... is Michael Spavor , the second Canadian to go missing in China ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;12877;TOOLONG ... charges detained Canadians Kovrig and Spavor with suspected **30;12912;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt; ... href= " https : **33;12944;TOOLONG ... visitors for Michael Kovrig , Michael Spavor due to COVID-19 as 500th day in Chinese prison **26;12979;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... Spavor , 44 , is a businessman with deep ties to North Korea . He is the founder of Paektu Cultural Exchange , which is focused on opening up international ties with the insular country . The website for the organization says it is &amp;ldquo ; a non-profit social enterprise&amp;rdquo ; dedicated to cross-cultural exchanges , trade , and investment between North Korea and &amp;ldquo ; international organizations , businesses , and individuals to promote greater peace , friendship , and understanding . &amp;rdquo ; Based in China and Canada , Paektu promotes travel to North Korea , including a five-day trip to Pyongyang for 2019 New Year celebrations , with visits to hot springs and the de-militarized zone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;blockquote class= " instagram-media " Michael Spavor/Handout via REUTERS 3 / 11 https : **39;13258;TOOLONG ... #longislandicetea #cocktails #reuters #yachtparty #yatch #kimjongun #dprk #wonsan #smoking #paektuexchanges http : **40;13299;TOOLONG ... &lt;p style= " color : #c9c8cd ; font-family:Arial , sans-serif ; font-size:14px ; line-height:17px ; margin-bottom:0 ; margin-top:8px ; overflow:hidden ; padding:8px 0 7px ; text-align:center ; text-overflow:ellipsis ; white-space:nowrap ; " &gt;A post shared by &lt;a href= " https : **33;13341;TOOLONG ... style= " color : #c9c8cd ; font-family:Arial , sans-serif ; font-size:14px ; font-style:normal ; font-weight:normal ; line-height:17px ; " target= " blank " &gt; Michael Spavor&lt;/a&gt; ( @paektuexchanges ) on &lt;time style= " font-family:Arial , sans-serif ; font-size:14px ; line-height:17px ; " datetime= " **25;13376;TOOLONG " &gt;Oct 10 , 2017 at 11:25am **45;13403;TOOLONG ... async src= " **28;13450;TOOLONG " &gt;&lt;/sc ... Spavor has been photographed several times with Kim Jong Un and is one of only a handful of Westerners to ever meet the leader . In one Instagram post , Spavor is seen with Kim in a 2013 photo , sharing Long Island Iced Teas on the North Korean dictator&amp;rsquo ; s private yacht in Wonsan , North Korea . Other photos show him with both Kim Jong Un and NBA player Dennis Rodman . The Associated Press reports Spavor was a key player in bringing Rodman to North Korea in 2013 . Spavor is one of the few Westerners to travel extensively in North Korea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As an organization promoting closer ties to North Korea , Paektu Cultural Exchange has come under fire , given the Kim regime&amp;rsquo ; s human rights abuses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neither the Chinese nor Canadian governments have provided great detail about the conditions under which the two Michaels are being kept . Each has been allowed regular consular visits about once a month , but so far , neither man has been allowed contact with their family , or access to a lawyer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;13480;TOOLONG ... title= " In this file photo taken on March 6 , 2019 , Louis Huang of Vancouver Freedom and Democracy for China holds photos of Canadians Michael Spavor and Michael Kovrig , who are being detained by China , outside British Columbia Supreme Court , in Vancouver . " alt= " In this file photo taken on March 6 , 2019 , Louis Huang of Vancouver Freedom and Democracy for China holds photos of Canadians Michael Spavor and Michael Kovrig , who are being detained by China , outside British Columbia Supreme Court , in Vancouver . " data-portal-copyright= " ASON REDMOND/AFP via Getty Images " **28;13515;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573401 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; It came to light at the end of 2018 that the two had been kept in cells with the lights on all day and all night ; both were continuously subjected to interrogation at least three times a day . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Spavor and Kovrig charged : A timeline of the Meng Wanzhou case and rising tension between Canada and China : &lt;/strong&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;2018&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aug. 22 : A New York court issues a warrant for the arrest of Huawei Technologies chief financial officer Meng Wanzhou. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 1 : Canadian authorities arrest Meng at Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s airport after an extradition request from the Americans . The news becomes public on Dec. 5. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 6 : China demands Canada release Meng and &amp;ldquo ; immediately correct the mistake&amp;rdquo ; officials made in arresting her . The Chinese say they were not briefed on the reasons for Meng&amp;rsquo ; s arrest . In Ottawa , Prime Minister Justin Trudeau says Meng&amp;rsquo ; s case is part of an independent legal process with no outside political influence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 7 : Meng appears in a Vancouver court , where allegations of fraud are laid out . The U.S. alleges Meng misled American banks in a bid to get around American sanctions on Iran . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 8 : Canada&amp;rsquo ; s ambassador to China , John McCallum , is summoned to a meeting with China&amp;rsquo ; s assistant foreign minister so the country can register complaints about Meng&amp;rsquo ; s arrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 9 : China summons the American ambassador to China to lodge similar complaints about Meng&amp;rsquo ; s case and demand the U.S. rescind the order for her arrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 10 : Chinese authorities arrest two Canadian men . Michael Kovrig , who was on leave from Global Affairs Canada , and entrepreneur Michael Spavor . Kovrig&amp;rsquo ; s arrest becomes public on Dec. 11 . Spavor&amp;rsquo ; s becomes public on Dec. 12. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 11 : Meng is released on $10 million bail . U.S. President Donald Trump tells Reuters that he would &amp;ldquo ; certainly intervene&amp;rdquo ; in Meng&amp;rsquo ; s case &amp;ldquo ; if I thought it was necessary&amp;rdquo ; to help forge a trade deal with China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 12 : China&amp;rsquo ; s foreign ministry says it has no information about Kovrig but says the organization he worked with &amp;mdash ; the International Crisis Group &amp;mdash ; was not registered in China , making its activities in the country illegal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 13 : Trump&amp;rsquo ; s trade adviser , Peter Navarro , says the arrests of the two Canadians were plainly a response to Meng&amp;rsquo ; s arrest . China&amp;rsquo ; s foreign ministry says Kovrig and Spavor have been detained on suspicion of &amp;ldquo ; endangering national security . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 14 : Canadian officials are granted consular access to Kovrig , and McCallum meets with him in Beijing . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 16 : Canadian diplomats in China are granted consular access to Spavor. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 20 : Indictments unsealed in the United States allege two Chinese citizens targeted companies in Canada and around the world as part of a years-long hacking campaign to steal data . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 21 : Kovrig&amp;rsquo ; s employer , the International Crisis Group , says he has not been given access to a lawyer while in custody . A source familiar with the conditions of Kovrig&amp;rsquo ; s detention says he is questioned three times a day and kept in a room with the lights on continuously . Foreign Affairs Minister Chrystia Freeland formally demands both men be let go , calling in a statement &amp;ldquo ; for their immediate release . &amp;rdquo ; Similar statements come out from the United States , Britain and the European Union . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 24 : China&amp;rsquo ; s foreign ministry calls out the U.S. , Britain and EU , saying the trio should be condemning Canada for Meng&amp;rsquo ; s arrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;2019&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 3 : At a news conference , China&amp;rsquo ; s prosecutor general Zhang Jun says Kovrig and Spavor &amp;ldquo ; without a doubt&amp;rdquo ; violated Chinese law . He says the investigation is also following the rule of law but doesn&amp;rsquo ; t provide more details about the allegations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 7 : The Prime Minister&amp;rsquo ; s Office says Trump has affirmed his respect for judicial independence . In a summary of a phone call between Trump and Trudeau , the PMO indicated the leaders discussed the high-profile U.S. extradition request &amp;mdash ; though Meng was not named &amp;mdash ; and agreed on the importance of respecting the independence of judges and the rule of law . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 9 : China&amp;rsquo ; s envoy in Ottawa suggests Canada and its Western allies are white supremacists for calling for the release of two Canadians imprisoned last month by his country&amp;rsquo ; s communist government . Ambassador Lu Shaye makes the accusation in an op-ed in the Hill Times . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 14 : Trudeau says he&amp;rsquo ; s very concerned to see China &amp;ldquo ; acting arbitrarily&amp;rdquo ; by applying the death penalty to a Canadian convicted of drug trafficking . He says Canada will do all it can to intervene on Robert Lloyd Schellenberg&amp;rsquo ; s behalf when a court in Dalian in northeastern Liaoning province announced it had given Schellenberg the death penalty after reconsidering his case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 15 : China expresses its &amp;ldquo ; strong **25;13545;TOOLONG ; with Trudeau over his criticism of Schellenberg&amp;rsquo ; s sentence . Trudeau should &amp;ldquo ; respect the rule of law , respect China&amp;rsquo ; s judicial sovereignty , correct mistakes and stop making irresponsible remarks , a foreign ministry spokeswoman Hua Chunying says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;13572;TOOLONG ... title= " Huawei chief financial officer Meng Wanzhou leaves her home to attend a court hearing in Vancouver on Wednesday October 2 , 2019. " alt= " Huawei chief financial officer Meng Wanzhou leaves her home to attend a court hearing in Vancouver on Wednesday October 2 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " THE CANADIAN PRESS/Darryl Dyck " **28;13607;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78358761 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 16 : The U.S. State Department says China&amp;rsquo ; s death sentence against Schellenberg is &amp;ldquo ; politically motivated . &amp;rdquo ; A statement says U.S. Secretary of State Mike Pompeo and Freeland spoke and &amp;ldquo ; expressed their concerns about the arbitrary detentions and politically motivated sentencing of Canadian nationals . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 17 : Ambassador Shaye says Canada&amp;rsquo ; s arrest of Meng was an act of &amp;ldquo ; backstabbing&amp;rdquo ; by a friend . Lu warns of &amp;ldquo ; repercussions&amp;rdquo ; if Canada bars the firm from its new 5G network for security reasons , as have three of its intelligence-sharing allies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 18 : Public Safety Minister Ralph Goodale says the government&amp;rsquo ; s decision on whether to ban Huawei from being used in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s next generation 5G wireless network will not be influenced by threats of retaliation from China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 22 : China demands the U.S. drop a request that Canada extradite Meng . Foreign Ministry spokeswoman Hua Chunying said Meng&amp;rsquo ; s case was out of the ordinary and Canada&amp;rsquo ; s extradition treaty with the U.S. infringed on the &amp;ldquo ; safety and legitimate rights and interests of Chinese citizens . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 23 : Ambassador McCallum says there are strong legal arguments Meng can make to help her avoid extradition to the United States . Speaking to Chinese reporters a day earlier in the Toronto area , McCallum listed several arguments Meng&amp;rsquo ; s legal team can make in her defence . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 24 : Trudeau dismisses calls to remove McCallum following his comments to Chinese reporters , saying such a change wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t help the two Canadians detained by Chinese authorities get home any sooner . Later , McCallum says he &amp;ldquo ; misspoke&amp;rdquo ; when he suggested detained Huawei executive Meng Wanzhou had a strong case to avoid extradition to the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 25 : McCallum tells StarMetro Vancouver it would be &amp;ldquo ; great for Canada&amp;rdquo ; if the United States drops its extradition request . &amp;ldquo ; We have to make sure that if the U.S. does such a deal , it also includes the release of our two people . And the U.S. is highly aware of that , &amp;rdquo ; he told the Star . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 26 : McCallum resigns as ambassador to China at Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s request . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 28 : The U.S. Department of Justice formally levels criminal charges against Huawei , two subsidiaries and Meng . The charges , contained in two newly unsealed indictments , allege that Huawei misrepresented its ownership of a Hong Kong-based subsidiary to circumvent American sanctions against Iran . Furthermore , they say Huawei stole telecommunications technology , trade secrets and equipment from U.S. cellphone provider T-Mobile USA . Meng is charged with bank fraud , wire fraud and two counts of conspiracy to commit both . In a statement , Huawei denied committing any of the violations cited in the indictment . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 29 : The Canadian government says it has received a formal request for the extradition of Meng , who appears in a Vancouver court to change the people who are providing her with some of the financial sureties for her release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 1 : The Justice Department gives the formal go-ahead for the extradition case to proceed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 3 : Meng&amp;rsquo ; s defence team announces it has filed a notice of civil claim alleging &amp;ldquo ; serious violations&amp;rdquo ; of their client&amp;rsquo ; s constitutional rights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 4 : China accuses the two detained Canadians of acting together to steal state secrets . Trudeau dismissed the allegation made in Chinese state media , saying : &amp;ldquo ; It is unfortunate that China continues to move forward on these arbitrary detentions. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 6 : A lawyer for Meng tells a judge the United States bid for extradition raises serious concerns about the political motivations behind the case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 14 : Huawei pleads not guilty in a New York court to charges accusing it of plotting to violate Iran trade sanctions . Lawyers enter the plea in federal court in Brooklyn , two weeks after Huawei pleaded not guilty to separate federal charges filed in Seattle accusing the company of stealing technology from T-Mobile. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 22 : A judge orders the RCMP to provide copies of the content on seven electronic devices owned by Meng after they were seized when she was arrested . Justice Heather Holmes of the British Columbia Supreme Court says the RCMP must make copies of data on an iPhone , an iPad , a Macbook Air , a Huawei phone , two SIM cards and a flash drive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 29 : The federal government says it is considering subsidizing farmers hit by China&amp;rsquo ; s $2-billion ban on Canadian canola imports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; April 29 : Saskatchewan Premier Scott Moe says Ottawa needs to treat Chinese imports with the same scrutiny China is showing Canada&amp;rsquo ; s canola shipments . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;13637;TOOLONG ... title= " Premier Scott Moe. " alt= " Premier Scott Moe. " data-portal-copyright= " BRANDON HARDER " **28;13672;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78569935 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 1 : The federal government changes a payment program for canola farmers to help those affected by China&amp;rsquo ; s decision to ban the Canadian product . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 2 : China suspends the export permits of two Canadian pork exporters , including Quebec-based Olymel LP , amid growing tensions between the two countries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 8 : Meng&amp;rsquo ; s defence team tells a judge it plans to argue that she shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be extradited to the United States because she hasn&amp;rsquo ; t committed fraud under Canadian laws and her arrest was unlawful . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 9 : Canadian diplomats are joined in a Chinese courtroom by American , British , French and German colleagues to watch an appeal in Schellenberg&amp;rsquo ; s case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 16 : China says it has formally arrested Kovrig and Spavor , bringing them closer to trial on vaguely defined state security charges . A Chinese Foreign Ministry spokesman says they have been arrested for allegedly stealing state secrets . Goodale says the federal government is &amp;ldquo ; deeply concerned&amp;rdquo ; about China&amp;rsquo ; s decision , adding that no evidence has been produced to indicate any validity to allegations made against them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 23 : China&amp;rsquo ; s ambassador to Canada says the bilateral relationship is at &amp;ldquo ; rock bottom&amp;rdquo ; compared to any time since diplomatic ties were first established decades ago . In prepared text for a speech , Lu Shaye says he&amp;rsquo ; s saddened Canada-China relations are at what he called a &amp;ldquo ; freezing point . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; June 5 : Lawyers for the RCMP and Canada Border Services Agency deny allegations that their officers searched Huawei executive Meng Wanzhou&amp;rsquo ; s phones and electronic devices after a border official wrote down her passwords . A joint response filed in court to Meng&amp;rsquo ; s civil lawsuit says a border officer asked Meng for her phone numbers and passwords in case he was required to search the devices for customs or immigration purposes . Meng&amp;rsquo ; s civil lawsuit filed in B.C. Supreme Court alleges &amp;ldquo ; serious violations&amp;rdquo ; of her constitutional rights and accuses officers of detaining and questioning her for three hours before notifying her of her arrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; June 13 : Freeland rejects the purported view of former prime minister Jean Chretien that the extradition request be cancelled to improve relations with China and win the release of Kovrig and Spavor . Freeland said while she respects Chretien , Canada is either a rule of law country or it is not . Chretien&amp;rsquo ; s comments were reported in the Globe and Mail citing anonymous sources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; June 24 : Defence lawyers for Meng ask Ottawa to stop the extradition process against their client , saying the request made by the United States was for political purposes , not legitimate law enforcement reasons . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aug. 20 : Court documents released ahead of Meng&amp;rsquo ; s extradition hearing suggest a Canadian border official questioned her about her business before the RCMP arrested her . The nearly 1,100 pages of material released by the court were collected by Meng&amp;rsquo ; s defence team . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Aug. 21 : Meng&amp;rsquo ; s lawyers allege Canadian officials acted as &amp;ldquo ; agents&amp;rdquo ; of American law enforcement while she was detained at Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s airport for three hours ahead of her arrest . In court documents , they point to handwritten notes by Canadian officers indicating Meng&amp;rsquo ; s electronics were collected in anticipation of a request from the Federal Bureau of Investigation in the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sept. 4 . Business consultant Dominic Barton is named as Canada&amp;rsquo ; s new ambassador to China . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sept. 23 : The Crown says Canadian officials followed the law when they detained Meng and the defence has no proof to substantiate its &amp;ldquo ; conspiracy theory&amp;rdquo ; that she was illegally arrested . The Attorney General of Canada says in court documents that there&amp;rsquo ; s no evidence to suggest that the RCMP or the FBI asked border agents to elicit information from Meng during the detainment. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sept. 24 : A lawyer for Meng says there was nothing &amp;ldquo ; routine&amp;rdquo ; about the way she was questioned by border officials before she was read her rights and informed of her arrest as Meng&amp;rsquo ; s defence team asks the B.C. Supreme Court to compel the release of further documents to support its arguments ahead of her extradition hearing in January . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oct. 1 : A Crown prosecutor tells a judge Canadian border guards mistakenly gave the RCMP passcodes to electronic devices belonging to Meng when she was questioned at the Vancouver airport . The Crown says when the border agency realized it had made a mistake , it told the RCMP the codes could not be used or shared because they&amp;rsquo ; d been obtained during a border examination . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oct. 29 : The federal government says Canada&amp;rsquo ; s new ambassador to China has met with Michael Kovrig and Michael Spavor. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Oct. 31 : In documents , Meng&amp;rsquo ; s lawyers maintain there is an &amp;ldquo ; air of reality&amp;rdquo ; to an allegation the RCMP illegally shared details of her electronic devices with the FBI , despite new affidavits from the Mounties denying the claim . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nov. 5 . A Chinese ban on the import of Canadian pork and beef products estimated to have cost farmers almost $100 million is lifted . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nov. 24 : Canada&amp;rsquo ; s new foreign affairs minister spends an hour in talks with his Chinese counterpart over the fate of Kovrig and Spavor . Francois-Philippe Champagne spoke with China&amp;rsquo ; s Wang Yi a day earlier at a G20 meeting of foreign ministers in Japan . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nov. 28 : Lawyers for Meng say the United States is &amp;ldquo ; dressing up&amp;rdquo ; its complaint that she violated sanctions as a case of fraud as they ask the B.C. Supreme Court to decline her extradition . In court documents released , Meng&amp;rsquo ; s legal team says the alleged misrepresentation does not amount to fraud and the transactions processed by HSBC were not illegal in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 2 : Meng says she has experienced feelings of helplessness , torment and struggle since being arrested , but no longer fears the unknown . In a post on the Chinese telecom company&amp;rsquo ; s website , Meng says she has passed the time on bail in one of her homes in Vancouver reading books , chatting with colleagues and painting . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dec. 10 : Justice Minister David Lametti says he is troubled that Kovrig and Spavor have been denied access to lawyers as they face trials where convictions are virtually assured . Meanwhile , Meng wins an application in the B.C. Supreme Court for more documents to be disclosed as she alleges there was an abuse of process during her arrest . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;b&gt;2020&lt;/b&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Jan. 23 : A lawyer for Meng argues the extradition case against her is a test of whether courts will reject foreign charges that run contrary to Canadian values as a hearing wrapped up in the B.C. Supreme Court . The hearing focused on the legal test of double criminality . Crown counsel Robert Frater says the judge does not necessarily need to consider American sanctions law for the allegations to amount to fraud in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; March 13 : Amid the COVID-19 pandemic , the Chinese Embassy in Canada says Kovrig has been allowed to have a telephone conversation with his father , who is very ill . The embassy says in a statement that they allowed the call for humanitarian reasons , and it also says Kovrig and fellow detainee Michael Spavor are being given better food to strengthen their immunity against the novel coronavirus. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; May 27 : Holmes rules against Meng on the double-criminality argument . The B.C. Supreme Court judge says the allegations against Meng could constitute a crime in Canada . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; June 15 : Lawyers for Meng accuse authorities in the United States of misleading the B.C. Supreme Court judge presiding over the extradition case . Meng&amp;rsquo ; s defence team says it is seeking a stay in the proceedings arguing that U.S. case records are &amp;ldquo ; grossly inaccurate and based on deliberate and/or reckless misstatements of fact and material omissions , thereby constituting a serious abuse of the extradition process . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;13702;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;13738;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; June 19 : China charges Kovrig and Spavor with spying . Trudeau says he wo n't address new allegation of sexual harassment against former Liberal MP Marwan Tabbarahttp : **33;14678;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;14713;TOOLONG ... title= " MP Marwan Tabbara with Prime Minister Justin Trudeau . " data-portal-copyright= " Facebook " **28;14748;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573316 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t address new allegations that a Member of Parliament currently facing serious criminal charges was previously allowed to seek re-election as a Liberal despite a sexual harassment allegation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Marwan Tabbara , who represents the Ontario riding of Kitchener South-Hespeler , left the Liberal caucus this month after it was revealed he was charged in April with criminal harassment of a woman , break-and-enter , and assault against the same woman and a man . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Neither Tabbara nor the Guelph Police Service , who laid the charges , said anything about it at the time . Trudeau earlier said he did not know of the charges until the day of the media reports . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Friday , Trudeau did not deny new allegations reported by CBC that the Liberal party knew of an earlier allegation of sexual harassment made against Tabbara. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tabbara was approved to seek re-election as a Liberal in 2019 despite the party&amp;rsquo ; s internal investigation into allegations of inappropriate touching and unwelcome sexual comments against a female staff member during his initial 2015 election campaign , CBC reported , attributing the information to anonymous party sources . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;14778;TOOLONG ... Marwan Tabbara has brief court date as new allegation of sexual harassment is **25;14813;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;14840;TOOLONG ... Ontario MP Marwan Tabbara sat in jail over Easter , there was no backlash because nobody **25;14875;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;14902;TOOLONG ... MP allegedly watched home for three months before arrest there for assault , break and enter , **31;14937;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &; ... The Liberal probe substantiated some of the allegations but whether Tabbara faced any consequences is not known , according to the report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau said he wouldn&amp;rsquo ; t discuss specific cases to respect the confidentiality of the process . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Part of that process , and this is the part that is always frustrating for media and for many people watching , is that confidentiality is an integral part of that process to protect the people coming forward . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I will also need to respect the confidentiality of everyone involved in this case , and can not comment further . Other than to emphasize , as I always have , that we take every single case extremely seriously . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau said it is &amp;ldquo ; disappointing&amp;rdquo ; that a sitting MP can be criminally charged without anyone knowing . National Post reported last week that a Parliamentary rule on this is not being followed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In regards to the police proceedings , we&amp;rsquo ; ve all reflected on the fact that there is apparently a rule in the House of Commons standing orders that if a Member of Parliament is arrested , the Speaker or the House needs to be officially informed . That rule has apparently not been followed or applied much over the past years , &amp;rdquo ; Trudeau said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s perhaps a reflection that that is something that we can look into . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is certainly disappointing as a party leader that the individual in question never chose to inform the party in which he was a part of these charges , and I&amp;rsquo ; ll let the police and the prosecutors in this case speak for themselves . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When it comes to the police , at least , they are saying almost nothing . As for the prosecution , Tabarra had a brief hearing Friday morning prior to Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s remarks . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tabbara , 35 , was charged with two counts of assault , one count of break and enter and commit an indictable offence , and one count of criminal harassment on April 10. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;14970;TOOLONG ... title= " MP Marwan Tabbara. " alt= " MP Marwan Tabbara. " data-portal-copyright= " Facebook " **28;15005;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78573315 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tabbara&amp;rsquo ; s court date Friday in the Ontario Court of Justice in Guelph , the city where he was arrested near his riding , was held by teleconference because of COVID-19 restrictions . Tabbara was apparently not on the phone line , nor did he need a lawyer . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Tabbara&amp;rsquo ; s name and case file number were simply included with other criminally charged accused whose cases were postponed to Aug. 28 , in keeping with the court&amp;rsquo ; s handling of out-of-custody criminal matters during COVID-19 restrictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; According to a police document obtained by the Post , Tabbara is accused of watching a home in Guelph starting Jan. 1 and ending April 9 , in a manner that made the female occupant fear for her safety . That led to the criminal harassment charge . PoliticsNewsKovrig and Spavor : Trudeau says Canada trying public and private measures to secure freedomhttp : **33;16075;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;16110;TOOLONG ... title= " Louis Huang of Vancouver Freedom and Democracy for China holds photos of Canadians Michael Spavor and Michael Kovrig , who are being detained by China , outside British Columbia Supreme Court in Vancouver on March 6 , 2019. " alt= " Louis Huang of Vancouver Freedom and Democracy for China holds photos of Canadians Michael Spavor and Michael Kovrig , who are being detained by China , outside British Columbia Supreme Court in Vancouver on March 6 , 2019. " data-portal-copyright= " Jason Redmond/AFP via Getty Images " **28;16145;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78335889 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;mdash ; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau says the government is using public and private measures to secure the release of two Canadians detained in China for 18 months , who on Friday were formally charged with espionage in the politically charged case . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau said he was &amp;ldquo ; very disappointed&amp;rdquo ; with the charges Chinese prosecutors unveiled , while Deputy Prime Minister Chrystia Freeland described feeling personally angry at the news . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Former diplomat Michael Kovrig was charged in Beijing on suspicion of spying for state secrets and intelligence . Businessman Michael Spavor was charged in Dandong near the North Korean border on suspicion of spying for a foreign entity and illegally providing state secrets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Speaking at a press conference in Chelsea , Que. , just north of the national capital , Trudeau said the only reason the two are being detained is because of independent judicial proceedings involving Meng Wanzhou , a top executive at Chinese tech giant Huawei who is fighting an extradition request to the United States . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau said aside from public statements , there is &amp;ldquo ; action behind the scenes in very direct and firm ways&amp;rdquo ; to secure their release . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have developed a certain expertise in what has worked to get Canadians home in very difficult circumstances over the past years , &amp;rdquo ; Trudeau said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In the case of the two Michaels , I can say that we are using a wide range of public and private measures to ensure that everything is being done to get these Michaels home . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The two have been held since December 2018 , shortly after Canadian authorities arrested Meng at Vancouver&amp;rsquo ; s airport . U.S. authorities want her extradited over allegations she circumvented American sanction on Iran . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The daughter of Huawei&amp;rsquo ; s founder and the company itself deny any wrongdoing . She remains out on bail as hearings at the B.C. Supreme Court continue in her case . A judge rejected the first set of arguments from her lawyers late last month in a bid to set her free . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We will not , and must not , and can not interfere in the independence of our judiciary in this country , &amp;rdquo ; Trudeau said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The Chinese authorities have directly linked the case of the two Michaels to the judicial proceedings against Ms. Meng , which is extremely disappointing because for us &amp;hellip ; there obviously are no links except in politics . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Wrapped up in the case is Huawei&amp;rsquo ; s bid to be a player in Canada&amp;rsquo ; s 5G network , which was put in doubt after Bell and Telus announced partnerships with the Chinese company&amp;rsquo ; s European rivals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Liberals have yet to decide whether Huawei will have a role in building the network . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The United States has warned Canada and other allies that it will limit sharing intelligence with countries that have Huawei equipment in their 5G networks &amp;mdash ; citing its potential use for spying by China , an allegation Huawei denies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The charges against Spavor and Kovrig were announced Friday by China&amp;rsquo ; s highest prosecutor&amp;rsquo ; s office in brief social media posts . Chinese foreign ministry spokesperson Zhao Lijian said each is charged with &amp;ldquo ; secretly gathering state secrets for overseas forces with particularly serious consequences . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The facts are clear and the evidence solid and sufficient , &amp;rdquo ; Zhao told reporters at a daily briefing , without providing further details . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The International Crisis Group , where Kovrig worked at the time of his arrest , said the diplomat regularly interviewed Chinese officials to accurately reflect their views in his reports and had a constructive relationship with Chinese officials . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This is yet another arbitrary and baseless step in a case that has been arbitrary and baseless from day one , &amp;rdquo ; the group&amp;rsquo ; s chief executive , Robert Malley , said in a statement . &amp;ldquo ; Michael was not endangering China&amp;rsquo ; s security : everything he was doing was in the open and well known to China&amp;rsquo ; s authorities . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Kovrig and Spavor have had no access to lawyers or their families as of May , with the exception of a phone call the Chinese embassy said Kovrig was allowed to make to his sick father in mid-March . Consular visits have also been suspended because of the COVID-19 pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Speaking at a midday press conference on Parliament Hill , Freeland said it was essential to restore consular access to the two men . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They are a priority for Canadian foreign policy in general , they are a priority for our government in general , they are not forgotten and we are going to continue to work very , very hard for their release , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative Leader Andrew Scheer said the government&amp;rsquo ; s &amp;ldquo ; naive approach to Beijing&amp;rdquo ; has hampered efforts to release Spavor and Kovrig. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This case should be being dealt with at the highest levels . But Justin Trudeau has repeatedly refused to intervene , &amp;rdquo ; Scheer said in a statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; This report by The Canadian Press was first published June 19 , 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;mdash ; With files from the Associated Press &lt;/p&gt; The Canadian PressFri , 19 Jun 2020 12:49:06 **45;16175;TOOLONG ... COVID-19 coronavirus is changing . What 's not clear is what it meanshttp : **35;16222;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;16259;TOOLONG ... title= " A doctor cares for an elderly patient with COVID-19 on June 18 , 2020 in Santiago , Chile . " alt= " A doctor cares for an elderly patient with COVID-19 on June 18 , 2020 in Santiago , Chile . While there are reports in some places that COVID-19 appears to be weakening , in other places it appears as potent as ever . " data-portal-copyright= " Marcelo Hernandez/Getty Images " **28;16294;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572687 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; When a handful of doctors in northern Italy &lt;a href= " https : **33;16324;TOOLONG ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;recently claimed&lt;/a&gt; that the virus that causes COVID-19 appears to be losing steam , that it no longer seems to be the same &amp;ldquo ; biological bomb&amp;rdquo ; it was when the pandemic first hit , expert reaction ranged from bewilderment to horror . Some thought something must have been lost in translation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Then , only days later , Dr. Donald Yealy , chief of emergency medicine at the University of Pittsburgh Medical Centre , a US$21 billion health enterprise that spans 40 hospitals across western and central Pennsylvania , New York State and Maryland , gathered his notes for his latest press briefing . Yealy remarked that , anecdotally , COVID-19 cases seem less severe than a few weeks ago . People aren&amp;rsquo ; t requiring as much intensive care . The proportion needing to be placed on a ventilator to help them breathe has fallen . The&amp;nbsp ; total amount of the virus in those who test positive seems &amp;ldquo ; much less&amp;rdquo ; than in the earlier stages of the crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yealy said he wasn&amp;rsquo ; t attributing the observations &amp;ldquo ; solely or even predominantly&amp;rdquo ; to mutations. &amp;ldquo ; We simply don&amp;rsquo ; t know . &amp;rdquo ; Viral pandemics are dynamic and complicated , he said . They wax and wane . Numerous factors could be at play . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He knows how hungry people are for a glimmer of hopeful news about a virus occupying so many minds . In an interview this week with the National Post , he chose his words carefully . &amp;ldquo ; I am not saying it&amp;rsquo ; s gone away &amp;mdash ; the message isn&amp;rsquo ; t to declare victory , or say that we have this beaten , &amp;rdquo ; Yealy said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But it&amp;rsquo ; s fair to say that the experience with the virus is changing . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It&amp;rsquo ; s been more than 100 days since the World Health Organization declared COVID-19 a pandemic on March 11 and called on countries to ready their hospitals . By then , 4,291 people had lost their lives . One hundred days later , the global death toll is nearing 450,000 . Even as cases fall in Canada , no one is ruling out a second wave . Instead , health officials are bracing for the unknown . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While Yealy said people in his regions &amp;ldquo ; don&amp;rsquo ; t seem to have the same level of sickness as a few weeks ago , &amp;rdquo ; several doctors in Canada said they have seen nothing of the kind , that there&amp;rsquo ; s no evidence the virus is losing potency . &amp;ldquo ; In short , no , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Alan Drummond , of the Canadian Association of Emergency Physicians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16359;TOOLONG ... moves to update COVID-19 guidance after ' great news ' in drug **26;16394;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16422;TOOLONG ... do n't need to wear masks or follow strict social distancing when they go to school : SickKids **27;16457;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16486;TOOLONG ... does COVID-19 kill some and spare others ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... While the number of total confirmed cases in Ontario is falling , Toronto General Hospital&amp;rsquo ; s Dr. Niall Ferguson sees no sign of &amp;ldquo ; weakening , &amp;rdquo ; though the hospital , because of its heroic life support program known as ECMO , receives some of the most severe cases . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In the last 10 days , we still put five patients on extracorporeal life support , the most extreme support that we have for people who are failing mechanical ventilation , &amp;rdquo ; said Ferguson , head of critical care for Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s University Health Network . The youngest patient was 22. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In London , Ont. , similar proportions of people are still being admitted to hospital and the ICU . The proportion requiring mechanical ventilation hasn&amp;rsquo ; t changed ; it&amp;rsquo ; s still around two-thirds of patients , said Dr. Wael Haddara , chief of critical care medicine at London Health Sciences Centre . &amp;ldquo ; So , there is no indication that the severity has diminished at all . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In fact , the opposite might be true . &lt;a href= " https : **33;16521;TOOLONG ... in China reported last month&lt;/a&gt; that people in a new cluster of cases in two northeast regions were taking longer to show symptoms , and longer to recover . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16556;TOOLONG ... week , scientists at Scripps Research in Florida&lt;/a&gt; reported , in a pre-print paper , that a genetic mutation in the virus circulating throughout Europe and the U.S. significantly increases its &amp;ldquo ; infectivity. &amp;rdquo ; The mutation , known as D614G , quadrupled the number of spikes the virus uses to latch onto , break into and infect human cells . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Genome Canada , with $41 million in federal backing , has launched its own massive viral genome-sequencing project . &amp;ldquo ; We ourselves have seen the D614G gene in sequences of our own . So it&amp;rsquo ; s here , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Samira Mubareka , a medical microbiologist at Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s Sunnybrook Hospital . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; What&amp;rsquo ; s not clear is what any of this means . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Notions that SARS-CoV-2 will evolve into the Andromeda Strain or become as benign as the common cold prey on our ignorance and fear , &amp;rdquo ; &lt;a href= " https : **33;16591;TOOLONG ... biologists wrote in Undark Magazine . &lt;/a&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The WHO said last week nothing has changed in terms of severity or transmissibility and that the last thing needed is to suggest the virus has suddenly mutated into something less dangerous . The virus , said the WHO&amp;rsquo ; s emergencies chief Mike Ryan , remains a &amp;ldquo ; killer . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;16626;TOOLONG ... title= " While there are some subtle genetic changes in COVID-19 around the world , none have resulted in differences in the way the virus spreads or the type of disease it causes , the WHO has said . " alt= " While there are some subtle genetic changes in COVID-19 around the world , none have resulted in differences in the way the virus spreads or the type of disease it causes , the WHO has said . " data-portal-copyright= " AAMIR QURESHI/AFP via Getty Images " **28;16661;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572688 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; SARS-CoV-2 only showed itself to humans roughly seven months ago , and while it is changing , it seems to be mutating at a relatively low rate . &amp;ldquo ; What&amp;rsquo ; s hard to tease out is , when you do see changes &amp;mdash ; you might see anywhere between half a dozen and a dozen changes in any given virus &amp;mdash ; is to really understand what the significance is , &amp;rdquo ; said Mubareka , a virologist and infectious diseases doctor . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; More than 40,000 genome sequences have been uploaded to publicly available websites . Scientists are scouring the genetic codes , reading through the 30,000 chemical letters , looking for blips and deletions and comparing sequences coming out of other countries , and while there are some subtle changes , none have resulted in differences in the way the virus spreads or the type of disease it causes , the WHO has said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yealy has no hard scientific data ; &amp;ldquo ; it&amp;rsquo ; s just a general , crude observation&amp;rdquo ; that cases under his care look different , he said . &amp;ldquo ; Perhaps we&amp;rsquo ; re better at treating patients . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Western and central Pennsylvania are experiencing nowhere near the peak of people with suspected or actual disease . It&amp;rsquo ; s possible physical distancing , face masks and better hygiene are having an effect . It&amp;rsquo ; s possible that the &amp;nbsp ; most susceptible were the ones disproportionately attacked in the first wave of the pandemic . The warmer weather and humidity may be playing into this , Yealy added . But viruses can change over time . &amp;ldquo ; This particular virus doesn&amp;rsquo ; t look like it&amp;rsquo ; s changing a lot . But it&amp;rsquo ; s a possibility to think about . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are probably six different factors at play and we won&amp;rsquo ; t understand all of this . This is a virus that no one knew anything about eight months ago . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Scientists are frantically trying to analyze outbreaks in real time , looking at regions where waves have come and gone . The virus that finally reached us in Canada &amp;ldquo ; would have been passed so many more times in humans than it did at the beginning of the outbreak , &amp;rdquo ; in Wuhan , Mubareka said . &amp;ldquo ; We have a lot of blindspots as far as the genome goes . &amp;rdquo ; Even with the process of sequencing itself , it&amp;rsquo ; s hard to figure out what&amp;rsquo ; s a signal of something of consequence , and what&amp;rsquo ; s just meaningless noise . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When a virus enters a host , it replicates thousands of times . RNA viruses like this one can accumulate small , random mutations , because the molecular machinery it uses to copy itself sometimes gets sloppy . &amp;ldquo ; It makes errors , and sometimes if those errors benefit the virus they&amp;rsquo ; ll be selected for &amp;mdash ; they&amp;rsquo ; ll persist and get passed on and on because there is some kind of fitness advantage , &amp;rdquo ; Mubareka said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But sometimes less lethal versions emerge . After sampling nasal swab samples from 382 COVID-positive people , Arizona State University researchers found a single sample that was missing a piece of its genome , 81 of the letters gone . The deletion uncannily mirrors a mutation that arose in the SARS outbreak in 2003 that attenuated the virus and that scientists believe caused much less severe disease later in the outbreak . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , &amp;nbsp ; &amp;ldquo ; we have to make sure that whatever statements we make are backed up by good , hard science , so it&amp;rsquo ; s reproducible , &amp;rdquo ; said Dalhousie University immunologist David Kelvin . That means testing isolates of the virus in the lab , in cells and animals , before anyone can really say , yes , this means something . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Because the downside is really bad if you mislead the public to think that the virus is no longer as potent as it once was . People will of course relax their vigilance and succumb to a disease they probably could have avoided , &amp;rdquo ; Kelvin said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Governments have also expanded testing to include people with more mild disease or no symptoms at all . And if we&amp;rsquo ; re testing people with less severe disease , &amp;ldquo ; well , sure , it will look like ( the virus ) is becoming less severe , &amp;rdquo ; Mubareka said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The amount of virus on a swab also isn&amp;rsquo ; t a measure of potency , added Arinjay Banerjee , of McMaster University&amp;rsquo ; s Institute of Infectious Diseases . Sampling time is critical . How much virus is scooped up on a nose swab depends on when in the course of the infection it was taken . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , an ideal pathogen , a clever virus , learns to co-exist with its host , Banerjee said , &amp;ldquo ; because if a host dies , if the individual dies , the virus dies with it . &amp;rdquo ; A virus has to be able to leap from the infected to the uninfected , spreading its progeny to the next victim , to sustain the virus population . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ll just be honest and upfront : We don&amp;rsquo ; t know . We just don&amp;rsquo ; t know if it&amp;rsquo ; s weakening , &amp;rdquo ; Banerjee said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some coronavirus scientists have speculated that perhaps centuries ago there could have been an outbreak like this with coronaviruses that today are seasonal bugs . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We don&amp;rsquo ; t have data on this ; we haven&amp;rsquo ; t tested this , &amp;rdquo ; Banerjee said . &amp;ldquo ; But who knows , maybe the coronaviruses that cause mild cold-like symptoms could have been lethal in human populations many , many years ago . The virus and the host learned to co-adapt. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Italy has suffered some of the world&amp;rsquo ; s worst COVID-19 death tolls , but fewer people are being admitted to hospital compared to three months ago , and in some regions , &amp;ldquo ; even the patients that are admitted , especially in emergency , are less severe , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Matteo Bassetti , director of an infectious disease clinic at San Martino Hospital in Genoa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They have fever , some respiratory symptoms . They have cough . But even in similar ages , they don&amp;rsquo ; t have the same aggressive picture they had three months ago . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am not suggesting not to use the masks , or not to wash the hands or not stay one metre ( in Canada , the official advice is two metres ) away from the other , &amp;rdquo ; Bassetti said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am just giving the clinical impression , and the clinical impression is the disease is different . I am more optimistic than I was three months ago . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;16691;TOOLONG " **35;16721;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **32;16758;TOOLONG ... class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;sharonkirke ... ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; Sharon KirkeyFri , 19 Jun 2020 11:00:24 **45;16792;TOOLONG ... Ivison : Peter MacKay has his mojo back , performance during debates showshttp : **35;16839;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;16876;TOOLONG ... title= " Conservative Party of Canada leadership candidate Peter MacKay speaks during the English debate in Toronto on June 18 , 2020. " alt= " Conservative Party of Canada leadership candidate Peter MacKay speaks during the English debate in Toronto on June 18 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Tijana Martin/The Canadian Press " **28;16911;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572664 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Peter MacKay&amp;rsquo ; s performance in the Conservative leadership race has vindicated the criticisms levelled by rival Erin O&amp;rsquo ; Toole that the former defence minister is yesterday&amp;rsquo ; s man ; an entitled , policy-light , serial flip-flopper who keeps putting his foot in it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That was all true until around the hour mark in the French-language debate on Wednesday , when MacKay appeared to remember why he is running . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked how he planned to attract new support , MacKay said the Conservative Party had a perception problem in the last election . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to change that perception . The Conservative Party is a big tent but we have to prove that to people , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to welcome everyone into the party and present ourselves as an inclusive , modern party with clear and modern policies . We need a plan to protect the environment . I also support LGBTQ2 communities without reservation . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have to reach out to young people , cultural communities , Indigenous people and francophones. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It is crucial to present ourselves as a party that says , &amp;lsquo ; Yes , come in , we respect you , what&amp;rsquo ; s your opinion , what&amp;rsquo ; s your perspective ? &amp;rsquo ; We need to have a party where people see themselves reflected . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;16941;TOOLONG ... is painful as hell ' : Conservative leadership debate earns candidates mixed reviews for French **27;16976;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17005;TOOLONG ... first debate of Conservative leadership contest , it was the O'Toole and MacKay **25;17040;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &g; ... This proclamation will be music to the ears of Conservatives who recognize the need to peel off disaffected centrist voters from the Liberals . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The fact that he said it in reasonably coherent French , added to the feeling that MacKay has re-discovered his mojo. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Over the course of two nights , MacKay appealed not just to Conservative partisans , but to the wider voting public &amp;ndash ; which suggests he believes his lead is large enough to risk upsetting social conservatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On Wednesday , O&amp;rsquo ; Toole tried to insert himself into the debate but MacKay brushed off O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s accusations that he is dividing Conservatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You say you want to bring the party and the country together but you&amp;rsquo ; re the wrong person to do that . You&amp;rsquo ; re an angry man , Mr. O&amp;rsquo ; Toole . Why are you always angry ? &amp;rdquo ; asked MacKay . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It was the defining moment of this most unremarkable of campaigns . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That the barb stung was apparent by O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s more subdued performance in the English debate . As a result , the debate on Thursday night was much less cantankerous , with far fewer clashes between the front-runners. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;17067;TOOLONG ... title= " Conservative Party of Canada leadership candidates ( left to right ) Erin O'Toole , Peter MacKay , Derek Sloan and Leslyn Lewis wait for the start of the French-language leadership debate in Toronto on June 17 , 2020. " alt= " Conservative Party of Canada leadership candidates ( left to right ) Erin O'Toole , Peter MacKay , Derek Sloan and Leslyn Lewis wait for the start of the French-language leadership debate in Toronto on June 17 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Frank Gunn/The Canadian Press " **28;17102;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571125 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; The two other candidates &amp;ndash ; Derek Sloan and Leslyn Lewis &amp;ndash ; were handicapped on Wednesday by their limited French language abilities , effectively kissing goodbye to support in Quebec . With every riding carrying equal weight , it is hard to win this contest if you have written off a quarter of the points going in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Both were markedly better in English , particularly on the question of racism , where Lewis distinguished between individual and systemic racism , and Sloan criticized Quebec&amp;rsquo ; s ban on religious symbols as an example of systemic racism . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lewis still has the potential to spring a shock . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it was MacKay who consolidated his status . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s face reflected as much in the post-French debate press conference . The former veterans&amp;rsquo ; affairs minister looked like he&amp;rsquo ; d found a dead fly in his water glass . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay can consider himself fortunate that he does not have a more compelling , bilingual opponent . For all his fine words , his platform is not much of an improvement on the one offered by Andrew Scheer last fall &amp;ndash ; an offering that was summarily rejected by the electorate . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay talked about protecting the environment but his plan cancels the Liberal carbon tax and offers no alternative route to reducing greenhouse gas emissions . He criticized O&amp;rsquo ; Toole for presenting an environment plan that &amp;ldquo ; looks like a Liberal plan&amp;rdquo ; because it allows provinces to bring forward their own form of carbon pricing . He even labelled his rival , &amp;ldquo ; Erin Trudeau&amp;rdquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet , there is nothing in his platform to suggest he has a better idea . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay&amp;rsquo ; s campaign has lurched from one gaffe to another , as the candidate has taken advice from people he might have been better off ignoring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet , at game time on Wednesday , MacKay discovered his value proposition &amp;ndash ; that he is the best-placed candidate to win a general election &amp;ndash ; and zinged his closest rival as being too cocky , egotistical and combative. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay stood tall , looked comfortable , sounded credible and even appeared to be enjoying himself , disproving critics who didn&amp;rsquo ; t think he&amp;rsquo ; d be able to string two sentences together in French . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Toole was reduced to complaining that MacKay had attacked him , contrary to his claims of cordiality . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The contest between the two men has become personal &amp;ndash ; if there was mutual respect before , it is gone now . O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s team is led by Fred DeLorey , who ran and lost in MacKay&amp;rsquo ; s former Central Nova riding in the 2015 election , without much help from the former MP . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The O&amp;rsquo ; Toole campaign has hardly taken a New Testament approach to turning the other cheek . The MP for Durham got his retaliation in first in a digital ad that claimed : &amp;ldquo ; With Peter MacKay as leader , the next election is already lost . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The effects seem to have been no more lasting than the damage MacKay did to his own campaign . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Yet the Nova Scotian has emerged from two potentially tricky debates in better shape than he went in. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In his concluding remarks Thursday , MacKay reminded Conservatives than he is the co-founder of their party , a six times elected former MP , who held three of the highest offices in the land during nearly a decade in Stephen Harper&amp;rsquo ; s cabinet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In effect , he posed the same query as Henry Ford when he was asked about leadership : &amp;ldquo ; The question : &amp;lsquo ; Who ought to be boss ? &amp;rsquo ; is like asking , &amp;lsquo ; Who ought to be the tenor in the quartet ? &amp;rsquo ; Obviously the man who can sing tenor , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;Editor 's note : A version of this piece said Fred DeLorey ran in the last election . He , in fact , ran in 2015 . The mistake has been corrected . &lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;17132;TOOLONG " **35;17162;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : //twitter.com/IvisonJ " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;IvisonJ&lt;/ ... &lt;/p&gt; John IvisonFri , 19 Jun 2020 03:13:09 **45;17199;TOOLONG ...
