" data-portal-copyright= " Tijana Martin/The Canadian Press " **28;17317;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572659 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the last chance that Conservative party members had to watch the four leadership contenders debate , the gloves came back on . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While the French-language debate on Wednesday night featured lengthy , furious exchanges between Peter MacKay and Erin O&amp;rsquo ; Toole , the English debate on Thursday night had a much friendlier tone as the candidates debated issues ranging from environmental policy to systemic racism to how to grow the party&amp;rsquo ; s base . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The changed dynamic was likely due to all four candidates being on equal ground , as Derek Sloan and Leslyn Lewis played a much bigger role on Thursday . The night before , both Sloan and Lewis largely stayed on the sidelines because of their poor French . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , the lack of fiery arguments also meant that MacKay and O&amp;rsquo ; Toole , as the heavy favourites to win the race to replace Andrew Scheer , largely escaped unscathed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In the opening rounds , candidates made their cases for why they can lead the party to victory in the next election , and each put their emphasis in a different area . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; For O&amp;rsquo ; Toole , it&amp;rsquo ; s about winning in the suburbs , a crucial swing region the party largely failed in during the last two elections . For MacKay , it&amp;rsquo ; s about his history of bringing conservative factions together and founding the modern party with Stephen Harper . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17347;TOOLONG ... first debate of Conservative leadership contest , it was the O'Toole and MacKay **25;17382;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17409;TOOLONG ... is painful as hell ' : Conservative leadership debate earns candidates mixed reviews for French **27;17444;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p ... O&amp;rsquo ; Toole repeatedly cited the fact he&amp;rsquo ; s won his Greater Toronto Area riding three elections in a row . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We need a principled leader who will unite our party by respecting all conservatives , a leader who can show more urban and suburban Canadians that their values of liberty , family and equality are at the core of our party , &amp;rdquo ; O&amp;rsquo ; Toole said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He accused MacKay of dividing the party , a likely reference to the &amp;ldquo ; stinking albatross&amp;rdquo ; comment MacKay made about social conservative issues after the party&amp;rsquo ; s defeat in the federal election last fall , and which MacKay has since said he regrets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I&amp;rsquo ; ve only focused on bringing conservatives together , &amp;rdquo ; MacKay fired back . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s what I&amp;rsquo ; ve done since my earliest days back in 1997 , when I first left the prosecutor&amp;rsquo ; s office to join the Conservative party in that election . I&amp;rsquo ; ve worked with Stephen Harper , I&amp;rsquo ; ve worked with social conservatives , fiscal conservatives across the spectrum . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay also touted the fact he has more endorsements from the party&amp;rsquo ; s caucus than O&amp;rsquo ; Toole. &amp;ldquo ; We need a leader who attacks liberals , not conservatives , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; I will unite the party as I did before with Stephen Harper . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One of the few times MacKay and O&amp;rsquo ; Toole clashed directly was during an exchange on carbon pricing , with MacKay blasting O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s platform promise to look at a pricing regime for large industrial emitters . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s only one person on this stage who&amp;rsquo ; s talking about , and I quote , &amp;lsquo ; Forging a national industrial , regulatory and pricing regime across the country , ' &amp;rdquo ; MacKay said . &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s Mr. O&amp;rsquo ; Toole. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Toole said his plan is in line with what&amp;rsquo ; s advocated by conservative leaders such as Alberta Premier Jason Kenney and Ontario Premier Doug Ford . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I find it stunning that Mr. MacKay would suggest he would not support a plan put forward by Premier Kenney with respect to large emitters , or Premier Ford , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; They know their economies better than some bureaucrat in Ottawa . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lewis and Sloan , meanwhile , showcased their much different approaches despite both being backed by social conservative groups . While Sloan hit a hard-right message throughout the debate , Lewis had a much more optimistic tone . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I will never compromise on conservative values , I will never sell out Canada and I will always put Canadians first , &amp;rdquo ; Sloan said . &amp;ldquo ; I will never give an inch to political correctness . I won&amp;rsquo ; t cede an ounce of our sovereignty to international organizations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He promised to end Canada&amp;rsquo ; s funding of the World Health Organization , to pull Canada out of the Paris climate agreement , and expressed support for U.S. President Donald Trump . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Lewis , meanwhile , described herself as &amp;ldquo ; the right leader with the right policies at the right time . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I am the leader that can withstand the liberal attacks and the lies about who we are as Canadians , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; We are good people . To win the next election , we are going to need somebody to lead with courage , compassion and common sense . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At various times during the debate , both O&amp;rsquo ; Toole and MacKay were careful to highlight their agreement with a point made by Lewis . That&amp;rsquo ; s likely due to the ranked ballot , where Lewis&amp;rsquo ; s more moderate vision of social conservatism could well put her in the role of kingmaker . Sloan&amp;rsquo ; s hardline approach may mean his voters are less likely to throw support to the more establishment candidates . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Voting in the leadership race will take place this summer by mail-in ballot , and is scheduled to conclude Aug. 21 . The race uses a ranked ballot , meaning a candidate will need to eventually collect 50 per cent of the vote to win . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **27;17473;TOOLONG " **34;17502;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : //twitter.com/btaplatt " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;btaplatt&lt; ... &lt;/p&gt; Brian PlattFri , 19 Jun 2020 00:21:58 **45;17538;TOOLONG ... PoliticsNewsTrudeau blames failed UN Security Council bid on late entry to race , others blame ' superficial ' foreign policyhttp : **34;17585;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;17621;TOOLONG ... title= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau holds a news conference outside his residence at Rideau Cottage in Ottawa , on June 18 , 2020. " alt= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau holds a news conference outside his residence at Rideau Cottage in Ottawa , on June 18 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " Justin Tang/The Canadian Press " **28;17656;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572535 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;ndash ; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau blamed Canada&amp;rsquo ; s failed attempt to gain a UN Security Council seat on entering the race late &amp;mdash ; a decision he deliberately made in 2016 after other countries&amp;rsquo ; campaigns were well underway . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It was the prime minister&amp;rsquo ; s decision to enter late , &amp;rdquo ; said Adam Chapnick , a professor at the Royal Military College and author of a book on Canada and the UN . &amp;ldquo ; He has identified the exact reason that Canada did not perform as well as the government wanted it to . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But one former Canadian ambassador blamed the failure on the Trudeau government&amp;rsquo ; s superficial foreign policy that hamstrung its diplomats . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It shows that through the Trudeau years Canada&amp;rsquo ; s superficiality and insouciance in foreign affairs got through to the rest of the world , and the world decided we were too flimsy , unfocused , ad hoc and chaotic to merit support , &amp;rdquo ; said Stephen Lewis , a lifelong New Democrat who was appointed by then Progressive Conservative prime minister Brian Mulroney to be his UN ambassador in the 1980s. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canada lost the vote Wednesday finishing behind Norway and Ireland in the contest for two non-permanent seats on the Security Council . Norway had 130 votes , Ireland 128 and Canada 108. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Trudeau , who personally championed the campaign , spoke to dozens of world leaders and made the initial decision to vie for the seat in 2016. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The reality was that coming in five years later than them , gave us a delay that unfortunately we weren&amp;rsquo ; t able to overcome , &amp;rdquo ; said Trudeau at a press conference Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17686;TOOLONG ... loses bid for seat on UN Security Council , finishing third to Norway and Ireland in **25;17721;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;17748;TOOLONG ... Gurney : Canada 's back , and the United Nations rightly did n't **27;17783;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p ... Chapnick said Ireland and Norway had indicated they would like a seat roughly a decade before Canada had anything to say , giving them a long advantage . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He noted the first briefing books provided to Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s first Foreign Affairs Minister St&amp;eacute ; phane Dion didn&amp;rsquo ; t mention making a bid . He said realistically , with the protocol and informal rules about countries competing against each other it could be 2033 before Canada could make another run for the council . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; However , Lewis dismissed Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s assessment as &amp;ldquo ; nonsense . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Four years should be more than enough to secure the seat . If commitments to Norway and Ireland were earlier made , they would readily have been changed if we had something to offer , &amp;rdquo ; said Lewis , adding that the government&amp;rsquo ; s campaign platform hamstrung the current ambassador Marc-Andre Blanchard . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Blanchard was excellent , but he had nothing to work with , &amp;rdquo ; said Lewis . &amp;ldquo ; The prime minister is just fine with rhetorical flourishes . But when it comes to concrete policy , there just isn&amp;rsquo ; t any . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;17812;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;17848;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; The loss was also &amp;ldquo ; humiliating&amp;rdquo ; for Canada and for Trudeau personally , said Michael Bociurkiw , a Canadian-born analyst who has worked abroad for the Organization for Security Co-operation in Europe and the United Nations Children&amp;rsquo ; s Fund . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I think this also indicates the decline of influence of the Trudeau brand , possibly impacted by previous incidents such as the India state visit and the blackface revelations , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Shuvaloy Majumdar , a senior fellow at the MacDonald Laurier Institute and former adviser on foreign policy in Stephen Harper&amp;rsquo ; s conservative government , said , &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s really the biggest embarrassment ( Trudeau ) will suffer in his prime minister-ship in Canada , particularly on international affairs . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Former Quebec Premier Jean Charest was enlisted as a special envoy for the bid . He said Canada had a lot of support , but many countries had made commitments to Ireland and Norway before Canada&amp;rsquo ; s campaign began . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ireland&amp;rsquo ; s 128 votes was exactly the number to avoid a second ballot . Charest said he believed Canada would have done better on a second ballot . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Countries make commitments and a lot of them feel they&amp;rsquo ; re committed to a first round , but not the second one and Canada would have done well on the second round . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Charest said he got no sense from foreign diplomats that there were concerns with Canada&amp;rsquo ; s foreign policy , but also conceded they may have simply been keeping quiet . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; re dealing with diplomats and so people are not inclined to sit at a table and take a run at you . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative leader Andrew Scheer denounced the campaign as a waste of money and Canada&amp;rsquo ; s moral standing . He said Trudeau had failed to show global leadership repeatedly during the campaign . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He remained silent when standing beside leaders of countries with terrible human rights records , because he was afraid of offending them , &amp;rdquo ; he said in the House of Commons . &amp;ldquo ; He has also turned our backs on our friends and allies , voting in favour of one-sided anti-Israel motions at the United Nations . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;17884;TOOLONG ... title= " Prime Minister Justin Trudeau meets with Senegal 's President Macky Sall during as part of a trip to Africa in early February meant to drum up support for Canada 's UN Security Council bid . " data-portal-copyright= " Sean Kilpatrick/The Canadian Press/File " **28;17919;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78531287 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Scheer drew particular attention to Trudeau&amp;rsquo ; s visit earlier this year to Senegal , on a trip through Africa . He said Trudeau had failed to defend LGBT rights . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He stood right beside the president of Senegal when that president defended Senegal&amp;rsquo ; s laws making homosexuality a crime . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Charest said the campaign got Canada reengaged diplomatically and it should continue to step up those efforts . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s missing in action in some parts of the world , Africa , the Middle East , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &amp;ldquo ; We let the American relationship , maybe some will say justifiably so , suck up all the air in the room , but at the end of the day , we have to have a stronger presence in the world . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; -- with files from The Canadian Press and Bloomberg News&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **29;17949;TOOLONG " **36;17980;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a class= " twitter-follow-button " href= " https : **25;18018;TOOLONG " &gt; **32;18045;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; Ryan TumiltyThu , 18 Jun 2020 23:34:11 **45;18079;TOOLONG ... PoliticsNewsCanadian economy headed for ' significant rebound ' as provinces reopen , but roadblocks remain : economistshttp : **30;18126;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;18158;TOOLONG ... title= " Scotiabank Chief Economist Jean-Fran ? ois Perrault , one of the economists who spoke before the House of Commons finance committee on Thursday . " alt= " Scotiabank Chief Economist Jean-Fran ? ois Perrault , one of the economists who spoke before the House of Commons finance committee on Thursday . " data-portal-copyright= " Peter J. Thompson/National Post/File " **28;18193;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572526 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;mdash ; The Canadian economy is showing green shoots and is poised for a sharp recovery as provincial governments gradually lift COVID-19 restrictions , chief economists at Canada&amp;rsquo ; s major banks said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; And yet , it could be years before the economy returns to pre-pandemic levels , as some battered sectors continue to drag on overall growth , several economists told the House of Commons finance committee on Thursday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Their comments come as improved labour and consumer spending data in May provide at least some room for optimism on the Canadian economy , after the COVID-19 pandemic earlier this year pummelled corporate balance sheets and sent governments deep into debt . The federal budget watchdog on Thursday posted a &amp;ldquo ; significant&amp;rdquo ; update to its economic outlook for Canada , estimating a much smaller contraction in GDP than it projected in April . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Canada has just experienced the deepest , sharpest economic downturn in the post-war era , but it also now looks to have been the shortest recession ever as well , &amp;rdquo ; said Douglas Porter , chief economist at BMO Bank of Montreal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; His comments were echoed by other leading economists , who said the Canadian economy is likely headed for a steep climb in coming months , after scraping bottom in the second quarter of 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s very important to think about this in two halves : the first part of the year ( was ) a tremendously damaging economic scenario , and the second part of the year , obviously a very significant rebound as we reopen , &amp;rdquo ; said Jean-Fran&amp;ccedil ; ois Perrault , chief economist at Scotiabank. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18223;TOOLONG ... or no lockdown , study shows COVID-19 's economic destruction followed a similar path either way&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18258;TOOLONG ... of Canada says economy facing prolonged , uneven **29;18293;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... Higher rates of growth in the housing and automotive sectors in particular have boosted growth over the past month , while the labour market showed modest signs of improvement . But Perrault also cautioned that Canada , similar to other economies , would have &amp;ldquo ; a long way to go&amp;rdquo ; before returning to typical levels of activity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These are all indications that the economy is on the path to normalizing &amp;mdash ; but it shouldn&amp;rsquo ; t be read or interpreted as the economy is going back to where it was . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Sharp improvements in labour and consumer spending levels come as the Liberal government continues to pour billions into COVID-19 financial assistance programs , which economists said have staved off economic ruin . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In my opinion , it is far too early to say that we are experiencing the worst economic crisis in decades , and that a depression is inevitable , &amp;rdquo ; said Mathieu D&amp;rsquo ; Anjou , deputy chief economist at Desjardins Group . &amp;ldquo ; The drop in GDP will be spectacular this year , but if we succeed in ending lockdowns in the next few months , the consequences for households and for businesses could be limited . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Still , improved economic data will only intensify pressure on Prime Minister Justin Trudeau to begin unwinding support programs like the Canada Emergency Response Benefit ( CERB ) , which provides $2,000 a month to jobless Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Economists have long urged Ottawa to refrain from allowing CERB and other financial assistance programs to become permanent , as it could turn recent deficits into structural government expenses . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; While the two-month extension to the CERB is welcome , I would encourage the government to start planning now towards a new , modern EI system , &amp;rdquo ; said David Macdonald , senior economist at the Canadian Centre for Policy Alternatives . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Failure to adeptly roll back social support programs could now run the risk of creating drag on the wider economy , keeping more Canadians out of the workforce . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We&amp;rsquo ; ve already seen evidence that shows that high COVID unemployment benefits can encourage layoffs , discourage work and delay productive reallocation , &amp;rdquo ; said Sherry Cooper , chief economist at Dominion Lending Centres . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Even with sizeable corporate supports in place , she said some industries might never climb out of the hole left by economic shutdowns. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The risk is that the pandemic is inflicting a reallocation shock , in which some firms and even entire sectors suffer lasting damage . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Liberal government also needs to begin planning longer term by providing the public with its expectations around vaccines for COVID-19 , Cooper said , which could influence its policy response under various scenarios . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Economic theory and econometric modelling do not provide a specific roadmap , &amp;rdquo ; she said . &amp;ldquo ; The government needs to explain its view on the likelihood of a vaccine , and antivirals , over a six-month , one-year and three-year timeframe. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Finance Minister Bill Morneau is now scheduled to release a fiscal update on July 8 , after delaying his annual budget and refusing for months to provide an interim fiscal update , citing volatility in the markets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The federal government is expected to run a $256 billion deficit in 2021 , according to the report released by the PBO on Thursday . The economy is now expected to shrink by 6.8 per cent in 2020 , from an earlier estimate of 12 per cent in April . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;18324;TOOLONG " **35;18354;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **32;18391;TOOLONG ... class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;jessesnyder ... &lt;/p&gt; Jesse SnyderThu , 18 Jun 2020 23:13:44 **45;18425;TOOLONG ... PoliticsNewsU.S. accuses five Canadians of running $25M COVID-19 pump-and-dump stock market swindlehttp : **33;18472;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;18507;TOOLONG ... title= " When the COVID-19 pitches went out in the alleged stock fraud , " trading volume spiked to over 3 million shares per day , " according to the SEC. " alt= " When the COVID-19 pitches went out in the alleged stock fraud , " trading volume spiked to over 3 million shares per day , " according to the SEC. " data-portal-copyright= " BRENDAN SMIALOWSKI/AFP via Getty Images " **28;18542;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78572488 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; U.S. authorities accuse five Canadians of running a methodical $25-million investment fraud that climaxed with fake promises of producing COVID-19 protective equipment at the height of pandemic panic . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The four men and one woman are accused of taking the old pump-and-dump stock fraud method to higher levels of obscurantism. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They are variously accused of using a phoney passport , fake identities and bogus lawyer&amp;rsquo ; s letters to resurrect and steal defunct companies and using foreign trading accounts to hide who controlled them . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The companies&amp;rsquo ; stocks were then artificially inflated using plagiarized websites , email campaigns and sales calls , U.S. authorities allege . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some of the claims to boost share value preyed on pandemic fears : one firm claimed to make medical masks and another touchless sales kiosks for retailers . Neither did either of those things , according to securities officials . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The enforcement actions announced last week by the U.S. Securities Exchange Commission ( SEC ) target : Shane Schmidt , 52 , of Vancouver ; Nelson Gomes , 38 , a resident of Bahamas and Hong Kong ; Michael Luckhoo , 31 , of Mississauga , Ont. ; Douglas Roe , 47 , of Vancouver ; and Kelly Warawa , 36 , of Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18572;TOOLONG ... charges firm president , trader in COVID-19 fraud **28;18607;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;18637;TOOLONG ... has taken down over 1,500 coronavirus-related scam **26;18672;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... Schmidt also faces criminal charges of securities fraud and conspiracy to commit securities fraud . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Among the stocks authorities say were illegally manipulated was a Minnesota penny stock company called Sandy Steele Unlimited , Inc . Originally started in 1968 by a woman named Sandy Steele , it slid into inactivity and was dissolved in 2006. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Almost 15 years after its demise , the company was resurrected . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Change of control documents were filed with the Over-the-Counter Markets stock service that facilitates public trading in companies not listed on national securities exchanges . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The documents declared that Sandy Steele , the company , changed hands in 2017 when Sandy Steele , the original president , resigned ; someone named John Scott took over . The documents showed they had been notarized in Vancouver . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The FBI says the corporate resurrection was based on lies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The documents were fake , the takeover was fake , the reinstatement claims were fake and even John Scott was fake . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An FBI agent tracked down the real Sandy Steele . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; She did not sign , nor was she even aware of , the documents purporting to reflect her resignation , &amp;rdquo ; FBI Agent Keith Brown said in a sworn statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Before that came to light , however , the complex scheme went a long way . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Using lawyer&amp;rsquo ; s letters , a company website and purported transactions , Sandy Steele Unlimited once again was actively trading , although authorities say there was no legitimate business done . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; By late 2019 , the company claimed a shift in direction , moving into &amp;ldquo ; wearable tech , &amp;rdquo ; specializing in cold-weather clothing with heating fibres powered by rechargeable batteries . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These revolutionary garments feature built-in heating panels and are made with material that provides lightweight warmth , comfort , and versatility , &amp;rdquo ; declared a new company website , rebranding as Sandy Tech . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Its future , the company said , was far bigger . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Initial consultations are ongoing with several G20 countries and their militaries who expressed an interest in our products , &amp;rdquo ; and it was working on government projects to use its technology in drones. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We aim to be the new world standard in battery production and the useful applications are limitless , &amp;rdquo ; the company claimed . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Photos of its products , however , appear to have been taken from other manufacturers and the site pushed its stock rather than products . But it was good enough to dupe unsophisticated investors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After its launch , stock sales increased , as did its price . From Oct. 18 , 2019 , to Dec. 13 , 2019 , share prices jumped from $0.05 to $0.61 ; then slid down to $0.02 by the end of January 2020. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The investment flurry , the FBI alleges , came from unsolicited sales calls to elderly people , claiming the company was about to land an NFL contract , which would send value soaring . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That reflects the traditional &amp;ldquo ; pump-and-dump&amp;rdquo ; stock swindle &amp;mdash ; using a so-called boiler room of salesmen to convince people to buy dodgy stock to boost its price and then insiders sell their shares before the price crashes . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Just when things seemed to be wrapping up for the Sandy Steele venture , along came COVID-19 and a huge demand for personal protective equipment . In March , Sandy Steele salesmen started pushing a new reason why the company&amp;rsquo ; s value would skyrocket &amp;mdash ; it was retooling to make medical masks , according to the SEC. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The coronavirus has created a world of opportunities for investors , &amp;rdquo ; a promotional email said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the COVID-19 pitches went out , &amp;ldquo ; trading volume spiked to over 3 million shares per day , &amp;rdquo ; according to the SEC . Again , the insiders started selling shares . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The ride ended in April when the SEC suspended trading , one of the first COVID-19 securities enforcement actions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; When the FBI and the SEC started looking , it didn&amp;rsquo ; t take long to piece together an alleged fraud . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The company president used a false passport with Schmidt&amp;rsquo ; s photo and date of birth but the name John Scott . Payments were made by a company Schmidt controlled or by Schmidt&amp;rsquo ; s personal bank account , the FBI said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; While his acumen with business records seems impressive , his internet operational security was lax . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; He used the same IP addresses in Vancouver to create and log into the company&amp;rsquo ; s website and emails as he used for his personal banking and a Yahoo account in Schmidt&amp;rsquo ; s real name , the FBI said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Canadian phone records linked another IP address to a Vancouver address of a woman Schmidt had a close relationship with . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Similar methods were used on several different stocks &amp;mdash ; in all , the SEC alleges , Schmidt and his colleagues made more than US$25 million from illegal stock sales . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The SEC seeks restraining orders and asset freezes as well as repatriation orders that would moved &amp;ldquo ; all ill-gotten gains&amp;rdquo ; in Canada to the United States to return to any swindled investors . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Emails sent by National Post to the corporate contacts for Sandy Steele Unlimited seeking comment were returned as undeliverable. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; * Email : &lt;a href= " **31;18700;TOOLONG " **38;18733;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **25;18773;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;ADHumphreys ... &lt;/p&gt; Adrian HumphreysThu , 18 Jun 2020 22:43:47 **45;18800;TOOLONG ... has boosted some premiers ' approval ratings by 30 percentage points , Legault benefitting most : pollhttp : **36;18847;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;18885;TOOLONG ... title= " Quebec Premier Francois Legault announces his intention to re-open primary schools during a news conference on the COVID-19 pandemic , Monday , April 27 , 2020 at the legislature in Quebec City . It found that not only had Legault&amp;rsquo ; s approval rating increased by ten points to a total of 76 per cent , but the approval rate of every premier had increased since the beginning of the COVID-19 pandemic &amp;mdash ; some dramatically . The approval rating of five of the nine premiers measured skyrocketed by more than 30 percentage points since the beginning of Canada&amp;rsquo ; s pandemic response . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a press release , Dart pointed out that its last poll was conducted in early March , a few days before Canada would start implementing social distancing policies and travel restrictions . As COVID-19 cases began to rise in Canada , citizens turned to their premiers&amp;rsquo ; daily press conferences for guidance and information , putting their policies and quick-thinking skills under scrutiny . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s no question that the COVID-19 responsiveness by these premiers has given them a huge lift , &amp;rdquo ; said John Wright , a partner at Dart C-Suite Communicators . &amp;ldquo ; We had premiers who were on their deathbed , politically , who have gone right to the top . " data-portal-copyright= " April Judd " **28;19361;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571659 " /&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 100% " height= " 100% " frameborder= " 0 " allowfullscreen= " true " src= " https : //www.youtu ... &gt; &lt;/iframe&gt; &lt;p&gt; April Judd could not sleep . Her mind was crackling , poring over the list of all the things in life that she had missed out on because of COVID-19 , and all the things that &amp;ldquo ; sucked , &amp;rdquo ; because of COVID-19 , and how devastating the pandemic was for the global collective . &amp;nbsp ; People everywhere were struggling . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Nineteen-year-olds , like Judd , fresh out of John Abbott College on the island of Montreal , were graduates without having had a formal graduation . No cap . No gown . No big send-off , no tearful goodbyes , no nothing , except for an abrupt move home to Mom and Dad&amp;rsquo ; s place in mid-March and , by mid-May , another restless night mulling stuff over , batting away anxieties , finding the silver linings . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I was lying in bed thinking about everything I had missed , but also about all the opportunities I&amp;rsquo ; d been given because of COVID-19 , &amp;rdquo ; Judd said in mid-June , from her family&amp;rsquo ; s dairy farm/winery an hour west of Ottawa . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;19391;TOOLONG ... title= " April Judd serenading her cat , Monty . " data-portal-copyright= " April Judd " **28;19426;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571656 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Seeing peoples&amp;rsquo ; resilience throughout the pandemic , it just got me thinking these deep thoughts , and then my cat jumped on my bed and sat on my face . It was hilarious , because it was symbolic of what life is : a big collage of good , bad , beautiful , chaotic &amp;ndash ; and hilarious &amp;mdash ; &amp;nbsp ; and I wanted to try and show that . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Judd , a self-described &amp;ldquo ; introverted-extrovert , &amp;rdquo ; is also a ham . She is a fan of musicals , and a theatre kid , and she was set to play the role of Mrs. Banks in a school production of Mary Poppins , before all productions and everything else ceased . Her father , Scott , is known to break into spontaneous jigs . Younger brothers , Isaac and Sam , and older sister , Emma , view ribbing one another as family sport . Around the Judd homestead , nourishment involves drinking deep from the well of laughter . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I always think of the other people in my family as being far funnier than I am , &amp;rdquo ; Judd says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Judd can&amp;rsquo ; t name any comedians off-hand , Canadian or otherwise . She is aware , as a people , that Canadians are regarded in some corners as being funny ( see : Catherine O&amp;rsquo ; Hara , Russell Peters , Eugene Levy , Martin Short , Bob and Doug , Lorne Michaels , SCTV , Kids in the Hall , This Hour Has 22 Minutes etc. ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But she never thought of herself as one of the funny ones . &amp;nbsp ; Be that as it may , Girls&amp;rsquo ; Voices at Home , a program of the Seattle-based American non-profit , &lt;a href= " http : //GreaterGood.org " target= " blank " rel= " noopener " **29;19456;TOOLONG , felt Judd was indeed a funny one , and selected her as the winner of its &amp;ldquo ; Class of 2020 Challenge . &amp;rdquo ; The international challenge , open to female high school seniors and college grads worldwide , was to submit a video answering the question : How did the coronavirus change your senior year ? &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;19487;TOOLONG ... title= " April Judd ( upside down ) with her siblings . " alt= " April Judd ( upside down ) with her siblings . " data-portal-copyright= " April Judd " **28;19522;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571658 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hundreds of entries rolled in from 49 countries &amp;mdash ; smart , achingly honest videos , typically several minutes in length . The idea was to present all the videos as a virtual yearbook ; as a record of young women&amp;rsquo ; s voices , from all over , during a shared moment in time . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was &lt;a href= " https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=PcOrC7Z ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Audrey&lt;/a&gt; , from Maryland , crafting a message of spiritual uplift ; &lt;a href= " https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=lWFcxmp ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Katherine&lt;/a&gt; , from Texas , sounding earnest and true ; &lt;a href= " https : //www.youtube.com/watch ? v=FjtxO0t ... target= " blank " rel= " noopener " &gt;Alicia&lt;/a&gt; , from Georgia , unspooling a mesmerizing blast of spoken-word poetry . &amp;nbsp ; In short : the Canadian faced serious competition in a challenge she resolved to accept only after Smudge ( the cat ) had pounced on her head , and the thousand dollar cash scholarship prize loomed on her computer screen while she was researching ways to help pay for university starting September . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;19552;TOOLONG ... title= " April Judd and her family . " alt= " April Judd and her family . " data-portal-copyright= " April Judd " **28;19587;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571657 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I wasn&amp;rsquo ; t really expecting anything to come out of it , to be honest , except to be part of the virtual yearbook , &amp;rdquo ; Judd says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Judd&amp;rsquo ; s video starts like every other contest entry , with an introduction . But it soon shifts , cutting away from a bright , warm , bubbly kid speaking from her bedroom , to Judd practicing ventriloquism in front of the bathroom mirror while speaking of discovering new aspects of the self . There are new hobbies ( screeching on a violin , painting a water-colour happy face ) ; improved multi-tasking ( texting while reading a novel ) ; and learning to exhale ( screaming beneath a tree ) . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It is not spoken-word , but it is poetic : Judd makes you laugh . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe width= " 640 " height= " 360 " src= " https : **34;19617;TOOLONG ... frameborder= " 0 " allow= " accelerometer ; autoplay ; encrypted-media ; gyroscope ; picture-in-picture " **34;19653;TOOLONG &lt;p&gt; Patricia Cogley , the San Francisco Bay area-based director of Girls Voices , is aware of some funny Canadians . She knew , upon watching Judd&amp;rsquo ; s almost four-minute clip , that the kid from the dairy farm west of Ottawa was deploying the national weapon . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In times like these , it is really more advanced to use humour to address things , &amp;rdquo ; Cogley says . &amp;ldquo ; All of us thought it was really refreshing , and relatable , having April talk about the pandemic from a humorous perspective . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19689;TOOLONG ... more stories about heroes of the **29;19724;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;19755;TOOLONG ... of the pandemic : North Bay teens made a do-it-yourself sterilizer kit that can kill the COVID-19 **26;19790;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... The video isn&amp;rsquo ; t all guffaws . What has been lost to Judd , to each of us , is real . The pandemic happened , still is happening , and yet it won&amp;rsquo ; t last forever . Judd will head off to university , in person , eventually . Life will move on , although maybe not in a way anyone could have foreseen . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I don&amp;rsquo ; t want to leave this global pause and go back to the way things were , &amp;rdquo ; Judd says , toward the video&amp;rsquo ; s conclusion . &amp;ldquo ; I want to leave , and have a heightened appreciation for all the things that make life full . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; From me , to me &amp;ndash ; to you &amp;mdash ; graduates and non-graduates , and anyone who is struggling to adjust or overcoming loss , both big and small : we got this . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Or at least we will , eventually . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Hear , hear . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;National Post&lt;/em&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **29;19818;TOOLONG " **36;19849;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " **27;19887;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;oconnorwrite ... &lt;/p&gt; Joe O'ConnorThu , 18 Jun 2020 20:46:10 **45;19916;TOOLONG ... work week : This rural N.S. municipality could be the first in Canada to make it a realityhttp : **33;19963;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;19998;TOOLONG ... title= " A rural N.S. municipality might be the first in Canada to change to a four-day working week . " alt= " Ottawa may well need its painted bike lanes , but critics say smaller rural communities have use for new roadways and sewer systems rather than energy-efficient projects or others related to transit . " data-portal-copyright= " Tony Caldwell/Postmedia/File " **28;20417;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571179 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;mdash ; The Liberal government is increasingly at odds with some provinces over its sprawling infrastructure program , with provincial officials saying that a focus on &amp;ldquo ; green&amp;rdquo ; or other specific project types could limit Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s ability to build projects following the COVID-19 pandemic. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Concerns among some provinces comes as federal Infrastructure Minister Catherine McKenna readies roughly $3 billion in COVID-19 infrastructure spending , aimed at public health efforts like encouraging outdoor activities or providing sanitation in facilities . Provinces are broadly supportive of the funding , but say the program remains overly rigid , amounting to what one provincial official called a &amp;ldquo ; public relations campaign&amp;rdquo ; by the Trudeau government to prioritize the sort of projects that fit with its wider political message . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Recent differences over the infrastructure program are an intensification of what has been a years-long disagreement between the feds and some provinces over the structuring of Ottawa&amp;rsquo ; s $187-billion spending plans . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Provinces say they would prefer funding for basic projects like roads or waste water treatment centres , for example , while the federal program forces them to apply through specific project streams like public transit , which they say could hamper their ability to build necessary projects following the crisis . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The National Post reached out to 11 provincial and territorial infrastructure offices about the federal program , and spoke directly with five officials , who were granted anonymity due to their ongoing discussions with Ottawa . Four provincial and territorial offices responded with written comments about the program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The streams have been very prescriptive in what we can do with them , &amp;rdquo ; said one provincial official . &amp;ldquo ; It&amp;rsquo ; s frustrating because the province does not necessarily share the same the priorities as the federal government , especially when it is very ideologically driven like this one . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another lamented that Ottawa is considering a short , 18-month window for the COVID-19 infrastructure stream , saying it would provide a &amp;ldquo ; ridiculously short timeline&amp;rdquo ; to complete projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Smaller rural communities , the person said , might have little need for public transit funding , but have a major demand for new roadways and sewer systems &amp;mdash ; a need that is not always recognized under the current program structure . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; What&amp;rsquo ; s the point of this , aside from having a lovely public relations campaign ? &amp;rdquo ; the person said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;20447;TOOLONG ... government still $2B shy of spending targets under sprawling infrastructure program : **27;20482;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;20511;TOOLONG ... unveil new COVID-19 stream for provincial infrastructure **28;20546;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... A third official said funding for COVID-19 infrastructure amounts to a repackaging of existing funds , and does not provide new funding that would help stimulate the economies of cash-strapped provinces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re just moving pieces around on a chessboard , &amp;rdquo ; the person said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Ottawa is considering pitching in 80 per cent of the cost of projects for the pandemic response , up from an earlier portion of around 30 per cent . Final details of the COVID-19 package have yet to be released . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Governments have for some time urged Ottawa to remove some restraints on its Investing in Canada Infrastructure Plan ( ICIP ) , saying they don&amp;rsquo ; t allow the flexibility required to meet the needs of individual communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We have shared our concerns that the infrastructure priorities Canada has identified for funding through the ICIP do not match the kinds of infrastructure Saskatchewan communities most need , &amp;rdquo ; a spokesperson for Saskatchewan deputy premier Gordon Wyant said in a written statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;20576;TOOLONG ... title= " Infrastructure Minister Catherine McKenna . " alt= " Infrastructure Minister Catherine McKenna . " data-portal-copyright= " Justin Tang/The Canadian Press/File " **28;20611;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78571100 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Provincial officials in Yukon and Nova Scotia said they have not had differences with Ottawa over the program . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; ve been very receptive to our requests for flexibility , &amp;rdquo ; said a spokesperson for the Yukon infrastructure minister . Yukon&amp;rsquo ; s infrastructure budget under the Liberal program has tripled , from around $30 million under the previous Conservative government to $75 million today , the person said . Nova Scotia said the talks have been &amp;ldquo ; very positive . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a written response on Wednesday , McKenna&amp;rsquo ; s office said it has approved &amp;ldquo ; hundreds&amp;rdquo ; of applications in recent weeks under its COVID-19 infrastructure plans , saying the new stream of funding would promote &amp;ldquo ; a broader range of projects that can start quickly and be built in the next year or two . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The funding will allow for projects like expanded bike lanes as a way to enable better social distancing , for example , or for the installation of handwashing stations . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Observers in Canada have long warned against Ottawa funnelling money into specific project types , saying it should instead focus on developments that will increase Canada&amp;rsquo ; s economic productivity . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In a report by C.D. Howe Institute on Monday , a group of 11 academics and corporate lobbyists said they were &amp;ldquo ; skeptical of the push for broad &amp;lsquo ; green&amp;rsquo ; stimulus&amp;rdquo ; under the Liberal infrastructure program . The report said those efforts risked &amp;ldquo ; subsidizing assets like renewable generation for which market forces and carbon pricing should provide commercial incentives . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The report also warned against forcing projects into overly tight timelines , saying that &amp;ldquo ; many &amp;lsquo ; shovel ready&amp;rsquo ; projects may not be the most effective use of public funds compared with transformative projects that enhance productivity or connect markets . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; One provincial official who spoke to the National Post said tight timelines proposed by McKenna on the latest round of projects risks repeating past faults in the infrastructure program , which caused spending delays . Phase one of the ICIP program included $14 billion that was expected to be spent within a roughly two-year window , beginning in 2016 . Today , that initial $14 billion won&amp;rsquo ; t be spent until 2022 at the earliest , according to the 2019 budget . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Prime Minister Justin Trudeau introduced the $187-billion infrastructure program as part of a 2015 campaign promise , in which Ottawa would run budget deficits in part to fund critical projects , and to close Canada&amp;rsquo ; s long-standing infrastructure gap . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The Parliamentary Budget Officer , in a separate report released on Wednesday , said it was unsure whether funding under the COVID-19 stream would boost economic activity due to the depleted financial positions of the provinces . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Given the significant fiscal pressures faced by provinces and municipalities , it is unclear whether federal funding will be able to leverage new provincial and municipal money over the medium-term for new projects , &amp;rdquo ; the report said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It found that total spending under ICIP has now reached $51 billion , or roughly $2 billion less than was projected in 2019 . Compared with 2016 estimates , which projected that spending would have reached $59 billion , total spending is $8 billion shy of targets . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That has in turn limited the economic impact of the program , down to 0.74 per cent of GDP , which was &amp;ldquo ; primarily attributable to delays in the roll-out of the program , &amp;rdquo ; the study said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In January , the federal opposition passed a motion that called for an official audit of the infrastructure program to determine the full scope of funding . McKenna in February said the federal government has spent $57 billion across 52,000 individual projects . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Conservative infrastructure critic Luc Berthold on Wednesday said uncertainty around the full scope of the program points to the need for an official audit . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These failures demonstrate why it was important to have Parliament call on the Auditor General to audit the entire Investing in Canada Plan , and why it is essential that the Office of the Auditor General be properly funded in order to do their job . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **28;20641;TOOLONG " **35;20671;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **32;20708;TOOLONG ... class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;jessesnyder ... &lt;/p&gt; Jesse SnyderThu , 18 Jun 2020 13:48:07 **45;20742;TOOLONG ... A Seattle-based company has invented a new technology to literally shield travellers from the coronavirus as they board airplanes , a first-of-its-kind move since the pandemic began . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;The technology , called ' AirShield , ' is a 3D-printed device fitted to cabin ventilation nozzles that will direct breath droplets downwards by " engineering blades of air . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt;Industrial design company Teague describes the innovation on its &lt;a href= " https : **33;20887;TOOLONG ... as an " invisible germ isolation unit around each passenger . " &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;span style= " font-weight:400 ; " &gt; " When a passenger breathes , coughs , or even sneezes , the water vapor is contained within that passenger space and is immediately redirected downwards and out of the cabin to the HEPA filtration units before it has the opportunity to enter the personal space of a neighboring passenger , " they write . &lt;/span&gt; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;20922;TOOLONG ... title= " The AirShield is a 3D-printed device fitted to cabin ventilation nozzles in the aircraft . " alt= " The AirShield is a 3D-printed device fitted to cabin ventilation nozzles in the aircraft . MacKay pointed out O&amp;rsquo ; Toole had to remove a promise from his platform to end fossil fuel subsidies , and suggested O&amp;rsquo ; Toole had to back down because his Western supporters &amp;mdash ; specifically Alberta Premier Jason Kenney &amp;mdash ; were angry about it . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; There was also a significant clash between the two on social issues and who is best able to build an inclusive party . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; MacKay brought up a recent Radio-Canada report that featured leaked audio of O&amp;rsquo ; Toole&amp;rsquo ; s comments to Quebec social conservatives . According to the report , O&amp;rsquo ; Toole appealed for them to rank him high on their ballot , and said he had some concerns with the Liberals&amp;rsquo ; proposed legislation aimed at banning conversion therapy . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; O&amp;rsquo ; Toole subsequently posted a tweet in French saying he condemned conversion therapy , but MacKay nonetheless accused him of saying one thing to French audiences and another to English . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; You don&amp;rsquo ; t have a clear position , &amp;rdquo ; MacKay repeatedly told O&amp;rsquo ; Toole , and demanded that O&amp;rsquo ; Toole clarify whether he&amp;rsquo ; s pro-life or not . Norway was first with 130 and Ireland garnered 128 votes . The Outaws and the Hells Angels Motorcycle Club have a long and volatile rivalry ; the Red Devils are a support club to the Hells Angels . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The others feature alarming interactions between bikers and the public and even police . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; On May 21 in Kingston , three young men were waiting in a car at a McDonald&amp;rsquo ; s drive-thru at 7:30 p.m. when a man pulled up beside them , accusing them of making fun of him and threatening them , Kingston police said . The men , in their teens or twenties , quickly left . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; The accused gave chase in his vehicle , pulling up alongside them , and pointed a handgun at them and again verbally threatened to shoot them . The victims , fearing for their lives , were eventually able to flee from the accused . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Police found the man later that night . Weapons and ammunition were hidden throughout his vehicle : a sawn-off shotgun , a loaded handgun , collapsible baton , folding knife and various shotgun and handgun ammunition , police said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;22866;TOOLONG ... title= " Guns , drugs and biker paraphernalia were seized in April . The gang-on-gang one is specific to their ongoing tension , &amp;rdquo ; he said of the longstanding and sometimes deadly rivalry between the Hells Angels and the Outlaws. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Part of it could be volatile biker attitude exasperated by COVID-19 restrictions . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That&amp;rsquo ; s potentially part of it . It may just be coincidence but it also may be that with COVID there are less people on the street and more attention brought to them , &amp;rdquo ; said Wade . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; After the fight in St. Catharines , police pieced events together from video because everyone left before police arrived . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; He was hit . But we don&amp;rsquo ; t know the extent of the injuries because the victim isn&amp;rsquo ; t co-operative , &amp;rdquo ; said Wade . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Police charged Matthew Quattrociocchi , 20 , of Port Dover with assault with a weapon and possession of a weapon dangerous to public peace . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt; * Email : &lt;a href= " **31;22931;TOOLONG " **38;22964;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **25;23004;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;ADHumphreys ... &lt;/p&gt; Adrian HumphreysWed , 17 Jun 2020 23:30:37 **45;23031;TOOLONG ... CRA systems are ' systemically oppressive ' towards vulnerable populations : taxpayers ' ombudsmanhttp : **31;23078;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;23111;TOOLONG ... title= " Taxpayers ' Ombudsman Sherra Profit : " One 's perception is reality . It is their reality . " " alt= " Taxpayers ' Ombudsman Sherra Profit : " One 's perception is reality . It is their reality . " " data-portal-copyright= " Sean Kilpatrick/The Canadian Press/File " **28;23146;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78570975 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; OTTAWA &amp;ndash ; The federal taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; ombudsman says some of Canada Revenue Agency&amp;rsquo ; s ( CRA ) processes are &amp;ldquo ; systemically oppressive&amp;rdquo ; towards vulnerable populations as well as indigenous , rural and northern communities . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; These ( tax ) filers feel they receive conflicting information , the processes are unfair and the CRA does not address their unique circumstances and needs , &amp;rdquo ; Canada&amp;rsquo ; s outgoing Taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; Ombudsman , Sherra Profit , writes in her 2019-2020 annual report published Wednesday . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This general belief leads to reluctance to interact with a system much of the population believes to be systemically oppressive and in turn reduces the likelihood people receive all the benefits , credits and deductions to which they are entitled , &amp;rdquo ; the report says . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That belief by indigenous , rural and northern communities isn&amp;rsquo ; t just a question of perception , Profit later specified in an interview with the National Post . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; One&amp;rsquo ; s perception is reality . It is their reality , &amp;rdquo ; said the head of the taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; watchdog . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23176;TOOLONG ... Revenue Agency opens snitch line to information about federal COVID-19 aid program **26;23211;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23239;TOOLONG ... claiming $4.4B from Canadian companies and individuals suspected of tax **28;23274;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23304;TOOLONG ... collectors , auditors to help field ' historic ' numbers of benefit-seeking **28;23339;TOOLONG &lt;/ul&gt; &lt; ... Over her five-year mandate that ends on July 5 , Profit says she found multiple instances where CRA&amp;rsquo ; s bureaucracy was overly rigid and had significant communication issues with taxpayers . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; She says that can be particularly problematic for vulnerable populations who don&amp;rsquo ; t always have quick or timely access to some basic services . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are aspects of the CRA systems and processes that will be more oppressive to certain groups ( &amp;hellip ; ) For example , so many people don&amp;rsquo ; t have a family doctor . So asking for a letter from a family doctor isn&amp;rsquo ; t going to work from them , &amp;rdquo ; Profit said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are also socio-economic classes of people who are in housing situations where it may be very difficult to get a lease or a letter on a letterhead from a landlord , &amp;rdquo ; she said , adding that these CRA systems are not intentionally designed to be oppressive . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; In her report , she highlights one particularly shocking case where a woman who depended on the Canada Child Benefit ( CCB ) for day-to-day living was suddenly cut off from payments and demanded to reimburse $16,000. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;23369;TOOLONG ... title= " Canada Revenue Agency 's headquarters in Ottawa . " alt= " Canada Revenue Agency 's headquarters in Ottawa . " data-portal-copyright= " Chris Roussakis/Postmedia/File " **28;23404;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78526909 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; After she reached out to CRA for an explanation , the agency told her the documents she submitted in her reapplication after separation from her ex-spouse were &amp;ldquo ; not legible&amp;rdquo ; . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Instead of requesting she resend them , the CRA stopped her benefits . The complainant resent the documents and called several times to get updates , without success , &amp;rdquo ; Profit explains in the report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; It took an urgent intervention by the Taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; Ombudsman&amp;rsquo ; s office to have CRA conduct a review of the file , according to the report . The CRA then understood why it was more difficult for the applicant to reapply for CCB. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; At this time it became known that her living situation was not safe and she was forced into an emergency shelter while trying to find permanent housing . Losing the CCB further complicated finding a suitable home for her children , &amp;rdquo ; the report said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; At that point , the CRA not only quickly approved her new application , but also manually processed a retroactive CCB payment and then sent her the December payment early to assist with holiday spending . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; I do find there is a lot of breakdown in communication , &amp;rdquo ; Profit said . &amp;ldquo ; I know the CRA is making changes to how it administers programs like the CCB , but we&amp;rsquo ; re finding we&amp;rsquo ; re still seeing a lot of these complaints . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Another issue she&amp;rsquo ; s noted over her tenure as the taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; ombudsman is that various CRA departments tend to operate in silos. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; That would explain why someone could be repeatedly asked by the agency to provide the same information or documentation over and over , for example . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; There are systems that don&amp;rsquo ; t talk to one another , Profit said . That&amp;rsquo ; s something I&amp;rsquo ; ve really been constantly bringing up . ( CRA needs ) a more horizontal approach to look at things as a whole . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Overall though , Profit says she&amp;rsquo ; s encouraged by efforts made by the CRA to improve service to Canadians and gradually adopt more client-centric approach . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As an example , she noted the appointment of a Chief Services Officer in March 2018 whose job is to transform the agency&amp;rsquo ; s culture and significantly improve the quality and speed of services offered to Canadians . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; They&amp;rsquo ; re at least starting to talk the talk . They know there are issues , they know they are problems , and they have that Chief Services Officer who is looking at it with a whole-of-organization perspective , &amp;rdquo ; the ombudsman explained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But in order for her own organization to better do its job , Profit says the federal government needs to increase its budget and make her office report to Parliament . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; As of now , the Taxpayers&amp;rsquo ; Ombudsman reports directly to the Minister of National Revenue , who is in charge of CRA. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This structure creates an inherent element of conflict of interest in the ombudsman reporting to the Minister responsible for the department or agency the ombudsman is tasked with overseeing . A Minister has a vested interested in ensuring their department or agency is perceived to be operating effectively and efficiently , &amp;rdquo ; Profit agues in her report . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;em&gt;* Email : &lt;a href= " **27;23434;TOOLONG " **34;23463;TOOLONG Twitter : &lt;a href= " https : **25;23499;TOOLONG " class= " twitter-follow-button " &gt;ChrisGNardi &; ... &lt;/p&gt; &lt;hr&gt; &lt;p&gt; &lt;strong&gt;Listen to our news podcast , 10/3 , on &lt;a href= " https : **33;23526;TOOLONG ... Podcasts **25;23561;TOOLONG &lt;/p&gt; &lt;iframe src= " **40;23588;TOOLONG ... NardiWed , 17 Jun 2020 23:07:51 **45;23630;TOOLONG ... PoliticsChildren do n't need to wear masks or follow strict social distancing when they return to school : SickKids expertshttp : **33;23677;TOOLONG ... src= " https : **33;23712;TOOLONG ... title= " Children play at the Harris Academy 's Shortland 's school on June 4 in London , England . " alt= " Children play at the Harris Academy 's Shortland 's school on June 4 in London , England . " data-portal-copyright= " Dan Kitwood/Getty Images " **28;23747;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78570099 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Students returning to school this fall should not have to wear masks or maintain social distancing while at play , Canada&amp;rsquo ; s largest children&amp;rsquo ; s hospital argues in a new report that stresses the importance of getting young people back to class . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The advice from the Hospital for Sick Children seems to veer toward a more relaxed approach than taken by some jurisdictions , especially in East Asia , as they re-opened shuttered schools . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Requiring masks could lead to more infection , not less , while separating children as they socialize outside the classroom would have negative psychological effects , says a &lt;a href= " https : **33;23777;TOOLONG ... document&lt;/a&gt; SickKids released Wednesday . It does urge keeping desks as far apart as possible and cancelling assemblies . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Children have suffered anxiety , depression and loneliness and faced greater risk of family discord and abuse during the lockdown , hospital experts told a virtual news conference . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; They said the psychological benefits of resuming in-person classes this September outweigh COVID-19&amp;rsquo ; s risks , which they said can be lessened with measures like frequent handwashing and keeping sick students at home . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; It has never been 100 per cent safe to send children to school , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Ronald Cohn , the SickKids CEO . &amp;ldquo ; There&amp;rsquo ; s always been a risk of contracting some infectious disease , whether it&amp;rsquo ; s the flu , the Norovirus or other viruses . Children are at risk of being injured &amp;hellip ; and suffering mental and physical harm from bullying . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; But of course we can manage these risks and put mitigating factors in place to deal with them . This is exactly the kind of approach we have to take now . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;ul class= " relatedlinks " &gt; &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23812;TOOLONG ... daycares reopen , parents are facing impossible decisions to keep kids safe from **29;23847;TOOLONG &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href= " https : **33;23878;TOOLONG ... weather and still trapped by COVID : Are the kids going to be okay ? &lt;/a&gt;&lt;/li&gt; &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;p &; ... The hospital&amp;rsquo ; s advice did not sit well , though , with at least one prominent advocate of mass adoption of face coverings. &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Dr. David Fisman , head of the epidemiology division at the University of Toronto&amp;rsquo ; s school of public health , has urged public-health authorities to make mask use in certain public places mandatory , citing research suggesting the practice is key to curbing the virus&amp;rsquo ; s spread . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; That seems like the easiest of wins , especially for older kids , &amp;rdquo ; said Fisman Wednesday . &amp;ldquo ; My nieces are 11 and 13 . They live in Massachusetts . They are required to mask . It&amp;rsquo ; s not a big deal . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; This whole document reeks of the kind of laissez-faire approach that got us into trouble with long-term care facilities and which now has Sweden in so much trouble , &amp;rdquo ; he added . &amp;ldquo ; The complete lack of thought given to the role schools may play in amplifying disease that kills adults is just astounding. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But Cohn argued that schools are not likely to be a significant amplifier of COVID-19 . Despite indications early in the pandemic &amp;mdash ; and experience with flu and other respiratory viruses &amp;mdash ; children do not seem to be &amp;ldquo ; **25;23913;TOOLONG ; of COVID-19 , he said . In fact , it&amp;rsquo ; s more common for adults to infect young people than the other way around , Cohn maintained . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;23940;TOOLONG ... title= " Children wearing face shields sit in class at Kinugawa Elementary School on June 3 , 2020 in Nikko , Japan . " alt= " Children wearing face shields sit in class at Kinugawa Elementary School on June 3 , 2020 in Nikko , Japan . " data-portal-copyright= " Carl Court/Getty Images " **28;23975;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78570088 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Fisman said there is no basis for that statement . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; If you wanted to create super-spreader events , the way to do it would be to gather large groups indoors and have them sing , as this document appears to countenance , &amp;rdquo ; he said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Some research in recent weeks , though , has downplayed the likelihood of children being a major vector for the disease . A &lt;a href= " https : **33;24005;TOOLONG ... by British scientists&lt;/a&gt; , citing in part family clusters of cases where children were rarely the instigator , concluded that &amp;ldquo ; at the current time , children do not appear to be super spreaders. &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; An &lt;a href= " http : **34;24040;TOOLONG ... report&lt;/a&gt; last month on the experience in re-opened schools there found few cases and no evidence of students passing the disease to adults , suggesting &amp;ldquo ; children are not the primary drivers of COVID-19 spread . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; The SickKids report does recommend that desks in classrooms be separated as much as possible and class sizes kept as small as feasible to make physical distancing easier . It also advises against large assemblies and other gatherings , and that lunch times be staggered to reduce crowding . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; But it says requiring children to wear masks could do more harm than good . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; In young children in particular , masks can be irritating and may lead to increased touching of the face and eyes which could increase the risk of infection . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;img src= " https : **33;24076;TOOLONG ... title= " Students sit in a classroom at a primary school in Eichenau , southern Germany , on June 16 , 2020. " alt= " Students sit in a classroom at a primary school in Eichenau , southern Germany , on June 16 , 2020. " data-portal-copyright= " CHRISTOF STACHE/AFP via Getty Images " **28;24111;TOOLONG " 1 " data-license-id= " 78570089 " /&gt; &lt;p&gt; Children should be allowed to play at recess and engage in some sports , the document said . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Strict physical distancing should not be emphasized to children in the school setting as it is not practical and could cause significant psychological harm , &amp;rdquo ; said the report . &amp;ldquo ; Close interaction , such as playing and socializing , is central to child development and should not be discouraged . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Keeping children out of school has already caused &amp;ldquo ; significant adverse consequences , &amp;rdquo ; said Dr. Catherine Birken , a paediatrician leading a study of the lockdown&amp;rsquo ; s impact on young people . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; Parents are telling me their children are struggling with the lack of routine and structure , &amp;rdquo ; she told the news conference . &amp;ldquo ; Children are sad , irritable , clingy and lonely . &amp;rdquo ; &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; Asked about the hospital&amp;rsquo ; s more controversial recommendations around masks and social distancing , Ontario Education Minister Stephen Lecce side-stepped the issue . &lt;/p&gt; &lt;p&gt; &amp;ldquo ; We are working closely with the best medical and scientific leaders in Canada &amp;mdash ; including the Hospital For Sick Children and the COVID-19 Command Table &amp;mdash ; to develop health protocols that keep our schools safe , &amp;rdquo ;
